What was tested:
- Per task `test_steps`: opened both YAML files and confirmed:
  - `application.yml` covers all five required areas — datasource, session, auth, CORS origin, server port.
  - `application-local.yml` overrides datasource/CORS/cookie-secure for local development without duplicating production defaults.
  - Profile separation is clear: `application.yml` is the canonical/production-shaped baseline, `application-local.yml` only contains keys that diverge for local dev.
- Sanity check beyond the spec: `./mvnw -B -ntp compile` from `backend/` → BUILD SUCCESS. YAML files are not parsed at compile time, but the build still completed cleanly with the new `resources/` directory present.
- Skipped Gradle compile/test step from the loop instructions per the conditional in those instructions: backend uses Maven (`./mvnw`), no `gradlew`/`settings.gradle.kts` exists. Maven is the source of truth (architecture.md §1, tasks.json TASK-006/007/008 verbiage). Reconciliation already documented in TASK-006.
- Did NOT run `./mvnw spring-boot:run` — JPA auto-config still needs the `users` table that TASK-021 creates and Flyway has no migrations yet (TASK-012 baseline pending). Boot would fail at startup. Acceptable per task scope: the spec only asks to confirm the keys exist.

Profile-key choices (rationale for non-obvious ones):
- `spring.session.jdbc.initialize-schema=never`: avoids Spring Session's built-in Postgres schema bootstrap stomping on Flyway. TASK-023 will add the session DDL via Flyway migration explicitly.
- `spring.jpa.hibernate.ddl-auto=validate`: enforces that the JPA model matches the Flyway-managed schema; any drift surfaces at startup instead of silently mutating the DB.
- `server.servlet.session.cookie.secure=true` in `application.yml`, overridden to `false` in `application-local.yml`: production-safe by default, dev-pragmatic on localhost. Aligns with the .env.example note about flipping these for HTTPS.
- `trackify.*` prefix chosen instead of overloading `spring.*` so the upcoming `@ConfigurationProperties` classes (TASK-025) own a clean namespace.
- `spring.profiles.active=${SPRING_PROFILES_ACTIVE:local}` defaults to `local` so first-time clones work without exporting env vars; CI/prod set `SPRING_PROFILES_ACTIVE` explicitly.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- `trackify.cors.*` and `trackify.auth.*` are loose YAML keys today — there's no `@ConfigurationProperties` binding yet. TASK-010 (CORS config) will read the cors keys; TASK-025 (auth properties class) will bind the auth keys. If those tasks rename the keys, update both YAMLs and `.env.example` together.
- `spring.session.jdbc.table-name` is set to the default (`SPRING_SESSION`) explicitly. If TASK-023 chooses a custom table name, change here too.
- `application-local.yml` uses Postgres credentials matching `docker-compose.yml` defaults. If those defaults ever change (e.g. password rotation), update both files.
- `org.hibernate.orm.jdbc.bind=TRACE` logs raw bound parameter values — fine on local, must NEVER be enabled on shared/prod environments because it leaks PII into logs. The fact that this only lives in `application-local.yml` is the safety boundary; do not promote that line into `application.yml`.
- `trackify.auth.bootstrap.admin.password` will end up in the env in plaintext. That's acceptable for a self-hosted MVP but should be flagged in TASK-081's runbook ("set this once, then unset and restart"), or TASK-025/026 should consume it once and clear it from memory.
- `spring.flyway.clean-disabled=false` in local enables `./mvnw flyway:clean` for fast schema resets while iterating on migrations. Deliberately NOT set in `application.yml`, so Flyway's own default (`true`) protects production.
- No backend code changes; no changes to `pom.xml`, `.env.example`, or any other file. Package skeleton (TASK-009), CORS+health (TASK-010), error shape (TASK-011), Flyway baseline (TASK-012) all still pending.
- Next pending task: TASK-009 (package skeleton: `config/`, `common/`, `auth/`, `user/`, `workspace/`, `project/`, `task/`, `board/`, `timeline/` with placeholder config classes).


## 2026-04-26 — TASK-009: backend package skeleton + placeholder config classes

What changed:
- Created the full per-domain package tree under `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/` matching architecture.md §3 exactly: 9 top-level packages (`config`, `common`, `auth`, `user`, `workspace`, `project`, `task`, `board`, `timeline`) and 37 leaf packages.
- `config/`: three placeholder `@Configuration` classes — `WebConfig.java` (CORS goes here in TASK-010), `JacksonConfig.java` (ObjectMapper customization hook), `OpenApiConfig.java` (OpenAPI/springdoc hook if added later). All three are empty bodies — Spring will pick them up via component scan but they contribute no beans yet, so no behavior change.
- `common/{exception,response,security,util}` — 4 leaf packages, each with a one-line javadoc `package-info.java`.
- `auth/`, `user/`, `workspace/`, `project/`, `task/` — each with the standard 5-layer split per architecture §7.1: `controller/`, `application/`, `domain/`, `infrastructure/`, `dto/`. 25 leaf packages total, each with `package-info.java`.
- `board/`, `timeline/` — each with `controller/`, `application/`, `query/`, `dto/` (note: `query/` instead of the standard `infrastructure/` per architecture §3, because these modules host read-models rather than write-side persistence). 8 leaf packages total, each with `package-info.java`.
- 37 leaf `package-info.java` files in total. Each one is a single short javadoc comment + the package declaration — purely structural, no logic. They give every package a Java-visible source file (Git can't track empty dirs and javac needs at least one .java per package for it to "exist" in the compiled module).
- Marked TASK-009 status = done in tasks.json.

What was tested:
- Per task `test_steps`: `./mvnw -B -ntp compile` from `backend/` → BUILD SUCCESS in 0.5s. Compiled 41 source files (3 config classes + 37 package-info + 1 TrackifyApplication). Java 21 target preserved.
- Sanity check: `find src/main/java/com/trackify -type d | sort` confirms exactly the 9 top-level + 37 leaf directories from architecture.md §3 (no extras, no missing).
- Skipped Gradle compile/test step from the loop instructions per the conditional in those instructions: backend uses Maven (`./mvnw`), no `gradlew`/`settings.gradle.kts` exists. Maven is the source of truth (architecture.md §1, tasks.json TASK-006/007/008/009 verbiage). Reconciliation was already documented in TASK-006.

Design choice — `package-info.java` over `.gitkeep`:
- `.gitkeep` would persist the directory in Git but javac would never see those packages — they'd have no compiled `Package` object, no place to attach package-level annotations, and tools like Spring's component scan or static analyzers would skip them. `package-info.java` is the idiomatic Java answer: it's a real source file that javac recognizes, anchors the package in the compiled module, and gives us a per-package documentation slot for free. Cost is 37 single-line files; benefit is that every leaf package is a first-class Java entity from day one.

Design choice — empty `@Configuration` classes for `config/`:
- Architecture.md §3 names exactly three classes (`WebConfig`, `JacksonConfig`, `OpenApiConfig`). I created them as empty `@Configuration`-annotated classes rather than as bare placeholder files because: (1) the annotation makes Spring aware of them at component-scan time, so future tasks can add `@Bean`/`@EnableWebMvc`/etc. methods without changing the class declaration; (2) an empty `@Configuration` registers no beans and has no runtime cost; (3) it documents intent — someone reading these classes knows immediately they're meant to host beans.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- `OpenApiConfig` is a forward-looking placeholder. There's no springdoc or OpenAPI dependency in `pom.xml` (TASK-007 didn't include it). If we never add OpenAPI, this class can be deleted without consequence. Leaving it because architecture.md §3 lists it explicitly.
- `JacksonConfig` is also forward-looking — Spring Boot's default Jackson config is fine for the MVP. Keep the class in place so when we eventually need `JavaTimeModule` tweaks or custom serializers (likely once Gantt date payloads start round-tripping `LocalDate`/`Instant`), we have a home for them without inventing a new file.
- `package-info.java` files use one-liner javadocs with implementation hints (e.g. "CORS wiring lands here in TASK-010", "Source of truth for task mutations (architecture.md §12)"). These are anchors for future agents working through the task list — when TASK-010 starts, grep for "TASK-010" in package-info files surfaces `WebConfig`'s comment immediately. If those task-id breadcrumbs feel noisy later, they're cheap to remove in TASK-081's polish pass.
- Empty `@Configuration` classes mean Spring's component scan now sees three more configurations than before. No `@Bean` methods are registered, so no measurable startup-time difference and no functional change. If someone later adds `@ConditionalOnMissingBean` or `@EnableConfigurationProperties` to one of these files, it'll Just Work because the class is already wired into the scan.
- `board/query/` and `timeline/query/` deliberately replace the usual `infrastructure/` layer for those two modules — that matches architecture.md §3 and §7.2 ("board read model… board-level query optimized for Kanban UI" / "Gantt read model… timeline query shaped for bars"). The write-side for `board` (move-task command) and `timeline` (schedule update) lives in the `task` module (architecture.md §12: "The `task` module is the source of truth for task mutations"), so `board/` and `timeline/` legitimately need no `infrastructure/` of their own.
- No changes outside `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/`. `pom.xml`, `application.yml`, `application-local.yml`, `.env.example`, `docker-compose.yml`, `README.md` are all untouched.
- The full `tree` is now ready to receive code from TASK-010 onward without any further structural decisions — every layer has a permanent, javac-visible home.
- Next pending task: TASK-010 (CORS config + `GET /api/health` endpoint). `WebConfig.java` is the natural home for the CORS bean (its javadoc already says so).


## 2026-04-26 — TASK-010: CORS config + GET /api/health

What changed:
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/config/WebConfig.java`: replaced the empty `@Configuration` placeholder with a `CorsConfigurationSource` bean. The bean reads `trackify.cors.{allowed-origin,allowed-methods,allowed-headers,allow-credentials}` via `@Value`, builds a `CorsConfiguration`, and registers it on path pattern `/api/**` via `UrlBasedCorsConfigurationSource`. Methods/headers are bound as `String[]` so comma-separated YAML values (`GET,POST,PUT,PATCH,DELETE,OPTIONS`) split cleanly.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/common/HealthController.java` (new): `@RestController @RequestMapping("/api/health")` with a single `@GetMapping` returning `Map.of("status", "ok")`. Spring MVC + Jackson serialize that as `{"status":"ok"}` with `Content-Type: application/json`.
- `backend/src/test/java/com/trackify/common/HealthControllerTest.java` (new — first ever test file in the backend; created `src/test/java/...` tree from scratch). `@WebMvcTest(controllers = HealthController.class, excludeAutoConfiguration = SecurityAutoConfiguration.class)` slice. `@TestPropertySource` injects the four `trackify.cors.*` keys so `WebConfig` (loaded as a `@Configuration`/MVC infra by the slice) resolves its `@Value` placeholders during context startup. The test asserts HTTP 200, `Content-Type` JSON, and `$.status == "ok"`.
- Marked TASK-010 status = done in tasks.json.

What was tested:
- `./mvnw -B -ntp test` from `backend/` → BUILD SUCCESS in 6.2s. Compiled 42 source files (was 41 before — +1 for HealthController; WebConfig modified, not added). 1 test class, 1 test method, 0 failures, 0 errors. Slice context started in 0.437s.
- The literal task `test_steps` is `curl http://localhost:8080/api/health` against a running app. That requires Postgres up + a Flyway-clean DB + a security chain that permits `/api/health` — none of which exist yet (Postgres is environmental, TASK-012 is the Flyway baseline, TASK-024 is the security chain). The MockMvc slice test is the closest in-scope substitute for TASK-010 and proves the controller contract end-to-end through the DispatcherServlet + Jackson pipeline. The integration-level curl will work end-to-end once TASK-012 + TASK-024 land; no extra TASK-010 code will be needed at that point.
- Per the loop instructions: skipped Gradle compile/test step because backend has no `gradlew`/`settings.gradle.kts`. Maven is the source of truth (decision finalized in TASK-006).

Design choices and trade-offs:
- `CorsConfigurationSource` bean vs `WebMvcConfigurer.addCorsMappings(...)`: chose the bean. Spring Security 6 (which is on the classpath now and gets a real `SecurityFilterChain` in TASK-024) consumes a `CorsConfigurationSource` bean automatically when `.cors(Customizer.withDefaults())` is called on `HttpSecurity`. The `addCorsMappings` MVC hook only runs *after* the security filter, so a preflight `OPTIONS` would already be 401'd by Spring Security before MVC sees it. The bean approach is the canonical "CORS-with-Spring-Security" pattern and avoids a refactor in TASK-024.
- `HealthController` lives at `com.trackify.common.HealthController` rather than under a new `common/health/` subpackage. Architecture.md §3 lists `common/{exception,response,security,util}` only; no `health` package was specified. A single top-level file in `common/` is cheaper than inventing a new subpackage for one class. If the controller grows (readiness, build info, etc.), promote to `common/health/` then.
- `Map<String, String>` return type instead of a dedicated `HealthResponse` record. The payload is one key, never sent over a typed client, and the architecture makes no claim about a stable health-payload schema. A record would be 5 extra lines for zero added safety — TASK-011 (global error shape) is where typed response envelopes earn their keep.
- `excludeAutoConfiguration = SecurityAutoConfiguration.class` in the test slice instead of `@AutoConfigureMockMvc(addFilters = false)`. Both would pass today, but excluding the autoconfig is cleaner: it removes Spring Security from the slice entirely so the test never touches `UserDetailsService`, `PasswordEncoder`, or `SecurityFilterChain` autoconfig — none of which are correctly wired until TASK-024. `addFilters = false` would still spin up the security beans and just bypass the filter at request time, which is more fragile to future autoconfig changes.
- `@TestPropertySource` over a `@Bean CorsConfigurationSource` mock. The `WebConfig` is real Spring config under test (its `@Value` placeholders must resolve), and stubbing it would mean the test no longer covers the config wiring. Providing the four properties is 4 lines and exercises the real bean.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- The CORS bean only takes effect once the security filter chain calls `.cors(...)`. Until TASK-024 lands, **no actual CORS handling happens at runtime** — Spring Security's default chain returns 401 for preflight `OPTIONS` requests before the bean is consulted. This is intentional: TASK-010 lays the bean, TASK-024 wires it. The MockMvc test does NOT cover preflight behavior because preflights are filter-chain concerns; trust TASK-024 to add a CORS-aware test (or extend `HealthControllerTest` then).
- `WebConfig` no longer implements `WebMvcConfigurer`. If a future task wants per-controller CORS overrides via the MVC registry, it'll need to add the interface back and override `addCorsMappings`. Recommend keeping CORS centralized in the bean.
- `@Value("${trackify.cors.allowed-origin}")` will fail context startup if `FRONTEND_ORIGIN` isn't set AND the active profile isn't `local` (because `application.yml` resolves it to `${FRONTEND_ORIGIN}` with no default, while `application-local.yml` defaults it to `http://localhost:5173`). That's the intended fail-fast behavior for prod-shaped boots — operators must set `FRONTEND_ORIGIN` explicitly. Mentioned in TASK-008 progress already; restating because TASK-010 now actually depends on it being set.
- `allowedHeaders` is bound as `String[]` even though `application.yml` currently has the single value `"*"`. Future-proofing for cases like `Authorization,Content-Type,X-Requested-With` without revisiting WebConfig. `Arrays.asList(allowedHeaders)` always returns a non-empty list.
- `allowCredentials=true` + a specific origin (`http://localhost:5173` in local) is CORS-spec compliant. If anyone ever sets `allowed-origin: "*"`, the browser will reject responses because credentialed wildcards aren't allowed. Worth a one-liner in TASK-081's runbook.
- `HealthController` has no `@PreAuthorize` or other security annotations — it relies on TASK-024's `permitAll()` for `/api/health`. The class javadoc points at TASK-024 as the contract owner.
- `excludeAutoConfiguration = SecurityAutoConfiguration.class` in the slice test is slightly load-bearing: when TASK-024 adds an explicit `@Configuration class SecurityConfig` with its own `SecurityFilterChain` bean, the slice will still load it (slices include `@Configuration` classes by default). At that point, either: (a) the SecurityConfig will permit `/api/health` and the test still passes, or (b) we'll need to switch to `@WithMockUser` or add `excludeFilters` to the slice. Re-evaluate when TASK-024 lands.
- First test in the project — established the convention `backend/src/test/java/com/trackify/<module>/...Test.java` mirroring the `src/main/java` layout. TASK-011 (error-shape test), TASK-024 (security chain test), TASK-026 (registration unit test), etc. should follow the same path.
- `pom.xml` already has `spring-boot-starter-test` (TASK-006) and `spring-security-test` (TASK-007) at `test` scope; no dep changes needed for this task. Mockito agent warning in test output is a Spring Boot 3.4 + JDK 21 noise message about dynamic agent loading — harmless, will be resolved when Mockito 5.x fixes self-attach. Not a TASK-010 concern.
- Next pending task: TASK-011 (global API error shape via `@RestControllerAdvice`). `common/exception/` is the natural home; the test should live next to `HealthControllerTest` under `common/`.


## 2026-04-26 — TASK-011: global API error shape

What changed:
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/common/response/ApiError.java` (new): immutable record envelope returned by every error path. Fields: `status` (int), `error` (String code: `NOT_FOUND` / `VALIDATION_FAILED` / `INTERNAL_ERROR`), `message`, `path`, `timestamp` (`Instant`), `details` (nullable `List<FieldViolation>`). Annotated `@JsonInclude(NON_NULL)` so the `details` field is omitted from non-validation responses instead of serializing as `null`. Two static factories: `of(status, error, message, path)` and `of(..., details)`. Nested `FieldViolation(field, message)` record for per-field validation errors.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/common/exception/NotFoundException.java` (new): `RuntimeException` subclass with a single `String`-message ctor. Used by application services to signal "resource not found OR caller can't see it" — combining the two cases is intentional (avoids leaking existence to unauthorized callers), and TASK-077 will refine 401/403 separately.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/common/exception/GlobalExceptionHandler.java` (new): `@RestControllerAdvice` mapping three exception families to `ApiError`. Handlers: `NotFoundException` → 404, `MethodArgumentNotValidException` → 400 with field-level details extracted from the `BindingResult`, and `Exception` (catch-all) → 500 with a sanitized `"Unexpected server error"` message (real cause logged via SLF4J at ERROR, never returned to the client). All three populate `path` from `HttpServletRequest#getRequestURI()`.
- `backend/src/test/java/com/trackify/common/exception/GlobalExceptionHandlerTest.java` (new): `@WebMvcTest` slice over a static-inner `TestController` with three endpoints (`/__test/not-found`, `/__test/validate`, `/__test/boom`). Three test methods assert the JSON shape for each branch: status code, `error` code, `message`, `path`, presence of `timestamp`, and (for validation) `details[0].field == "name"`. Slice excludes `SecurityAutoConfiguration` (same pattern as `HealthControllerTest` from TASK-010) and explicitly `@Import`s the advice + test controller so the slice doesn't auto-discover them via component scan.
- Marked TASK-011 status = done in tasks.json.

What was tested:
- Per task `test_steps`: ran `./mvnw -B -ntp test` from `backend/` → BUILD SUCCESS in 2.0s. Tests run: 4, Failures: 0, Errors: 0, Skipped: 0 (3 new in `GlobalExceptionHandlerTest` + 1 pre-existing `HealthControllerTest`). Compiled 44 main sources (was 42 before — +2 for `ApiError` and `GlobalExceptionHandler`; `NotFoundException` is the third new file but `ApiError$FieldViolation` counts as a separate compilation unit).
- Per the loop instructions: skipped Gradle `compileJava`/`compileTestJava`/`test` step because backend has no `gradlew`/`settings.gradle.kts`. Maven is the source of truth (decision finalized in TASK-006, restated in TASK-007/008/009/010).
- The "Unhandled exception" stack trace visible in the test output is the deliberate `IllegalStateException("kaboom")` from `/__test/boom` being logged by `GlobalExceptionHandler#handleGeneric`. Logging of the real cause is intentional — it proves the catch-all path runs and confirms the cause is logged but NOT exposed in the response body (the assertion `$.message == "Unexpected server error"` would have failed otherwise).

Design choices and trade-offs:
- **Record over class for `ApiError`** — Java 17+ records make the envelope immutable, give us a sane `equals`/`hashCode`/`toString` for free, and eliminate the boilerplate that would otherwise tempt us to add Lombok. Jackson serializes records via their canonical accessors with no extra config.
- **`@JsonInclude(NON_NULL)` on the record itself, not per field** — only `details` is conditional today, but applying the annotation at the record level future-proofs for additional optional fields without touching the annotation each time. Cost: zero. The `notFoundExceptionReturnsApiErrorEnvelope` test asserts `$.details` is `doesNotExist()` (not `isNull()`) so this behavior is locked down by a regression test.
- **Three handlers, not five** — task spec says "validation, not found, and generic server errors". Resisted the urge to also handle `ConstraintViolationException` (path/query-param validation), `HttpMessageNotReadableException` (malformed JSON), `MissingServletRequestParameterException`, etc. Those are correctly TASK-077's scope ("standardize 401/403/404"). The `Exception` catch-all handles them today by returning 500, which is wrong for malformed-JSON (should be 400) but acceptable until TASK-077 adds explicit handlers.
- **`error` field as a string code, not an enum** — codes like `"NOT_FOUND"` are stable contract for the frontend. Modeling them as a Java enum would be tidier internally but force consumers to track every code we add; a string keeps the contract open and matches what HTTP status semantics already convey. The `status` field carries the HTTP status separately so clients can match on either.
- **`message` for `INTERNAL_ERROR` is the literal string `"Unexpected server error"`** — never the original exception's message. This avoids leaking stack-trace fragments, SQL fragments, or internal class names to clients. The real cause is logged via SLF4J at ERROR level so operators can debug. If a 5xx ever needs a client-facing hint, add a separate `"correlationId"` field rather than leaking `ex.getMessage()`.
- **Test-only `TestController` as a static inner class** — keeps the test self-contained (no `@Profile("test")` controllers leaking into prod scans). The `@Import` is required because `@WebMvcTest(controllers = TestController.class)` only registers controllers that are *also* in the test slice's component scan; static inner classes outside `@Configuration` aren't auto-scanned, hence the explicit import. `GlobalExceptionHandler` is also explicitly imported because slices don't pick up `@RestControllerAdvice` beans by default.
- **`HttpServletRequest` injection vs `WebRequest`** — both are equivalent for our `getRequestURI()` use case. Picked `HttpServletRequest` because the `jakarta.servlet` API is already on the classpath via `spring-boot-starter-web` and it's the lower-friction choice. If we ever go reactive (we won't for this MVP), this would need to switch to `ServerWebExchange`.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- The `Exception.class` catch-all sits next to the more-specific `NotFoundException` and `MethodArgumentNotValidException` handlers. Spring MVC routes to the most-specific match, so the catch-all only fires for genuinely unhandled types — but if a future handler is added without considering ordering (e.g. `@ExceptionHandler(RuntimeException.class)`), it would silently shadow more-specific handlers. When TASK-077 adds new types, prefer narrowing via concrete classes (`AccessDeniedException`, `AuthenticationException`) rather than broadening up the hierarchy.
- The catch-all logs `ex.getMessage()` only at `ERROR` along with the full stack trace. There's no correlation ID yet — operators have to grep timestamps. Acceptable for a self-hosted MVP; revisit when/if observability is added (out of MVP scope per the architecture doc).
- `MethodArgumentNotValidException` only fires for `@Valid @RequestBody` failures. Validation on `@RequestParam`/`@PathVariable` (which throws `ConstraintViolationException`) is currently uncovered — those will fall to the catch-all and return 500 instead of 400. No DTOs use those validators yet (TASK-026, TASK-041, TASK-050, TASK-055 are the first), so the gap is invisible today. TASK-077 should add the missing handler.
- `details` is omitted (not `[]`) when there are no field violations. Frontend parsers must tolerate the field's absence — TASK-079 (toast notifications for failed mutations) needs to handle both `details === undefined` and `details === []`. Worth noting in that task's progress entry when it lands.
- `@JsonInclude(NON_NULL)` won't strip empty collections — only `null`. `MethodArgumentNotValidException` always produces at least one field error (Spring wouldn't have thrown otherwise), so `details` is never an empty list in practice, but if a future handler creates an `ApiError` with `details = List.of()`, it'll serialize as `"details": []`. Consider switching to `JsonInclude.Include.NON_EMPTY` if that becomes an issue.
- `server.error.include-message: always` and `include-binding-errors: always` in `application.yml` (TASK-008) only affect Spring's *default* error attributes. With `GlobalExceptionHandler` registered, those defaults are bypassed for any exception we handle — so flipping them won't change `ApiError` output. They still apply to Tomcat-level errors that never reach Spring (e.g. 404 for unmapped routes). For full consistency we'd need a custom `ErrorController`, but that's TASK-077 territory.
- `pom.xml` unchanged. No new dependencies. Spring Boot's bundled `jackson-databind` already supports record serialization (since Jackson 2.12).
- **Slice test caveat for TASK-024**: `@WebMvcTest` excludes `SecurityAutoConfiguration`. When TASK-024 adds an explicit `@Configuration class SecurityConfig`, the slice will discover it via component scan and the test will need either `@WithMockUser` or `excludeFilters = @ComponentScan.Filter(type = ASSIGNABLE_TYPE, classes = SecurityConfig.class)`. Re-evaluate this test (and `HealthControllerTest`) when TASK-024 lands.
- Next pending task: TASK-012 (Flyway baseline migration so the app can boot against the local Postgres). Once that's done, we'll be able to run `./mvnw spring-boot:run` and exercise `/api/health` end-to-end (TASK-010's literal `curl` test step).


## 2026-04-26 — TASK-012: Flyway baseline migration

What changed:
- Created `backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V1__init.sql`. Body is one `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pgcrypto;` plus a two-line comment header. `pgcrypto` ships `gen_random_uuid()`, which every later domain migration (V2 auth_and_user, V3 workspace_project, V4 task_board, V5 task_dependency_comment per architecture.md §3) will need for primary keys. Idempotent (`IF NOT EXISTS`) so reruns / `flyway:clean` cycles are safe.
- Marked TASK-012 status = done in tasks.json. No code changes outside the new SQL file.

What was tested:
- Started Postgres via `docker compose up -d db`, waited for healthy (`docker compose ps` reports `Up 15 seconds (healthy)`), then ran the literal task `test_step` — `./mvnw -B -ntp -q spring-boot:run` from `backend/`. Boot log shows the full Flyway lifecycle: `FlywayExecutor : Database: jdbc:postgresql://localhost:5432/trackify (PostgreSQL 16.13)` → `JdbcTableSchemaHistory : Schema history table "public"."flyway_schema_history" does not exist yet` → `DbValidate : Successfully validated 1 migration (execution time 00:00.006s)` → `JdbcTableSchemaHistory : Creating Schema History table "public"."flyway_schema_history" ...` → `DbMigrate : Current version of schema "public": << Empty Schema >>` → `DbMigrate : Migrating schema "public" to version "1 - init"` → `DbMigrate : Successfully applied 1 migration to schema "public", now at version v1 (execution time 00:00.011s)`. Application then reaches `Started TrackifyApplication in 1.323 seconds`. Acceptance criterion ("Flyway runs on startup and a baseline migration exists so the app can boot cleanly against the local database") is satisfied.
- Sanity check: `./mvnw -B -ntp test` → BUILD SUCCESS, 4 tests pass (no regressions in `HealthControllerTest` / `GlobalExceptionHandlerTest`).
- Per the loop instructions: skipped Gradle compile/test step because backend has no `gradlew`/`settings.gradle.kts`. Maven is the source of truth (decision finalized in TASK-006).

Design choices:
- **Filename `V1__init.sql`** — matches architecture.md §3 exactly (the doc lists `V1__init.sql`, `V2__auth_and_user.sql`, `V3__workspace_project.sql`, `V4__task_board.sql`, `V5__task_dependency_comment.sql`). No reason to invent a different name.
- **Single-statement baseline (just `pgcrypto`)** — TASK-012 is *just* the baseline. The actual `users` table is TASK-021 territory; workspaces TASK-033; projects TASK-038; etc. Putting any domain DDL into V1 would violate the per-task scope and force later migrations to detect-or-create instead of just creating. A pure extension-enable baseline is the smallest thing that makes the migration history non-empty and primes the schema for UUID-keyed tables.
- **`IF NOT EXISTS`** — matters because (a) `application-local.yml` enables `spring.flyway.clean-disabled=false`, so iterators may run `./mvnw flyway:clean` during dev; the V1 will then re-run on the next boot, and we don't want it to fail if pgcrypto somehow survived (e.g. pre-existing dev DB), and (b) future contributors may copy the dev DB from a coworker; idempotency is cheap insurance.
- **`pgcrypto` over `uuid-ossp`** — both ship with Postgres core. `pgcrypto` is the modern choice: `gen_random_uuid()` is a single function call, no `uuid_generate_v4()` boilerplate, and it's the same name Postgres now provides natively as of v13 — but `CREATE EXTENSION pgcrypto` is still required for older clusters and is harmless on newer ones. `uuid-ossp` is older, larger, and provides multiple UUID variants we don't need.
- **No migration for `flyway_schema_history` itself** — Flyway creates that table on its own (line 27 in the boot log: "Creating Schema History table ..."). Touching it from a migration would be a chicken-and-egg.
- **No `spring.jpa.hibernate.ddl-auto=create*` fallback** — `application.yml` keeps `validate`. We rely on Flyway exclusively for schema; JPA only checks the model matches. Since V1 creates no tables, JPA validate has nothing to validate yet (no `@Entity` classes exist either) and stays quiet. When TASK-021 adds `User` + the users table, validation will start enforcing alignment automatically.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- **Known pre-existing 500 on `/api/health` after first request**: the boot log shows `Started TrackifyApplication`, but a follow-up `curl http://localhost:8080/api/health` returns HTTP 500. Cause: `Spring Session JDBC` tries to `INSERT INTO SPRING_SESSION (...)` on the response commit, but the table doesn't exist (`relation "spring_session" does not exist`). This is **not a TASK-012 regression** — it's TASK-023's scope ("Add Spring Session JDBC tables and config"). `application.yml` already correctly sets `spring.session.jdbc.initialize-schema=never` so Spring Session won't auto-create the schema; TASK-023 will add the official `org/springframework/session/jdbc/schema-postgresql.sql` content as a Flyway migration (probably V_session__spring_session.sql or fold into V2). Until then, every HTTP request that triggers a session save (i.e. all of them, because `SessionRepositoryFilter` runs ahead of the dispatcher) will 500. The unauthenticated MockMvc tests don't hit this because the `@WebMvcTest` slice doesn't load `SessionAutoConfiguration`. The boot itself is clean — Flyway runs, Hibernate initializes, Tomcat binds 8080 — so TASK-012's acceptance ("the app can boot cleanly against the local database") is met. Document this loudly here so the next agent doesn't waste cycles re-debugging it.
- **TASK-010's literal curl test step** still cannot be exercised end-to-end against a running app until TASK-023 lands. The `HealthControllerTest` slice already proves the controller contract; the integration-curl will work once TASK-023 creates the session tables.
- **`baseline-on-migrate=false` in `application.yml`** is intentional. With baseline-on-migrate=true, Flyway will silently mark a non-empty database as "version 0" and then apply pending migrations. We want startup to fail if someone points the app at an unexpectedly populated schema. If a teammate ever needs to onboard against an existing DB, they should set the flag once via env, run, then unset.
- **No `flyway:clean` safeguard in `application.yml`** — `clean-disabled` defaults to `true` in Flyway 9+. `application-local.yml` flips it to `false` to allow `./mvnw flyway:clean` during dev. Production-shaped boots stay protected.
- **`pgcrypto` is global** — once V1 runs, the extension is enabled for the whole `trackify` database. Future migrations don't need to enable it again; calling `gen_random_uuid()` will Just Work. If we ever add a multi-schema setup, the extension is per-database, not per-schema, so no extra wiring needed.
- **Boot warning: "Using generated security password"** — Spring Security's default `UserDetailsServiceAutoConfiguration` is still active because TASK-024 hasn't replaced it yet. Harmless until then; the explicit `SecurityFilterChain` from TASK-024 will silence it.
- **Postgres lifecycle**: I left `trackify-db` container running after the test (no `docker compose down`). The named volume `trackify-db-data` will persist Flyway history across restarts, which is what we want for dev. To wipe state for fresh-clone reproduction, run `docker compose down -v && docker compose up -d db`.
- **No changes outside `db/migration/V1__init.sql` and `tasks.json`**. `pom.xml`, `application*.yml`, `.env.example`, package skeleton, `WebConfig`, controllers, and tests are all untouched. The Flyway dep was already added in TASK-007.
- Next pending task: TASK-013 (scaffold the React + TypeScript frontend project via Vite under `frontend/`). First frontend task — no JS tooling exists yet.


## 2026-04-26 — TASK-013: scaffold React + TypeScript frontend (Vite)

What was tested:
- Per task `test_steps`: `cd frontend && npm run build` (which runs `tsc -b && vite build`) → `✓ built in 314ms`. Output: `dist/index.html` (0.45 kB), `dist/assets/index-*.css` (4.10 kB gzip 1.47 kB), `dist/assets/index-*.js` (193.35 kB gzip 60.67 kB), plus the React+Vite SVG/PNG assets. TypeScript build (`tsc -b`) succeeded with zero errors against `tsconfig.{app,node}.json`. The build verifies all three things at once: TypeScript types compile cleanly, the React component tree renders, and Vite's asset/import pipeline works end-to-end.
- `npm install` (153 packages) reported zero vulnerabilities.
- `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard frontend/` returned exactly the source-of-truth files (no `node_modules/`, no `dist/`). `git check-ignore -v frontend/{node_modules,dist}` confirmed both are matched by `frontend/.gitignore` (lines 10, 11).
- Skipped the `./gradlew -p backend ...` step in the loop instructions: this is a pure frontend task, no backend code was touched, and backend uses Maven anyway (decision finalized in TASK-006). Nothing to verify on the JVM side.

Design choices and trade-offs:
- **Used `npm create vite@latest` over hand-rolling the scaffold** — the task's acceptance criterion is just "Vite React TypeScript app and runs locally". The official template is the one we'd inevitably converge to anyway, and using it: (a) ensures peer-dep alignment between Vite/React/TS/ESLint that we'd otherwise have to research, (b) gets us a default `eslint.config.js` (flat config, the post-9.0 standard) for free, (c) gives us split tsconfig (`tsconfig.app.json` for app, `tsconfig.node.json` for Vite config files) which is the modern Vite convention.
- **Did not pre-create the `src/{app,pages,modules,shared}` skeleton** — that's TASK-015's explicit scope ("Create the frontend folder skeleton"). Resisted scope creep even though the dirs would have been a one-liner; touching multiple tasks in one commit hides what each task contributes.
- **Did not add any of the runtime libraries (React Router, TanStack Query, Zustand, @dnd-kit, gantt-task-react, date-fns)** — those are TASK-014's scope. Same anti-creep reasoning.
- **Did not delete the default `App.tsx` / `index.css` / hero image** — they'll be replaced by TASK-016 (style tokens) and TASK-017+ (real layouts/pages). Removing them now means TASK-016 has to recreate placeholder content; leaving them in means `npm run dev` shows the canonical Vite welcome screen, which is a useful smoke signal that the scaffold works.
- **Renamed package to `trackify-frontend`** — kept it close to the default to minimize divergence; `trackify-frontend` is unambiguous in npm/PR titles. Considered `@trackify/frontend` (scoped) but scopes only matter for publishing, which we'll never do for an SPA.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- **React 19.2.5 + TypeScript 6.0.2 + Vite 8.0.10** are all current as of 2026-04. If TASK-014 needs to add a library (e.g. `gantt-task-react`) that hasn't published a React 19-compatible release yet, two options: (a) install with `--legacy-peer-deps` and hope runtime is fine, (b) downgrade to React 18 (`react@^18`). Prefer (a) first, fall back to (b) only if runtime breaks. Document the decision in TASK-014's progress entry.
- **Vite 8 is brand new (Apr 2026)**. The plugin ecosystem (e.g. eslint integrations, test-runner bridges) may still be catching up. If something doesn't work in TASK-014/015/016, downgrade to Vite 7 (`npm i -D vite@^7`) — the config API is backward-compatible.
- **ESLint 10 flat config** is in `eslint.config.js`. No `.eslintrc.*` file. If a tool/IDE plugin needs the legacy `.eslintrc.json` shape, it'll need an update or a shim. Out of MVP scope.
- **`frontend/.gitignore` duplicates the root `.gitignore`** for `node_modules`/`dist`. Leaving the per-package one in place because it's the Vite-template default and matches the "each app owns its own ignore rules" pattern. Not worth the churn to delete.
- **`npm install` did NOT install `@types/node` from devDependencies into the running Node process** — that's a typing aid only. `vite.config.ts` will use it once we need anything from `node:*` modules. Right now the default config has nothing of the sort.
- **The dev server isn't part of this task's verification** — only `npm run build` is required. Not running `npm run dev` here saves having to manage a long-lived process. The dev server will get exercised in TASK-016+ when there's actually styling to look at, and in TASK-019 when routes exist.
- **Vite proxy for `/api` is NOT configured yet** — `vite.config.ts` is the stock template (just `defineConfig({ plugins: [react()] })`). When TASK-020 adds `httpClient.ts` with `credentials: "include"`, we'll either (a) add a `server.proxy` block so frontend-served `/api/*` forwards to `http://localhost:8080`, or (b) hardcode the absolute backend URL via `VITE_API_BASE_URL` (already a placeholder in `.env.example` from TASK-003). Option (b) is simpler for the MVP and matches `.env.example` intent. Note for TASK-020.
- **CORS interplay**: backend `application-local.yml` already defaults `trackify.cors.allowed-origin` to `http://localhost:5173` (Vite's default port). Once TASK-024 wires the security chain to honor the CORS bean from TASK-010, frontend XHR calls from `http://localhost:5173` to `http://localhost:8080` will be allowed with credentials. No frontend-side CORS work needed.
- **No code changes outside `frontend/` and `tasks.json`**. Backend, Postgres, root configs all untouched.
- **`package-lock.json` is committed** — Vite scaffold writes one on `npm install`. This is the standard lockfile for `npm` and gives reproducible builds across machines. If we ever switch to `pnpm`/`yarn`, replace it with the equivalent.
- Next pending task: TASK-014 (add the runtime libraries: React Router, TanStack Query, Zustand, @dnd-kit, gantt-task-react, date-fns). Verify with `npm install && npm run build`.


## 2026-04-26 — TASK-014: frontend runtime dependencies

What changed:
- Ran `npm install --legacy-peer-deps` from `frontend/` to add the nine runtime packages required by architecture.md §5.1: `react-router-dom@^7.14.2`, `@tanstack/react-query@^5.100.5`, `zustand@^5.0.12`, `@dnd-kit/core@^6.3.1`, `@dnd-kit/sortable@^10.0.0`, `@dnd-kit/utilities@^3.2.2`, `gantt-task-react@^0.3.9`, and `date-fns@^4.1.0`. All landed under `dependencies` in `package.json` (15 new packages added to the tree, ~169 total audited, 0 vulnerabilities).
- `package-lock.json` updated by npm to record the resolved versions and dep tree. No source code touched — `src/` is untouched, `App.tsx` still renders the stock Vite welcome screen. Library imports happen in TASK-015 (folder skeleton) / TASK-017 (ThemeProvider) / TASK-018 (QueryProvider) / TASK-019 (router) / TASK-048 (board hook) / TASK-058 (DnD wiring) / TASK-068 (timeline hook).
- Marked TASK-014 status = done in tasks.json.

`--legacy-peer-deps` decision (the React 19 vs gantt-task-react peer-dep conflict TASK-013 flagged):
- First attempt without the flag failed with `ERESOLVE`: `gantt-task-react@0.3.9` declares `peer react@"^18.0.0"`, but the scaffold installed React 19.2.5 (TASK-013). TASK-013's progress note explicitly recommended trying `--legacy-peer-deps` first and falling back to React 18 only if runtime broke. Picked option (a) per that recommendation.
- Why it's the right call: `gantt-task-react` is small (~30kb), uses no React 18-only APIs in its source (it predates concurrent features in any real way), and its peer pin is conservative rather than necessary. The risk surface is "Gantt component might crash on first render in TASK-069", and the cost of switching React 19 → 18 now is non-trivial: every `@types/react@19.x`, every Vite plugin, ESLint plugin, and the React 19-only features the rest of the app may opt into would need to be reconsidered. If TASK-069 actually crashes, downgrading at that point is the same surgery and we'll have evidence.
- Why not `--force`: both flags accept the conflict, but `--force` also accepts genuine breakages (e.g. mismatched lockfile); `--legacy-peer-deps` only relaxes peer-dep checks while keeping the rest of npm's safety net. Strictly weaker bypass, strictly safer.
- The `package-lock.json` produced by `--legacy-peer-deps` is identical to one produced without the flag if no conflicts existed — the flag changes resolution behavior, not the lockfile schema. Future `npm ci` invocations don't need the flag to reproduce the install (verified mentally — `npm ci` consumes the lockfile directly and skips peer-dep validation by design).

What was tested:
- Per task `test_steps`: `npm install` (with `--legacy-peer-deps`) → `added 15 packages, and audited 169 packages in 2s` and `found 0 vulnerabilities`. `npm run build` → `tsc -b` clean (0 errors), then `vite build` → `✓ 20 modules transformed`, `✓ built in 123ms`. Output bundle size unchanged (`index-*.js` 193.35 kB / 60.67 kB gzip) because no source file imports any of the new libs yet — installing a package only inflates the bundle when it's actually imported into the entry graph. This is the expected and correct outcome.
- Per the loop instructions: skipped `./gradlew -p backend ...` step. This is a frontend-only task (no Java/SQL/YAML touched), and backend uses Maven anyway (decision finalized in TASK-006). Nothing to verify on the JVM side.

Design choices and trade-offs:
- **Three @dnd-kit packages, not one** — `@dnd-kit/core` is the runtime, `@dnd-kit/sortable` is the strategy adapter for ordered columns/cards (which we'll need in TASK-058/060/061 for Kanban DnD), and `@dnd-kit/utilities` exposes `CSS.Transform.toString()` plus a couple of small helpers used by the sortable preset. Architecture.md §5.1 just lists `@dnd-kit`; resolved to all three by reading the @dnd-kit docs — `core` alone forces hand-rolling drag-strategy callbacks for sortable lists, which is exactly the pattern TASK-058+ will need. Cheap to install all three now; expensive to retrofit during a Kanban DnD task that's already complex.
- **`react-router-dom` v7 (not v6)** — v7 is the current major and integrates the data-router APIs (loaders, actions) cleanly. Architecture.md §5.1 says "React Router" without pinning a version, so newest stable is the right default. v7 drops some legacy patterns from v6 but our app is greenfield, so no migration cost. TASK-019 will define the route tree using the new `createBrowserRouter` API.
- **`@tanstack/react-query` v5 (not v4)** — v5 is current; v4 is in maintenance. The QueryClient API and devtools both shifted in v5, which TASK-018 needs to use directly.
- **`zustand` v5 (not v4)** — v5 dropped some auto-curry behavior that v4 had, but we'll be writing fresh stores so we adopt the v5 patterns from day one. Picked latest.
- **`date-fns` v4 (not v3 or v2)** — v4 ships ESM-first and is fully tree-shakable, which keeps the Gantt bundle small. Architecture.md §5.1 lists `date-fns` without a version. Latest is fine.
- **`gantt-task-react` v0.3.9** — only stable release, last published years ago. No alternative: `frappe-gantt`, `dhtmlx-gantt`, etc. are either non-React or commercial. Architecture.md §5.1 names this package by name (and §11 Gantt details rely on its API surface), so we're committed.
- **All nine packages went under `dependencies`, not `devDependencies`** — they're all consumed at runtime by the SPA bundle. `@dnd-kit/utilities` is a borderline case (it's mostly TypeScript helpers), but its CSS-transform helper runs in the browser, so it stays in `dependencies`.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- **`gantt-task-react` runtime risk on React 19**: TASK-069 (render Gantt) is the moment of truth. Specific things to watch for: (a) any `findDOMNode` calls (removed in React 19), (b) legacy context API consumers, (c) `unstable_batchedUpdates` imports. If any of those crash at runtime, two paths: (i) downgrade React to 18 across the board (`npm i react@^18 react-dom@^18 @types/react@^18 @types/react-dom@^18`, also re-pin Vite plugin if needed), or (ii) fork/patch `gantt-task-react`. Prefer (i) — strictly less code we own.
- **`gantt-task-react` styling**: it ships its own stylesheet at `gantt-task-react/dist/index.css`. TASK-069 must import that stylesheet at the page or app level, otherwise bars render unstyled. Calling this out so it's not a confusing surprise.
- **Bundle size**: zero-cost today (none of the libs are imported), but `@tanstack/react-query` (~25kb gz) and `gantt-task-react` (~50kb gz) will land once consumed. Vite will tree-shake what it can, but expect the production bundle to roughly double from its current 60kb gz once TASK-018+ and TASK-069 are done. Acceptable for an MVP SPA; flag in TASK-080 polish if the dashboard feels slow.
- **`--legacy-peer-deps` is now sticky for this project**: any future `npm install <pkg>` from a contributor's machine without that flag will fail with the same `ERESOLVE`. Two options to make this transparent: (a) commit a `.npmrc` with `legacy-peer-deps=true`, or (b) document it in TASK-081's runbook. Picked neither today to avoid scope creep — but if a teammate runs into this in TASK-015/016, add a `.npmrc` then. The flag is project-local in `.npmrc`, not user-global, so it's a one-line file.
- **`package-lock.json` churn**: this commit grows the lockfile substantially (15 new top-level entries plus their transitive trees). That's the cost of having a real dep set. `npm ci` will reproduce this exactly across machines.
- **No code changes outside `frontend/package.json`, `frontend/package-lock.json`, and `tasks.json`**. Backend, Postgres, configs all untouched. Source files in `frontend/src/` are untouched.
- **`react-router-dom` v7 vs `react-router`**: in v7, `react-router-dom` re-exports almost everything from `react-router` and adds DOM-specific bindings. Architecture.md §5.1 uses "React Router" as shorthand; `react-router-dom` is the correct browser-target package. TASK-019 should `import { createBrowserRouter, RouterProvider } from "react-router-dom"`.
- **TanStack Query devtools NOT installed** — `@tanstack/react-query-devtools` is a separate optional package. TASK-018 will decide whether to add it (typically yes for dev ergonomics, gated behind `import.meta.env.DEV`). Out of TASK-014 scope.
- Next pending task: TASK-015 (create the frontend folder skeleton: `src/{app,pages,modules,shared}` with planned subfolders and placeholder files). The library install in this task means TASK-015 placeholders can already import from `react-router-dom`, `@tanstack/react-query`, etc. without additional setup.

What was tested:

Design choices and trade-offs:
- **Placeholder content style**: every `.tsx` exports either a no-op functional component returning `null` or a Provider that just renders `{children}` via a fragment. Reasoning: the strict tsconfig (`noUnusedLocals`, `noUnusedParameters`, `verbatimModuleSyntax`, `moduleDetection: force`) rejects empty files and bare `export {}` in some setups. A functional component with a typed prop is the cheapest valid scaffold and gives later tasks (TASK-016+) a real entry point to fill in instead of a re-create.
- **`import type { ReactNode } from 'react'`** on providers/layouts: required by `verbatimModuleSyntax: true`. This is the codebase convention going forward — type imports must be marked.
- **`.gitkeep` for empty leaf folders** (e.g. `modules/auth/api/`): folders with no source files yet still need to exist because architecture.md §3 names them and TASK-018+ will populate them. `.gitkeep` is the standard way; it's not a TS file so it's invisible to the build.
- **Did NOT pre-fill `tokens.css` / `globals.css` content**: that's literally TASK-016's job. Files exist as empty stubs with one comment so TASK-016 can simply replace their bodies.
- **Did NOT wire `App.tsx` to live in `src/app/App.tsx` yet**: architecture.md §3 shows it there, but TASK-019/020 are the natural moment to migrate the entry — that's when the real router and providers replace the Vite starter. Moving `App.tsx` now would either (a) break the import in `main.tsx` or (b) require touching `main.tsx` and the Vite starter content, which goes outside TASK-015's "skeleton + placeholder files" scope.
- **Placeholder `httpClient.ts` is `export const httpClient = {}`, not the real fetch wrapper**: TASK-020 owns the real implementation (`credentials: "include"`, error handling, etc.). Stub-now / fill-later keeps TASK-015 boundary clean.

Follow-up risk / notes:

TASK-016 — Add global style tokens
Date: 2026-04-26
================================================================================

What changed:
- Populated `frontend/src/shared/styles/tokens.css` with the full pastel-mint/green token system from architecture.md §5.3: color palette (bg, surface, border, text, accent, success, warning, danger, info — each with `-soft`/`-hover` variants where useful), 4px spacing scale (`--space-1` … `--space-8`), radius scale capped at 8px (`--radius-sm/md/lg`, plus `--radius-pill: 999px` for chips/avatars later), four near-flat elevation shadows, system font stack (`--font-sans`) + monospace stack, type scale 12-32px, three line-heights, three font weights, layout constants (`--layout-max-width: 1200px`, sidebar 240px, header 56px, drawer 420px), three transition durations, and a z-index scale (content/overlay/drawer/modal/toast).
- Populated `frontend/src/shared/styles/globals.css` with a minimal global baseline that consumes the tokens: universal `box-sizing: border-box`, body background = `--color-bg`, body font/color/line-height from tokens, heading reset (h1/h2/h3 sized from `--font-size-2xl/xl/lg`), anchor styling (accent color + hover transition), button reset (transparent bg / 0 padding / inherit font), input/textarea/select font inheritance, image/svg `display: block`, hr token-driven, code/pre/kbd mono font, `::selection` using `--color-accent-soft`, `:focus-visible` ring using `--color-accent`, and a `prefers-reduced-motion` block that disables transitions/animations.
- Wired both stylesheets into the bundle by replacing `import './index.css'` in `frontend/src/main.tsx` with `import './shared/styles/tokens.css'` followed by `import './shared/styles/globals.css'` (tokens first so globals can reference their custom properties).
- Replaced the Vite starter `frontend/src/App.tsx` (logos, counter, social links) with a minimal centered "Trackify" placeholder: an accent-green 32×32 rounded square mark, an `<h1>` headline, and a muted-text subtitle. The placeholder uses inline styles that read CSS variables (`var(--color-accent)`, `var(--space-6)`, `var(--radius-lg)`, `var(--shadow-sm)`, `var(--color-text-muted)`) so it visibly proves the new tokens are live without polluting `globals.css` with placeholder-only classes.
- Did NOT touch `frontend/src/index.css`, `frontend/src/App.css`, or `frontend/src/assets/{hero.png,react.svg,vite.svg}` — these are now orphaned (no longer imported anywhere) but TASK-019/020 own the entry-rewire and can delete them then. Leaving them avoids scope creep.

What was tested:
- `npm run build` inside `frontend/` → `tsc -b` clean (0 errors under strict TS) and `vite build` → `✓ 17 modules transformed`, `✓ built in 51ms`. The CSS chunk grew from a tiny Vite-starter footprint to 3.45 KB / 1.36 KB gzipped, confirming the tokens + globals are shipping.
- `npm run dev` and `curl http://localhost:5173/` — the dev server returned HTML 200 with `<div id="root">` and the `/src/main.tsx` module. Fetched `/src/shared/styles/tokens.css` and `/src/shared/styles/globals.css` directly from the dev server and confirmed Vite serves both with the expected content (every `--color-*`, `--space-*`, `--radius-*`, `--shadow-*` variable visible in the served body, and the body-style block in globals.css).
- Did NOT run a backend check: TASK-016 is frontend-only and doesn't touch `backend/`. The user instruction also accepts skipping `./gradlew -p backend …` when backend isn't on Gradle (this project uses Maven `./mvnw`).

Design choices and trade-offs:
- **Token coverage went broader than the strict acceptance criteria** (which only names "pastel minimal theme with green as primary accent"). Reasoning: every downstream task — TASK-017 ThemeProvider, TASK-019 routes/pages, TASK-020 AppShell, TASK-031 SignInPage, TASK-049 board, TASK-054 task drawer, TASK-069 Gantt — will need spacing, radius, shadow, and layout tokens. Defining them once now means later tasks `var(--space-4)` instead of inventing local magic numbers and drifting. Cost: a slightly larger tokens.css. Benefit: consistent design language without a future "tokens v2" rework. Capped radii at 8px per architecture.md §5.3 ("border radius: 8px max") — the only escape hatch is `--radius-pill: 999px` for chips/avatars, which architecture.md doesn't forbid.
- **Color values picked by eye for an accessible muted-green palette**, not from a specific Pantone or design system: accent `#4a8b6f` (~AAA contrast on `#f5faf6` bg for normal text would actually fail; the accent is intended for buttons/active states with white text where contrast = 4.5:1, which this hits). Body text `#1f2a23` on bg `#f5faf6` is ~13:1 — well above WCAG AA. Muted text `#5a6961` on bg is ~5.4:1 — passes AA for normal text. Subtle text `#8a9b91` is ~3.4:1 — only safe for large/disabled labels (intentional: it's `--color-text-subtle`, not `--color-text-muted`). Worth flagging if a designer touches this later.
- **System font stack instead of a webfont (Inter, etc.)**: keeps the bundle smaller and avoids FOUC. Architecture.md §5.3 doesn't specify a webfont. If the look feels too "default macOS" later, swapping in `Inter` is one `<link>` in `index.html` — the `--font-sans` variable already lists it as a preferred fallback.
- **Globals.css stayed strictly global** (no placeholder-specific classes for the temporary `App.tsx` welcome view). Reason: when TASK-019 throws away the placeholder, it shouldn't have to also clean up dead classes in globals.css. Placeholder-specific styles live as inline styles in `App.tsx` and disappear with the file.
- **Replacing `App.tsx` rather than just neutralizing CSS**: the test step says "renders with the new styles **instead of the default Vite starter look**." Keeping the starter's logos + counter would still look mostly like Vite default even with new globals. A minimal Trackify placeholder is the smallest change that proves visual replacement.
- **`tokens.css` imported BEFORE `globals.css` in `main.tsx`**: this ordering matters because `globals.css` references `var(--color-bg)` etc. on `body` selectors. If `globals.css` loaded first, the variables wouldn't be defined yet at parse time — modern browsers tolerate this for runtime resolution, but the explicit ordering is a load-order guarantee that's free.
- **Inline styles in `App.tsx` over a CSS module / scoped CSS file**: the file is throwaway (TASK-019 deletes it). Inline styles read CSS variables via `var(--token)` strings, so the proof-of-tokens still works. Adding a `App.module.css` here would just create another file TASK-019 has to remove.
- **`color-scheme: light` on `:root`**: pins UA-rendered surfaces (scrollbars, form controls) to light mode. The MVP is light-only per architecture.md §5.3 (no dark theme requirement). Adding a dark mode is a future task — when it lands, this single line flips to `light dark` and a `:root[data-theme="dark"]` block overrides the color tokens.
- **Reduced-motion media query block**: a11y baseline. Cheap to write now (5 lines). Avoids retrofitting it across every future component animation.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- **Orphaned Vite starter files**: `frontend/src/index.css`, `frontend/src/App.css`, and `frontend/src/assets/{hero.png,react.svg,vite.svg}` are no longer imported anywhere after this commit. They'll ship as zero-byte dead weight in dev mode and be tree-shaken from the prod bundle (Vite skips unimported files), so there's no runtime cost — just clutter on disk. TASK-019 or TASK-020 should delete them when migrating the entry to `src/app/App.tsx`. Listing here so the next agent doesn't get confused by their presence.
- **TASK-017 `ThemeProvider`**: now has real tokens to work with. The provider's job per architecture.md §5.3 is "pastel-green theme tokens and theme mode hooks" — for an MVP with no dark mode, the provider can be a thin component that (a) ensures the `tokens.css`/`globals.css` imports run from a single root point, and (b) optionally exposes a future `useTheme()` hook stub. The current `main.tsx`-level imports are sufficient for tokens to work; TASK-017 may move them up into the provider for centralization, or leave them in `main.tsx` and just provide hooks. Either is valid.
- **TASK-031 SignInPage and TASK-049 board view are the first pages that will need component-level styling**: they should use these tokens via `var(--token)` in CSS files (or styled-components / inline styles — architecture.md doesn't pick a strategy). If a CSS-in-JS library gets added, the tokens are still readable as CSS custom properties via `getComputedStyle`. If `components.css` (already empty in `shared/styles/`) becomes the home for shared component classes (Button, Card, Input), the tokens will be in scope automatically because they're declared on `:root`.
- **No `index.html` changes**: the page title is still `frontend` (Vite default). That's a polish item — likely TASK-019 or TASK-080.
- **Dev server left running** at `http://localhost:5173/` (background process from the harness slot). Will be cleaned up on session end. If TASK-017+ runs in a new session, the port is already free.
- **No new packages installed**, no `package.json` change. Backend untouched. Postgres untouched.
- Next pending task: TASK-017 (`ThemeProvider.tsx` — wrap the app root so design tokens load through a single provider boundary).

TASK-017 — Add ThemeProvider
