- **Next pending task: TASK-026** (local user registration service). Lands a service in `auth.application` that creates a `User` row with a hashed password and rejects duplicate logins via `UserRepository.existsByLogin(...)`. The new `PasswordEncoder` bean is exactly the input it needs.


================================================================================
TASK-026 — Implement local user registration service
Date: 2026-04-26
================================================================================

What changed:
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/auth/application/RegisterUserCommand.java`: new value-object record carrying the four registration inputs (`login`, `email`, `displayName`, `rawPassword`). Lives in `auth.application` because it is the input contract for an application service, not a domain entity. Raw password lives only on the call stack — never reaches persistence.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/auth/domain/DuplicateLoginException.java`: new typed `RuntimeException` thrown when the caller asks to create a user whose login already exists. Lives in `auth.domain` because duplicate-login is a business-rule failure (domain rule: logins are unique), not an infrastructure concern. Surfacing a typed exception lets future TASK-027 / controller layers map it to a 409 cleanly instead of catching `DataIntegrityViolationException` and string-matching the constraint name.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/auth/application/UserRegistrationService.java`: new `@Service` with one method, `register(RegisterUserCommand)`, marked `@Transactional`. Order of operations: (1) password length check against `AuthProperties.password().minLength()`, (2) `userRepository.existsByLogin(...)` pre-check throwing `DuplicateLoginException`, (3) `passwordEncoder.encode(...)`, (4) `userRepository.save(new User(...))` with the hash. Returns the persisted `User` for downstream callers (TASK-027 login auto-flow, future seed runner). Constructor-injects `UserRepository`, `PasswordEncoder`, `AuthProperties` — all three already exist (TASK-022, TASK-025).
- `backend/src/test/java/com/trackify/auth/application/UserRegistrationServiceTest.java`: new pure JUnit + Mockito unit test, no Spring context. Three cases: (a) happy path verifies the service hashes the password (asserts persisted hash equals the encoder's stub output AND is not equal to the raw password — the property the test step calls out), captures the `User` via `ArgumentCaptor` and checks every field, and confirms the encoder ran exactly once; (b) duplicate-login case verifies `DuplicateLoginException` fires and that neither `passwordEncoder.encode(...)` nor `userRepository.save(...)` is called — the short-circuit ordering matters so we never burn a bcrypt round on a doomed request; (c) under-min-length case verifies the password rule fires before the existence check, so we never even hit the database for an obviously-bad request.

What was tested:
- `./mvnw -q compile`: clean (exit 0). New types compile against existing pom.
- `./mvnw test`: BUILD SUCCESS. Tests run: 10 (up from 7), Failures: 0, Errors: 0, Skipped: 0. New: `UserRegistrationServiceTest` x3 (`registerHashesPasswordAndPersistsUser`, `registerRejectsDuplicateLoginBeforeHashingOrSaving`, `registerRejectsPasswordShorterThanConfiguredMinLength`). Existing 7 tests unchanged. The `[ERROR]` stack visible mid-output is from `GlobalExceptionHandlerTest`'s expected `log.error` call when it asserts the 500-path — same as in TASK-024 / TASK-025 runs, not a regression.
- The user prompt asked for `./gradlew -p backend ...`. Project is Maven (`./mvnw`) per CLAUDE.md; ran the equivalent Maven steps. Same as prior tasks.

Design choices and trade-offs:
- **Pre-check `existsByLogin` instead of relying on the DB unique constraint**: a pre-check costs one extra SELECT but lets the service throw a typed `DuplicateLoginException` deterministically, instead of catching `DataIntegrityViolationException` and inspecting the constraint name (which is fragile across Postgres versions and across H2 if anyone ever spins up an in-memory test profile). Race condition still exists between the SELECT and the INSERT, but the DB unique index is the actual guard — at worst the loser of a race gets a 500 from the unhandled `DataIntegrityViolationException`, which is acceptable for an MVP. A future TASK-077 hardening pass can add a `@ExceptionHandler(DataIntegrityViolationException.class)` mapping if this ever happens in practice.
- **No email pre-check**: TASK-026's test step explicitly says "rejects a duplicate login" — singular, login-only. The `users` table has `UNIQUE` on email too (V2 migration), so a duplicate email ultimately fails at insert time. Adding a pre-check + `DuplicateEmailException` now would be scope creep with no covering test step. If email-collision UX matters later, it's a one-line addition. Keeping the surface area small now keeps the diff reviewable and the test boundary tight.
- **`IllegalArgumentException` for password-too-short, not a typed `WeakPasswordException`**: the password rule is one numeric threshold, the message embeds the configured min length, and consumers (controller layer, TASK-027) can catch `IllegalArgumentException` and translate it. Inventing a type hierarchy for a single rule is premature. Once registration grows multiple validations (banned passwords, special-char policy, etc.) or once a controller needs different status codes per rule, splitting into typed exceptions becomes worth it.
- **Validation in the service, not via Bean Validation on `RegisterUserCommand`**: Bean Validation would pull in `@NotBlank`, `@Email`, `@Size` — *but* the size constraint is dynamic (driven by `AuthProperties.password().minLength()`), which Bean Validation cannot do without a custom `ConstraintValidator`. Keeping the rule in the service keeps the dynamic-config link explicit and lets the test exercise the real coupling. Static rules (`@NotBlank` login/email, `@Email` email format) belong on a future request DTO at the controller boundary (TASK-027 territory), not on this internal command.
- **`@Transactional` on the service method**: only one repository write today, so the boundary is trivial. Marked anyway because TASK-027 will likely auto-login the new user (loading + setting authentication context within the same transaction) and TASK-034 will piggyback workspace creation on registration. Setting the boundary now means future additions don't introduce subtle "did this commit yet" bugs.
- **Returning `User` vs returning the new id (UUID) vs returning a DTO**: returning the entity is the path of least resistance for TASK-027 (it can immediately call `SecurityContextHolder.getContext().setAuthentication(...)` with the loaded principal) and for TASK-034 (workspace creation needs the user id). Risk: the entity escapes the application layer. Mitigation: only `auth.application` and the future `auth/controller` consume it, and the controller will map to a DTO before serialization (architecture.md §7.1). If/when this contract leaks across modules, a return-type refactor is mechanical.
- **Not adding a `UserDetailsService` here**: that is TASK-027's territory (login + Spring Security wiring). Adding it now would mean adding a bean that nothing consumes, which would silence the auto-config "Using generated security password" warning prematurely and tangle TASK-026/027 boundaries.
- **Test uses `ArgumentCaptor` instead of stubbing `save` to return a fixed value**: lets the test verify the *exact* `User` instance the service constructed (including that the password field is the hash, not the raw input) instead of trusting whatever `save` returns. Catches a class of bugs where the service might accidentally pass `command.rawPassword()` to the `User` constructor.
- **Test does not boot Spring**: pure Mockito + JUnit 5. ~50 ms per test vs. ~1 s for `@SpringBootTest`. Three tests, all sub-200 ms total, no Postgres needed — `./mvnw test` stays runnable on a clean clone.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- **TASK-027 (login endpoint)**: now has all the building blocks. Two viable patterns: (a) build a custom `UserDetailsService` that loads `User` by login, then plug it into Spring Security's `DaoAuthenticationProvider` (auto-configured because both `UserDetailsService` and `PasswordEncoder` beans exist), and use `AuthenticationManager.authenticate(...)` from a `@PostMapping("/api/auth/login")`. (b) hand-roll the credential check by calling `userRepository.findByLogin(...)` and `passwordEncoder.matches(raw, user.passwordHash())`. (a) is more idiomatic Spring Security and lights up the rest of the framework's auth infrastructure (e.g., method security `@PreAuthorize`); (b) is simpler but bypasses framework checks. Recommend (a). Either way, after credential validation the controller calls `SecurityContextHolder.getContext().setAuthentication(...)` and Spring Session JDBC persists the session.
- **TASK-027 controller will need `POST /api/auth/register`**: per architecture.md §11. The controller will accept a request DTO (with Bean Validation), translate to `RegisterUserCommand`, call `userRegistrationService.register(...)`, and return a session (auto-login is the natural UX). This task did not add the controller because TASK-026 is scoped to "service" only — adding a controller now would tangle the `tasks.json` boundary.
- **TASK-034 (auto-create personal workspace on first login)**: the cleanest place to hook this is *after* a successful login (reuses the path for both registered and pre-existing users) — not inside `UserRegistrationService.register(...)`. Putting it in registration would miss the case where TASK-027 needs to create a workspace for the bootstrap admin (which is seeded directly via the registration service from a runner, not via login). TASK-027 / TASK-034 will resolve this by checking-and-creating in the login success handler. Flag: do not add `WorkspaceService.createPersonal(...)` to `UserRegistrationService` even when it's tempting; keep registration single-purpose.
- **No `AUTH_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_*` seed runner yet**: the env-var-driven seed admin still has no `ApplicationRunner` consumer. With `UserRegistrationService` now in place, that runner is a ~15-line class that reads `AuthProperties.bootstrap().admin()`, returns early on blank login, and calls `register(...)`. Likely lands in TASK-027 or alongside the first manual smoke-test setup. Flag: any seed runner must run *after* Flyway has executed (default behavior of `@Component` with `ApplicationRunner` already satisfies this) and must tolerate a re-run (the duplicate-login pre-check makes it idempotent for free — second boot throws `DuplicateLoginException`, which the runner can swallow with a log line).
- **Concurrent-registration race**: two simultaneous calls with the same login both pass `existsByLogin` (returns false), both proceed to `save`, second one fails on the unique constraint with `DataIntegrityViolationException`. Today that surfaces as a 500 via `GlobalExceptionHandler.handleGeneric`. Acceptable for MVP. Hardening (TASK-077): add `@ExceptionHandler(DataIntegrityViolationException.class)` that returns 409. Not in scope here.
- **Password length check ignores leading/trailing whitespace**: `"        "` (8 spaces) passes the length-8 check. For an MVP, fine — bcrypt hashes whatever it gets. A future password-policy task can enforce non-whitespace + complexity rules. Flagging because it surprised me when reviewing the test — the rule is purely length, intentionally.
- **`User` entity has setters**: `User.setPasswordHash`, `setLogin`, etc. exist (carried over from TASK-022). Registration service uses the constructor only and never touches setters, so it is not exercising the mutability today. Setters might be removed in a later refactor when the entity is closed off — does not affect TASK-026.
- **No frontend changes**: the registration UI is not yet defined as a task. TASK-031 is sign-in only. A future task will add a registration form if/when self-signup is enabled.
- **No DB / migration changes**: pure service code. V1 (users) and V2 (session) unchanged.
- **Reviewer note for TASK-027**: when the login controller calls `AuthenticationManager.authenticate(...)`, it expects `BadCredentialsException` for a wrong password and `UsernameNotFoundException` for a missing user. Spring's default behavior collapses the second into the first (to avoid login-existence enumeration via timing). Keep that default — exposing a distinct "user not found" status leaks user-existence info to anonymous attackers.
- **Next pending task: TASK-027** (login endpoint and session handling). Wires `POST /api/auth/login`, validates submitted credentials against the registered hash, and establishes a Spring-authenticated session backed by Spring Session JDBC (already migrated in TASK-023, configured in TASK-024). Will likely also introduce the `UserDetailsService` that loads a `User` by login.


================================================================================
TASK-027 — Implement login endpoint and session handling
Date: 2026-04-26
================================================================================

What changed:
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/auth/application/LocalUserPrincipal.java`: new `record` implementing Spring Security's `UserDetails`. Carries `userId` (UUID), `username` (login), and `passwordHash`. Carrying the persistent `userId` on the principal removes the need for downstream code (TASK-028 `/api/me`, future authorization checks) to do a second `findByLogin` lookup just to translate the authenticated name into the row primary key.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/auth/application/LocalUserDetailsService.java`: new `@Service` implementing `UserDetailsService`. Single method delegates to `UserRepository.findByLogin(...)` and wraps the result in a `LocalUserPrincipal`. Throws `UsernameNotFoundException` for a missing login; Spring Security's `DaoAuthenticationProvider` collapses that into `BadCredentialsException` so the API never leaks user-existence (the TASK-026 progress note flagged this exact concern).
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/auth/dto/LoginRequest.java`: new request record with `@NotBlank` `login` + `password`. Lives in `auth.dto` per architecture.md §7.1 ("DTOs cross controller boundaries").
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/auth/dto/LoginResponse.java`: minimal response record `{userId, login}`. The SPA hydrates the full profile from `/api/me` (architecture.md §9 step 6); this body just confirms the session was established and gives the client an immediate id without an extra round-trip.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/auth/controller/LoginController.java`: new `@RestController` mapped at `/api/auth`. `POST /api/auth/login` (1) builds a `UsernamePasswordAuthenticationToken` from the request, (2) calls the injected `AuthenticationManager` (which auto-builds a `DaoAuthenticationProvider` from `LocalUserDetailsService` + `PasswordEncoder`), (3) creates an empty `SecurityContext`, sets the returned `Authentication`, installs it on the holder, and (4) calls `securityContextRepository.saveContext(...)` so the context is written to the HTTP session — Spring Session JDBC then persists it to `SPRING_SESSION` / `SPRING_SESSION_ATTRIBUTES`. A controller-local `@ExceptionHandler(AuthenticationException.class)` returns a 401 with the shared `ApiError` envelope (`error: "INVALID_CREDENTIALS"`).
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/config/SecurityConfig.java`: two new beans + one filter-chain edit. (a) `AuthenticationManager` exposed via `AuthenticationConfiguration.getAuthenticationManager()` so the controller can inject it. (b) `SecurityContextRepository` exposed as a `HttpSessionSecurityContextRepository` so the controller has a concrete handle to save into and the filter chain knows which repository to read from. (c) The `securityContext(sc -> sc.securityContextRepository(...))` line wires that repository into the chain so on subsequent requests `SecurityContextHolderFilter` reads the persisted context back out — this is required in Spring Security 6 because it switched from automatic save (`SecurityContextPersistenceFilter`) to explicit save via the holder filter + repository.
- `backend/src/test/java/com/trackify/auth/controller/LoginControllerTest.java`: new `@WebMvcTest(LoginController.class)` slice. Imports `SecurityConfig`, `WebConfig`, `JacksonConfig`, `AuthSecurityBeans`, `LocalUserDetailsService`. Mocks `UserRepository` via `@MockitoBean` (Spring Boot 3.4's replacement for `@MockBean`). Three tests: (1) success — encodes the test password with the real `PasswordEncoder`, stubs `findByLogin`, hits `POST /api/auth/login`, asserts 200 + JSON body matches the principal, then reads the request's `HttpSession` and asserts the `SPRING_SECURITY_CONTEXT` attribute is a populated `SecurityContext`; (2) wrong password — same fixture, posts the wrong password, asserts 401 + `INVALID_CREDENTIALS` body, asserts no `SecurityContext` was persisted to the session; (3) unknown login — `findByLogin` returns empty, asserts the same 401 envelope (proves we don't expose user-not-found vs wrong-password).

What was tested:
- `./mvnw -q compile`: clean (exit 0). New types and modified `SecurityConfig` compile against existing pom.
- `./mvnw -q compile test-compile`: clean (exit 0).
- `./mvnw test`: BUILD SUCCESS. Tests run: 13 (up from 10), Failures: 0, Errors: 0, Skipped: 0. New: `LoginControllerTest` x3 (`successfulLoginEstablishesAuthenticatedSessionAndReturnsPrincipalIdentity`, `invalidPasswordReturnsUnauthorizedAndDoesNotEstablishSession`, `unknownLoginReturnsUnauthorized`). Existing 10 tests unchanged. The mid-output `[ERROR]` log line is `GlobalExceptionHandlerTest`'s expected `log.error` from the 500-path assertion — same noise as in TASK-024/025/026 runs, not a regression.
- The user prompt asked for `./gradlew -p backend compileJava compileTestJava` and `./gradlew -p backend test`. Project is Maven (no `gradlew`/`settings.gradle.kts`) per CLAUDE.md ("always use the wrapper `./mvnw`"); ran the equivalent Maven steps. Same convention as prior tasks.

Design choices and trade-offs:
- **`AuthenticationManager` from `AuthenticationConfiguration` instead of building a `DaoAuthenticationProvider` by hand**: with both `UserDetailsService` (LocalUserDetailsService) and `PasswordEncoder` beans on the context, Spring Security 6 auto-wires a `DaoAuthenticationProvider` and the global `AuthenticationManager` already knows about it. Pulling the manager out via `AuthenticationConfiguration` is the canonical pattern and avoids duplicating provider config in two places. Building a `DaoAuthenticationProvider` by hand would force me to also declare a separate `AuthenticationManager` and carefully avoid the global one — easy to introduce a "two managers, one wins" bug.
- **Custom `LocalUserPrincipal` instead of Spring's built-in `org.springframework.security.core.userdetails.User`**: the built-in carries only `username` + `password` + authorities. Every authorization check against a workspace/project membership ultimately needs the `users.id` UUID. Either I carry it on the principal (this approach) or I re-query `UserRepository.findByLogin(...)` on every request that needs a user id. The principal lives in memory inside the `SecurityContext` for the life of the session, so adding a UUID field has near-zero cost; re-querying the DB on every authenticated request to translate login → uuid would be wasteful. Carrying it from the start avoids a future churn task that would touch every authorization check.
- **`SecurityContextRepository` as an explicit `@Bean`, wired into both the filter chain and the controller**: in Spring Security 6 the default behaviour split into "read on entry" (handled by `SecurityContextHolderFilter`, which receives the repo from `securityContext(...)` config) and "write on exit" (the application now must call `saveContext` itself). If the controller built a `HttpSessionSecurityContextRepository` inline and the filter chain used a *different* default, reads and writes could land in different stores. Sharing one bean across both wiring points keeps them aligned. Default `HttpSessionSecurityContextRepository` is fine because Spring Session JDBC swaps the underlying `HttpSession` for a JDBC-backed one transparently — the repository never sees the difference.
- **Login response is `{userId, login}`, not `{}` and not the full user**: returning `{}` would be cleaner ("the cookie carries the truth, fetch /api/me") but forces the SPA to make a second round-trip before it can render. Returning the full user record from the login endpoint would couple this contract to the `/api/me` payload shape and make a future change to one rebuild the other. The compromise: the bare identity needed to confirm "yes you are signed in as X" lets the SPA show optimistic UI immediately while `/api/me` (TASK-028) hydrates the full profile.
- **`@ExceptionHandler(AuthenticationException.class)` on the controller, not on `GlobalExceptionHandler`**: TASK-024's progress note (and the `GlobalExceptionHandler` Javadoc) explicitly defer 401/403 mappings to TASK-077 ("Standardize 401, 403, and 404 API responses"). Adding a global mapping now would land in TASK-077's territory and force a re-decision later. The controller-local handler is scope-tight: it covers `LoginController`'s methods only, returns the `ApiError` envelope today, and TASK-077 can lift it to global without rewriting the body shape (or replace it entirely if the global rule diverges).
- **Catching the *broad* `AuthenticationException`, not just `BadCredentialsException`**: `DaoAuthenticationProvider` collapses `UsernameNotFoundException` into `BadCredentialsException` *only when* `setHideUserNotFoundExceptions(true)` is set (the default). If a future config change flips that — or if some other authenticator path throws `DisabledException`, `LockedException`, etc. — we still want a single-status 401 envelope rather than a 500 from `GlobalExceptionHandler.handleGeneric`.
- **`AuthenticationException` subclasses *do not* include `AccessDeniedException`**: that one is for *authorized* users hitting forbidden resources (a 403 case) — it extends `RuntimeException` directly, not `AuthenticationException`. So this handler will not accidentally swallow the future TASK-076 forbidden-membership case.
- **`@WebMvcTest` slice instead of `@SpringBootTest`**: `@SpringBootTest` would try to start JPA + Flyway + the real Postgres — none of which are needed to test the controller's auth orchestration. The slice mocks `UserRepository`, runs the real `AuthenticationManager` + `PasswordEncoder` + `LocalUserDetailsService`, and produces the same authentication path as production for the bits that matter. Test runs in <500ms with no DB dependency.
- **`@MockitoBean` instead of `@MockBean`**: `@MockBean` is deprecated in Spring Boot 3.4 in favor of `@MockitoBean` (package `org.springframework.test.context.bean.override.mockito`). Using the new annotation now keeps the test compatible across the upgrade path; the behaviour is identical for our case.
- **`ReflectionTestUtils.setField(user, "id", uuid)` to set the UUID in tests**: `User`'s id is generated by JPA on insert (`@GeneratedValue(strategy = UUID)`) and there is no public setter. Using `ReflectionTestUtils` is the canonical Spring way to materialize a "persisted-looking" entity in a unit/slice test without booting JPA. The alternative (adding a package-private setter or a test-only constructor) would weaken the entity's invariants for the sake of a test.
- **Test password uses encoder strength 4 (vs. production default 10)**: configured via `@TestPropertySource`. BCrypt cost is 2^strength, so 4 is ~64x faster than 10. Doesn't change behavior; just keeps the slice test fast. Production-strength is exercised by `AuthSecurityBeansTest` already.
- **`JacksonConfig` added to imports**: the existing `@WebMvcTest` slice tests in this project don't all import it because they don't serialize record-shaped responses. `LoginResponse` is a record, and Jackson's record support has surprised people in the past. Importing the project's actual `JacksonConfig` makes the slice match production wiring. Defensive but cheap.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- **CSRF still disabled, mutating endpoint added**: the chain disables CSRF (TASK-024 progress note explains why: SameSite=Lax + restricted-origin CORS). With `POST /api/auth/login` now live, this is the first endpoint where the no-CSRF stance has real teeth. The SameSite=Lax cookie blocks cross-site state-changing POSTs. If/when the project ever moves to `SameSite=None` (cross-origin SPA), CSRF must be re-enabled before that ships.
- **`secure: true` cookie + local dev still unverified**: `SERVER_SERVLET_SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE=true` is the default; on plain-HTTP `http://localhost:8080` the browser will not attach the cookie and login will appear to succeed but every subsequent request will return 401. TASK-031 (sign-in UI) will hit this hard. Workaround for local dev: set `SERVER_SERVLET_SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE=false` in `.env` for the `local` profile.
- **`AuthenticationManager` bean now exposed app-wide**: this is necessary for `LoginController` but it does mean any other class can inject it and bypass the controller. Acceptable because every authenticator path still funnels through `DaoAuthenticationProvider` → `LocalUserDetailsService` → `UserRepository.findByLogin`.
- **`/api/auth/register` is *not* in this task**: TASK-027 is scoped to login. The SPA's TASK-031 sign-in flow is the only consumer of `/api/auth/login` in the MVP and does not call register.
- **TASK-028 (`GET /api/me`) prerequisites met**: the principal is a `LocalUserPrincipal` with `userId`. `/api/me` can call `SecurityContextHolder.getContext().getAuthentication().getPrincipal()`, cast to `LocalUserPrincipal`, and either return identity directly or do a `userRepository.findById(principal.userId())` to get the full row including `displayName`/`email`.
- **TASK-029 (`POST /api/logout`)**: Spring's auto-`/logout` is disabled in the filter chain (TASK-024). The custom logout will need to call `request.getSession().invalidate()` *and* explicitly clear the `SecurityContext` from the holder + via `securityContextRepository`.
- **TASK-034 (auto-create personal workspace on first login)**: the cleanest hook is *after* `AuthenticationManager.authenticate(...)` succeeds. A `@Component` `AuthenticationSuccessEvent` listener could do it without coupling `LoginController` to workspace creation. Flag: do not add workspace logic inline to `LoginController`.
- **`UserDetailsServiceAutoConfiguration` warning gone**: with `LocalUserDetailsService` registered, Spring Boot's auto-config backs off and the "Using generated security password" startup line no longer prints.
- **Controller-side `@ExceptionHandler` will *not* fire for `MethodArgumentNotValidException`**: the global handler in `GlobalExceptionHandler` already maps it to a 400 with field violations. So a missing/blank `login` or `password` returns a structured 400 instead of a 401 — which is correct (it's a request-shape problem, not a credentials problem).
- **Idempotency / session fixation**: posting login twice with the same credentials simply replaces the SecurityContext on the existing session. No session fixation risk *in this task* because the session id is stable; once TASK-076 hardening lands, we may want to call `request.changeSessionId()` after a successful login. Flag for TASK-076 / TASK-077.
- **No frontend changes**: the auth client + sign-in UI land in TASK-030 / TASK-031. The contract this task ships (`POST /api/auth/login` request: `{login, password}`, response: `{userId, login}`, error: `ApiError`) is what those tasks consume.
- **No DB / migration changes**: pure Java. V1, V2, V3 unchanged. `flyway_schema_history` unchanged.
- **Next pending task: TASK-028** (`GET /api/me`). Reads `SecurityContextHolder.getContext().getAuthentication().getPrincipal()`, casts to `LocalUserPrincipal`, and returns the current user payload. Acceptance test covers authenticated + unauthenticated cases — the unauthenticated 401 is already proven by the `SecurityFilterChain` config; TASK-028 only needs to add the authenticated-200-with-payload case.


================================================================================
TASK-028 — Implement GET /api/me
Date: 2026-04-26
================================================================================

What changed:
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/user/dto/MeResponse.java`: new record exposing `id` (UUID), `login`, `email`, `displayName`. The `passwordHash` field is never included — only these four identity-relevant fields are serialized. Lives in `user.dto` because the controller that produces it lives in `user.controller` and the data originates from the `user` domain.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/user/application/MeService.java`: new `@Service` with a single `@Transactional(readOnly = true)` method `getCurrentUser(UUID userId)`. Calls `UserRepository.findById(userId)` (inherited from `JpaRepository<User, UUID>` — no addition needed) and maps the returned `User` to a `MeResponse`. Throws `NotFoundException` if the row is absent (a defensive invariant; in practice the session cannot outlive the user row without a deliberate purge). No business logic beyond the lookup-and-map.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/user/controller/MeController.java`: new `@RestController` mapped at `/api/me`. Single `GET` handler resolves the authenticated `LocalUserPrincipal` via `@AuthenticationPrincipal`, extracts `principal.userId()`, and delegates to `MeService`. The controller has no logic, no `@Transactional`, and no exception handling — the `GlobalExceptionHandler` already maps `NotFoundException` to 404, and Spring Security handles the unauthenticated path before the controller runs.
- `backend/src/test/java/com/trackify/user/controller/MeControllerTest.java`: new `@WebMvcTest(MeController.class)` slice. Imports `SecurityConfig`, `WebConfig`, `JacksonConfig`. Mocks `MeService` via `@MockitoBean`. Two tests: (1) authenticated — installs a `LocalUserPrincipal`-carrying `UsernamePasswordAuthenticationToken` via Spring Security's `authentication()` request post-processor, stubs `meService.getCurrentUser(...)` to return a fixed `MeResponse`, asserts HTTP 200 + all four JSON fields match, asserts `passwordHash` is absent from the body; (2) unauthenticated — no security context, asserts 401 (Spring Security's `HttpStatusEntryPoint` fires before the dispatcher servlet).

What was tested:
- `./mvnw -q compile test-compile`: clean (exit 0). New types compile without error.
- `./mvnw test`: BUILD SUCCESS. Tests run: 15 (up from 13), Failures: 0, Errors: 0, Skipped: 0. New: `MeControllerTest` x2 (`authenticatedUserReceivesIdentityPayloadWithoutPasswordHash`, `unauthenticatedRequestIsRejectedWith401BeforeControllerRuns`). Existing 13 tests unchanged.

Design choices and trade-offs:
- **Controller location (`user.controller`, not `auth.controller`)**: architecture.md §11 groups `/api/me` with the auth/session section, which might suggest `auth.controller`. However, the data the endpoint returns is a `user`-domain projection (`User` entity → `MeResponse`), and the service that fetches it lives in `user.application`. Collocating the controller with its data domain (`user`) avoids cross-domain imports from `auth.controller` into `user.application`. The auth-grouping in architecture.md is an API-path concern, not a code-ownership concern. This interpretation is noted explicitly in the controller Javadoc.
- **Option 2 for principal design (keep principal lean, load full user from DB per request)**: `LocalUserPrincipal` carries only `userId` + `username` (login), not `email` or `displayName`. `/api/me` therefore does one `findById` per call. The alternative (Option 1: embed all profile fields in the principal at login time) would mean the session-stored `SecurityContext` serves stale `displayName` / `email` if the user updates their profile between login and `/api/me` calls. For a once-per-page-load identity probe the extra DB round-trip is negligible; correctness wins over saving one SELECT. This is the same trade-off documented in the task brief.
- **`@Transactional(readOnly = true)` on `MeService.getCurrentUser`**: marks the transaction as a non-modifying read, allowing the JDBC driver and connection pool to route to a read replica if one is ever added, and suppresses Hibernate's dirty-check scan on flush. Cost: one more Spring proxy method call. Worth it as a signal to future maintainers.
- **`NotFoundException` for missing user id**: if the user row were deleted while a session is still alive, `findById` returns `Optional.empty()` and `MeService` throws `NotFoundException`. `GlobalExceptionHandler` maps this to 404. The alternative is silently returning `null` or 401 — both are wrong (a deleted user with a dangling session is an integrity problem, not an "unauthenticated" state). This is defensive programming that costs nothing at runtime under normal conditions.
- **`@AuthenticationPrincipal` annotation on the controller parameter**: Spring MVC resolves the typed principal directly from the `SecurityContext` via `AuthenticationPrincipalArgumentResolver`. No manual cast needed. If the principal is not a `LocalUserPrincipal` (e.g., during a future OAuth2 flow), Spring will inject `null` and `principal.userId()` would NPE — but we want that to blow up loudly so we know OAuth2 session hydration is incomplete. Alternatively, we could assert `principal != null` at the top of the method — a future task (TASK-077 or the OAuth2 integration task) should add that guard once the second principal type exists.
- **Test uses `authentication()` post-processor, not `@WithMockUser`**: `@WithMockUser` installs a `UsernamePasswordAuthenticationToken` whose principal is a plain Spring `User` object, not a `LocalUserPrincipal`. The controller would throw a `ClassCastException` on `(LocalUserPrincipal) authentication.getPrincipal()`. The `authentication()` post-processor from `spring-security-test` lets us install the exact token and principal type the real flow would produce. This is the cleanest pattern for slice tests with a typed principal.
- **`@MockitoBean` instead of `@MockBean`**: Spring Boot 3.4 replacement, consistent with `LoginControllerTest`. Same behavior, forward-compatible.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- **TASK-029 (`POST /api/logout`)**: the `MeController` and `LoginController` are both in different packages under `com.trackify`. Logout will need to invalidate the session and clear the `SecurityContextHolder`. The same `SecurityContextRepository` bean used by `LoginController` should be injected into the logout controller (or a dedicated logout application service) to call `securityContextRepository.saveContext(SecurityContextHolder.createEmptyContext(), request, response)` after `request.getSession().invalidate()`. This pattern matches how `LoginController` persists the context.
- **TASK-030 / TASK-031 (auth HTTP client + sign-in UI)**: the SPA will call `GET /api/me` after a successful login (or on app load to restore session state). Response shape is `{id, login, email, displayName}` — camelCase, all strings/UUID. The frontend should handle 401 from `/api/me` as "not logged in" and redirect to sign-in.
- **TASK-034 (auto-create personal workspace on first login)**: `/api/me` is called after login to hydrate the profile. If TASK-034 hooks workspace creation onto the first-login event, it will have fired by the time `/api/me` is called — no coupling needed here.
- **TASK-077 (standardize 401/403/404 responses)**: unauthenticated requests to `/api/me` return 401 with an empty body (Spring Security's `HttpStatusEntryPoint` only sets the status). TASK-077 should install a custom `AuthenticationEntryPoint` that wraps the 401 in the `ApiError` envelope so the SPA can display a consistent error message. Today the SPA must handle a potentially empty body on 401 from this endpoint.
- **OAuth2 integration (future)**: if/when Google OAuth2 lands, a `SecurityContext` may hold an `OidcUser` principal, not a `LocalUserPrincipal`. The `@AuthenticationPrincipal LocalUserPrincipal` parameter will receive `null`. Add a null-check and delegate to a second lookup path (e.g., find user by OAuth2 subject) before that feature ships.
- **No DB / migration changes**: pure service + controller code. V1, V2, V3 unchanged.
- **No frontend changes**: `/api/me` is consumed by TASK-030/031.

================================================================================
TASK-029: implement POST /api/logout
Date: 2026-04-26
================================================================================

What changed:
- Added `LogoutController` (`com.trackify.auth.controller.LogoutController`) exposing `POST /api/logout`. Reads the current session via `request.getSession(false)`; if present, calls `session.invalidate()` so Spring Session JDBC drops the row in `SPRING_SESSION` / `SPRING_SESSION_ATTRIBUTES`. Then clears `SecurityContextHolder` for the in-flight request and returns `200` with `{ "success": true }`.
- Added `LogoutResponse` record in `com.trackify.auth.dto`, mirroring the minimal shape of `LoginResponse`.

Path placement:
- `/api/logout` is **not** under `/api/auth/**`. It falls under the catch-all `.requestMatchers("/api/**").authenticated()` rule in `SecurityConfig`, so unauthenticated callers get a 401 from `HttpStatusEntryPoint` before the controller method runs. This matches architecture.md §11 verbatim.
- `SecurityConfig` is unchanged. The Spring Security `.logout(...)` DSL stays disabled via the existing `.logout(AbstractHttpConfigurer::disable)` line. One logout path = one thing to reason about.

What was tested:
- `LogoutControllerTest` (slice test, mirrors `MeControllerTest`):
  - `authenticatedLogoutInvalidatesSessionAndClearsSecurityContext`: pre-seeds a `MockHttpSession` with a `SecurityContext` under `HttpSessionSecurityContextRepository.SPRING_SECURITY_CONTEXT_KEY` and authenticates the request via the `authentication(auth)` post-processor; asserts 200, body `$.success == true`, and `session.isInvalid() == true`.
  - `unauthenticatedLogoutReturns401`: no auth, asserts 401 from the security filter chain before the controller runs.
- Commands run from `backend/`:
  - `./mvnw test -Dtest=LogoutControllerTest` -> 2/2 pass.
  - `./mvnw test` (full suite) -> 17/17 pass. No regressions in `LoginControllerTest`, `MeControllerTest`, `UserRegistrationServiceTest`, `HealthControllerTest`, `GlobalExceptionHandlerTest`, `AuthSecurityIntegrationTest`, `TrackifyApplicationTests`.
- I did not run a manual end-to-end logout against a live Postgres + Spring Session JDBC stack. Session invalidation is verified at the contract level only, which matches how TASK-027/028 covered their session interactions and satisfies the `test_steps` in TASK-029.

Decisions / trade-offs:
- **Controller endpoint vs. Spring Security `.logout(...)` DSL.** The login flow is already a plain controller (TASK-027). A controller-based logout keeps the pair symmetric, sidesteps the DSL's redirect-oriented defaults (which target server-rendered apps, not SPAs), and is easier to slice-test than asserting against `LogoutFilter` post-handlers. The DSL stays disabled in `SecurityConfig`, so there is exactly one logout path.
- **JSON body instead of `204 No Content`.** Mirrors `LoginResponse` shape so the SPA's `httpClient.ts` can treat both auth endpoints uniformly. Trivial to extend later (e.g., `nextUrl`) without breaking the contract.
- **No idempotent unauthenticated logout.** Returning 200 for an unauthenticated logout would carve a magic exception into the `/api/**` authentication wall. The SPA bootstraps its session knowledge from `/api/me`; a 401 on logout-without-session is the right signal to clear local auth state and redirect to `/sign-in`.
- **`SecurityContextHolder.clearContext()` is belt-and-suspenders.** `HttpSessionSecurityContextRepository` already clears the request-scoped context on each request, but matching the canonical `SecurityContextLogoutHandler` behaviour protects any custom filter / interceptor that re-reads the holder later in this request's lifecycle (e.g., audit logging).

Follow-up risk:
- **TASK-030 (frontend `AuthProvider`)** needs a `logout()` helper that calls `POST /api/logout`, then resets the TanStack Query cache and Zustand auth state. Backend is ready; nothing further here.
- **TASK-077 (standardized 401/403/404 envelope)**: today's unauthenticated logout returns 401 with an empty body, same shape as `/api/me` (both go through `HttpStatusEntryPoint`). Once TASK-077 installs an `ApiError`-shaped entry point, this endpoint inherits the new shape for free.
- **CSRF**: `csrf.disable()` is still global. If we ever re-enable CSRF for cookie-auth defence-in-depth, this endpoint will need to be on the CSRF token check list. Worth flagging in the task that re-enables CSRF.
- **Session cookie cleanup**: the controller does not emit `Set-Cookie: SESSION=; Max-Age=0` to actively expire the cookie on the client. The server-side row is already gone, so any subsequent request with that cookie is anonymous — technically correct but slightly wasteful. Add `Set-Cookie` clearing later if it ever matters.
- **No DB / migration changes**: pure controller code. V1/V2/V3 unchanged.
- **No frontend changes**: SPA wiring lands in TASK-030/031/032.

================================================================================
TASK-030: add frontend auth client and AuthProvider
Date: 2026-04-26
================================================================================

What changed:
- `frontend/src/modules/auth/types/auth.ts`: new module with `AuthUser` (matches backend `MeResponse`: `{id, login, email, displayName}`), `LoginRequest` (`{login, password}`), `LoginResponse` (`{userId, login}`), `LogoutResponse` (`{success}`), and `AuthStatus = 'loading' | 'authenticated' | 'unauthenticated'`. Replaces the `.gitkeep` placeholder.
- `frontend/src/modules/auth/api/authApi.ts`: typed wrappers around the three backend endpoints — `authApi.me()`, `authApi.login(payload)`, `authApi.logout()`. All go through the shared `httpClient` so `credentials: 'include'` is preserved (per CLAUDE.md "Components and feature hooks must not call `fetch` directly"). Replaces the `.gitkeep` placeholder.
- `frontend/src/modules/auth/hooks/useAuth.ts`: new consumer hook. Reads `AuthContext`, throws if used outside `<AuthProvider>` (defensive — prevents silent null-context bugs in pages added later).
- `frontend/src/app/providers/AuthContext.ts`: new file holding the `AuthContext` and `AuthContextValue` type. Lives outside `AuthProvider.tsx` so the provider file exports only a component (satisfies the project's `react-refresh/only-export-components` ESLint rule for HMR).
- `frontend/src/app/providers/AuthProvider.tsx`: replaced the `children` pass-through stub with a real provider. Uses TanStack Query under `queryKeys.auth.me` to fetch `/api/me` once on mount with `staleTime: Infinity`, `retry: false`, `refetchOnWindowFocus: false`. The query function `fetchMeOrNull` translates a 401 from the backend into `null` (unauthenticated state) instead of a thrown error — TanStack Query stores `null` as data, not as an error, so unauthenticated is a normal data state, not a render-time exception. Other errors still throw so they surface as query errors. Exposes `{status, user, signIn, signOut, refresh}` via context. `signIn` calls `authApi.login`, then `refresh()` (a manual `queryClient.fetchQuery`) so the cached `auth.me` reflects the new session. `signOut` calls `authApi.logout`, then `queryClient.clear()` to drop any user-scoped cached data, then explicitly re-seeds `auth.me` with `null` so the provider immediately reports unauthenticated.
- `frontend/src/shared/api/queryKeys.ts`: added `auth.me` key so the provider and any later auth-related hook invalidate against the same cache entry.
- `frontend/src/main.tsx`: wraps `<App />` with `<AuthProvider>` between `<QueryProvider>` and `<ThemeProvider>`. AuthProvider must sit *inside* QueryProvider because it calls `useQuery`/`useQueryClient`. Theme stays innermost so it doesn't depend on auth state.

What was tested:
- `npm run build` (which runs `tsc -b && vite build`): clean. 87 modules transformed, build succeeded in ~80ms. TypeScript strict-mode types check across the new files. The TASK-030 `test_steps` only require `npm run build` to succeed and confirmation that the app root wraps itself with `AuthProvider` — both verified (`main.tsx` line 11 imports `AuthProvider`, lines 11–13 wrap `<App />`).
- `npm run lint`: 2 pre-existing errors in `src/app/routes.tsx` (unrelated to TASK-030 — `routes.tsx` exports both component and helper functions, flagged for HMR but functionally fine). My new code passes lint after extracting `AuthContext` to its own file.
- The user prompt asked for `./gradlew -p backend compileJava compileTestJava` and `./gradlew -p backend test`. This is a frontend-only task with no backend changes, so the backend build commands are not applicable here. CLAUDE.md also documents the project as Maven (`./mvnw`), not Gradle.

Design choices and trade-offs:
- **`/api/me` 401 → `null` data, not thrown error**: there are two reasonable shapes for "is the user signed in?" with TanStack Query. (a) Treat 401 as a query error and read `error?.status === 401` in components. (b) Map 401 to `null` in the queryFn so unauthenticated becomes a normal data state. I picked (b) because the consumer surface stays simple — `useAuth().status` is `'authenticated' | 'unauthenticated' | 'loading'`, no error branch needed for the most common case. Other status codes (500, network errors, etc.) still throw and surface via the query's error state — those are real failures, not "not signed in."
- **`staleTime: Infinity` + `retry: false` + `refetchOnWindowFocus: false`** on the me query: the session cookie's lifetime is controlled by the backend; refetching on every window focus would hammer `/api/me` for no benefit because the cookie has not changed since mount. Explicit refresh paths exist via `signIn`, `signOut`, and the public `refresh()` callback — those are the only places the auth state needs to re-fetch. `retry: false` matters specifically so a 401 doesn't trigger 1+ retry attempts (the global default is `retry: 1`).
- **`AuthContext` extracted to its own file**: the project has the `react-refresh/only-export-components` ESLint rule on. A file that exports both a component (`AuthProvider`) and a non-component value (`AuthContext`) breaks Fast Refresh boundaries. Splitting the context out is the standard React 19 + Vite HMR pattern. The same is true for `AuthContextValue` (a type) — putting it next to the context keeps consumers' imports tight.
- **`useAuth` lives in `modules/auth/hooks/`, not in `app/providers/`**: architecture.md §5.1 owns the providers; §5.4 places auth-domain code under `modules/auth`. The provider is app-shell infrastructure (how the app boots its session), but the consumer hook is a domain-feature concern (how a feature reads "current user"). Keeps the import direction one-way: features import from `app/providers/AuthContext`, never the other way around.
- **`queryClient.clear()` on signOut + explicit re-seed of `auth.me`**: when the user logs out, every cached query is potentially user-scoped (workspaces, projects, tasks). Selectively invalidating each key as those features land would force re-touching this file later. `clear()` drops everything once and is the safest signal; it also drops the now-stale `auth.me` entry, so I re-seed it as `null` immediately afterward so the provider's `useQuery` reads `null` synchronously instead of going back to the loading state.
- **`signIn` returns the full `AuthUser` or throws**: the SPA's sign-in flow (TASK-031) needs to know whether sign-in succeeded so it can navigate. Returning the user lets the page do `await signIn(...)` then redirect — no second `useAuth()` read needed. The thrown `ApiError` propagates the backend's 401 envelope (`INVALID_CREDENTIALS`) so the form can render the error message.
- **`refresh()` exposed as part of the context**: TASK-034 (auto-create personal workspace on first login) and any future auth-event listener may need to re-pull `/api/me` mid-session. Having `refresh` on the context surface keeps that one-line for callers and avoids `queryClient.invalidateQueries(...)` sprinkled everywhere with the literal key.
- **No new dependency added**: TanStack Query, React, and the existing `httpClient` cover everything. No new package installs.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- **TASK-031 (SignInPage)**: now has the API surface it needs. The page will call `useAuth().signIn({login, password})`, then navigate to `/` on success or render the thrown `ApiError.message` on failure. The 401 shape from the backend includes `code: 'INVALID_CREDENTIALS'`; the SignInPage can branch on that for a localized error message.
- **TASK-032 (protected route behavior)**: the `useAuth().status === 'unauthenticated'` check is the redirect signal. While `status === 'loading'` (very brief — only the initial `/api/me` round-trip), routes should render a neutral loading shell rather than redirecting to sign-in, otherwise a freshly-loaded authenticated user would briefly see the sign-in page before the me-query resolves. This is a TASK-032 concern; the provider already separates loading from unauthenticated.
- **Backend cookie `secure: true` in local dev**: TASK-027's progress note flagged that `SERVER_SERVLET_SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE=true` will block the cookie on plain `http://localhost:8080`. The first hit will be when this provider tries to call `/api/me` from a non-HTTPS dev origin. Workaround in `.env` for local dev: `SERVER_SERVLET_SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE=false`. This is documented in TASK-027 progress; not re-fixed here because it is an infra/.env concern, not a TASK-030 acceptance criterion.
- **`VITE_API_BASE_URL`**: `httpClient` defaults to `/api`, which means in dev the SPA expects either a Vite proxy or same-origin reverse-proxy fronting `:8080`. There is no Vite proxy configured today. TASK-031 will need either `VITE_API_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8080/api` in `.env` *or* a `vite.config.ts` proxy entry. Cross-origin cookie auth works because (a) `httpClient` already sets `credentials: 'include'` and (b) the backend `WebMvcConfigurer` was configured for the frontend origin in TASK-010.
- **No backend changes**: this task is pure frontend wiring against contracts that TASK-027/028/029 already shipped. V1/V2/V3 unchanged. No new backend tests needed.
- **No imports from later modules**: workspace/project/task modules don't exist yet. The `auth.me` query key is the only key currently in `queryKeys`; new domains will add their own siblings without touching auth.
- **Next pending task: TASK-031** (SignInPage). It will consume `useAuth().signIn` directly — no intermediate hooks/forms factory needed at this stage.

================================================================================
TASK-031: build SignInPage
Date: 2026-04-26
================================================================================

What changed:
- `frontend/src/pages/SignInPage.tsx`: replaced the placeholder with a real sign-in form. Controlled inputs for `login` and `password` (local `useState` — form drafts are local-state per architecture.md §6 / CLAUDE.md state-boundary rules, never Zustand/Query). Calls `useAuth().signIn({ login: login.trim(), password })` from `modules/auth`. On success: `useNavigate('/', { replace: true })` so the back button does not return to the sign-in page after a successful login. On failure: branches on `ApiError.code === 'INVALID_CREDENTIALS'` (the backend's known 401 envelope from TASK-027) to render "Invalid login or password." inline; falls back to the `ApiError.message` for other API failures and to a generic "Sign-in failed. Please try again." for non-API errors (network, etc.).
- The page renders a card-shaped `<form>` inside the existing `AuthLayout` (already wired in `app/routes.tsx` from TASK-019); the layout supplies the centered "Trackify" wordmark and the page itself focuses on the form. Inline styles use design tokens only (`--color-surface`, `--color-border`, `--color-accent`, `--color-danger-soft`, etc.) — no Tailwind, no styled-components, per CLAUDE.md.
- Submit button is disabled while either field is empty or while `submitting === true`. Inputs are disabled during submit so a user cannot retype mid-flight. Error region uses `role="alert"` so screen readers announce it. Inputs use proper `autoComplete` values (`username` / `current-password`) so password managers fill correctly. The form has `noValidate` because the validation logic is already in JS — letting the browser's native validation fire would double-prompt and conflict with the inline error region.

What was tested:
- `npm run build` from `frontend/`: clean. `tsc -b && vite build` succeeded — 88 modules transformed, ~76ms. TypeScript strict-mode types check across the new file.
- `npm run lint` from `frontend/`: 2 pre-existing errors in `src/app/routes.tsx` (HMR `react-refresh/only-export-components` rule, documented in TASK-030's progress note). Zero errors in `SignInPage.tsx` or any file I touched.
- Dev server smoke test: `npm run dev` booted on `:5173` cleanly. `curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" http://localhost:5173/sign-in` returned `200`. The SPA HTML shell loads (router and `AuthProvider` boot without errors). End-to-end POST to `/api/auth/login` was *not* exercised in this test step — it requires the backend running plus a `VITE_API_BASE_URL` or Vite proxy (see Follow-up risk #2). The TASK-031 acceptance criterion is "renders a minimal branded sign-in screen with login and password inputs that submit to the backend login endpoint", which is satisfied at the contract level: the form's `onSubmit` calls `useAuth().signIn`, which calls `authApi.login` (TASK-030), which posts to `/auth/login` via `httpClient` (TASK-020) with `credentials: 'include'`.
- The user prompt asked for `./gradlew -p backend ...`. This is a frontend-only task, no backend changes; backend build is unaffected. Project is Maven (`./mvnw`), not Gradle, per CLAUDE.md.

Design choices and trade-offs:
- **Local `useState` for form fields, not Zustand or React Hook Form.** Form drafts are the textbook case for `useState` (architecture.md §6.4). Adding React Hook Form for one form with two fields would dwarf the form code in dependencies and indirection. If sign-in evolves into multi-step (forgot-password, MFA), revisit then.
- **Controlled inputs over uncontrolled + `FormData`.** `FormData` is tempting for a 2-field form, but controlled state lets the submit button reflect "both fields present" without an extra ref. The disabled-while-submitting behaviour also requires controlled state to actually disable inputs reliably.
- **`navigate('/', { replace: true })` on success, not `push`.** A `push` would put `/sign-in` in the history stack; the back button would return the now-authenticated user to the sign-in page (where TASK-032's redirect would bounce them right back to `/`, which is correct but wasteful). `replace` keeps the history clean.
- **Branching on `error.code === 'INVALID_CREDENTIALS'`** rather than `error.status === 401`. Both are technically the same signal today (backend returns 401 with `code: INVALID_CREDENTIALS`). Branching on the *code* is the more durable contract: if the backend later distinguishes "account locked" from "wrong password" via a different code at the same status, this page can branch on the code without re-checking 401. It also gracefully falls through to `error.message` for any other 401 case.
- **No "Remember me" / "Forgot password" links.** The MVP scope (`tasks.json`) explicitly excludes email invites and notifications. Password reset is not in scope, and "remember me" with a session cookie is a duration knob, not a UX one. Adding placeholder links creates dead UI.
- **No CSRF token in the body.** `SecurityConfig.csrf.disable()` is global today (TASK-024). When/if CSRF is re-enabled (`TASK-077`-adjacent or later), the login endpoint will need a token; this page can read it from `httpClient` at that point.
- **Trim only the login, never the password.** Trailing whitespace in passwords can be intentional. Trimming the login mirrors how Spring Security typically lookups by `username` (case- and whitespace-sensitive on our side; the server is the authority either way).
- **`autoFocus` on the login field.** Sign-in is single-purpose; the user is here to type a login. Skipping the focus saves a keystroke. (`autoFocus` is generally an a11y antipattern when the focus target is not the primary action — here it is.)
- **No "Sign up" affordance.** Local registration goes through `UserRegistrationService` (TASK-026) but there is no public registration endpoint exposed; the backend currently boots an admin via `AUTH_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_*`. A self-serve registration page is out of scope for this task and arguably for the MVP — defer to a future task.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- **TASK-032 (protected route behavior)** is the natural next step. Today, an already-authenticated user who navigates to `/sign-in` sees the form and could re-submit credentials; on success they redirect to `/`. That's harmless but ugly. TASK-032 will gate `/sign-in` against `useAuth().status === 'authenticated'` (redirect to `/`) and gate the AppShell routes against `'unauthenticated'` (redirect to `/sign-in`). I deliberately did NOT add either guard here — that scope belongs to TASK-032.
- **`VITE_API_BASE_URL` / Vite proxy is still not wired.** TASK-030's note already flagged this. With `httpClient` defaulting to `/api`, a dev-server-only test will hit `http://localhost:5173/api/auth/login` and 404. To actually submit credentials end-to-end in dev, either set `VITE_API_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8080/api` in `.env` or add a Vite dev-server proxy. Cross-origin cookie auth is already correct (`credentials: 'include'` + backend CORS allowing the frontend origin from TASK-010). Not blocking TASK-031 since the acceptance criterion is "submits to the backend login endpoint" which the form does — the dev infrastructure for actually receiving the request is an environment concern, not a UI concern.
- **`SERVER_SERVLET_SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE=true` in local dev** is still a footgun (documented in TASK-027/030 notes). Until `secure=false` is set in local `.env`, no cookie persists from a plain-HTTP login response, so `signIn` will succeed but the subsequent `/api/me` will return 401 and `AuthProvider` will report unauthenticated — the form will appear to "do nothing." Re-flagging here so the next test pass remembers to check `.env` first.
- **No loading "spinner" — just text swap.** Submit button text flips to "Signing in…". Adding a spinner SVG/component would create the only loading indicator in the codebase today; defer until there are at least two places that need one (TASK-078 covers loading states project-wide).
- **No backend changes**: pure frontend. No migrations, no controllers, no DTOs touched.
- **Next pending task: TASK-032** (protected route behavior).

================================================================================
TASK-032: add protected route behavior
Date: 2026-04-26
================================================================================

What changed:
- `frontend/src/app/guards/RouteGuards.tsx`: new file with two route-guard components — `RequireAuth` and `RequireAnonymous` — plus a private `AuthLoadingShell`. Both guards consume `useAuth()` from `modules/auth/hooks/useAuth` (TASK-030) and rely solely on `status` (`'loading' | 'authenticated' | 'unauthenticated'`).
  - `RequireAuth`: while `loading`, renders `AuthLoadingShell`; while `unauthenticated`, returns `<Navigate to="/sign-in" replace state={{ from: location.pathname }} />`; while `authenticated`, renders children. The `state.from` is included so a future task (TASK-031 follow-up or beyond) can post-login bounce the user back to where they came from. SignInPage does not read it today, which is intentional — adding the read is a separate scope.
  - `RequireAnonymous`: while `loading`, renders `AuthLoadingShell`; while `authenticated`, returns `<Navigate to="/" replace />`; while `unauthenticated`, renders children. This stops an already-signed-in user from seeing the sign-in form (and the duplicate-submit it implies).
  - `AuthLoadingShell`: minimal centered "Loading…" using the existing pastel tokens (`--color-bg`, `--color-text-subtle`, `--font-size-sm`). Not exported. Re-used by both guards so the brief initial `/api/me` round-trip looks identical regardless of which side of the auth boundary the user lands on.
- `frontend/src/app/routes.tsx`: `AuthLayoutRoute` is now wrapped in `<RequireAnonymous>` and `AppShellRoute` in `<RequireAuth>`. The router topology is otherwise unchanged — same paths, same page components, same `Outlet`-based layout composition. The wrapping is the sole behavioural change.

What was tested:
- `npm run build` from `frontend/`: clean. `tsc -b && vite build` succeeded — 89 modules transformed (vs. 87/88 before, accounting for the new guard module). TypeScript strict-mode checks pass for the new file. No new errors.
- `npm run lint` from `frontend/`: same 2 pre-existing errors in `src/app/routes.tsx` (HMR `react-refresh/only-export-components` rule on `AuthLayoutRoute`/`AppShellRoute` — these were already flagged in TASK-030 and TASK-031 progress notes; they are not regressions and not introduced by this task). Zero new errors. `RouteGuards.tsx` lints clean: it exports two components only, no helper exports.
- Dev server probe: `npm run dev` boots cleanly on `:5173`. `curl` against `/`, `/projects/abc/board`, and `/sign-in` all return 200 — expected for an SPA, since the server returns the same HTML shell for every path and the redirect happens client-side via React Router's `<Navigate>`. The TASK-032 acceptance test is *manual browser verification*; I cannot drive a real browser interactively, but the redirect logic is small enough to be verified by inspection: when `useAuth().status === 'unauthenticated'`, `RequireAuth` returns `<Navigate to="/sign-in" replace />`, which React Router translates to a client-side route change. The component's render is the only side-effect path, so there is no "stuck on the protected route" failure mode short of `useAuth()` itself being broken (covered by TASK-030's tests).
- The user prompt asked for `./gradlew -p backend ...`. This is a frontend-only task with no backend changes; backend build is unaffected. Project is Maven (`./mvnw`), not Gradle (per CLAUDE.md), and TASK-032's start condition + acceptance criteria are entirely SPA-scoped.

Design choices and trade-offs:
- **Two named guards instead of one with a `mode` prop.** A single `<AuthGate mode="require-auth" />` component would centralize the `useAuth` call but would also require every consumer to remember the mode prop and the type-narrow it implies. Two named components read better in `routes.tsx` (`<RequireAuth>` is self-documenting at the call site) and lets each guard ignore the dimension the other cares about. The cost is one extra component definition; the benefit is no boolean prop and no conditional return chains inside one body.
- **Single file `RouteGuards.tsx` instead of one file per guard.** The provider pattern in this codebase (`AuthProvider.tsx`, `QueryProvider.tsx`, `ThemeProvider.tsx`) is one-component-per-file. For these guards I bundled both into one file because they share the private `AuthLoadingShell` helper and have nearly identical control-flow shapes — splitting would either duplicate the loading shell or require a third file just to host it. ESLint's `react-refresh/only-export-components` rule is satisfied (both exports are components, no helper export).
- **Wrap layouts, not pages.** Two practical placements: (a) wrap each *page* component in a guard, or (b) wrap the *layout route* element in a guard. (b) is one wrapper per layout vs. one per page; it cannot drift when new pages are added; and `AuthLayoutRoute` / `AppShellRoute` are already the natural boundary between "anonymous-allowed" and "auth-required" UI. Putting the guard on the layout means every protected page automatically inherits gating without remembering to import the guard at each page. The cost is that both layouts now include a `useAuth` call — fine, since the context lookup is O(1) and the layouts already render unconditionally on every authenticated render.
- **`replace` on both `<Navigate>` calls.** A `push` would put the protected URL into the history and a back-button would loop the user through the redirect again. `replace` is the standard React Router pattern for redirects-as-policy. SignInPage already uses `navigate('/', { replace: true })` on success (TASK-031), so the back button cannot revisit `/sign-in` after a successful login either — the history stays clean from both directions.
- **Loading shell, not redirect-during-loading.** While the initial `/api/me` round-trip is in flight, `useAuth().status === 'loading'`. If `RequireAuth` redirected on `'loading'`, a freshly-loaded authenticated user would briefly see `/sign-in` while their session restored — a flicker that's both ugly and slightly misleading (the user is *not* signed out, just waiting for the bootstrap probe). The neutral loading shell is the correct intermediate state. Inverse argument applies to `RequireAnonymous`: redirecting while loading would briefly send an unauthenticated user from `/sign-in` to `/`, which would then bounce them back to `/sign-in`. Two redirects per pageload to land on the same place is a pure UX regression. The loading shell short-circuits both flicker patterns.
- **`state={{ from: location.pathname }}` even though SignInPage ignores it today.** Cheap to include now and useful later. If TASK-031's SignInPage starts reading it (e.g., to redirect a user who tried `/projects/abc/board` while signed out back to that URL after login), no change is needed in `RequireAuth`. If we ever decide we don't want post-login bounce-back, we can remove the prop without affecting protection. Including it costs three tokens and zero behaviour.
- **No "remember intended URL across signOut" handling.** `RequireAuth` only forwards `from` on the *first* unauthenticated visit. If the user logs out and lands on `/sign-in`, there's no "from" — they get the dashboard on next login. This is fine and is the typical signed-out experience; there's no acceptance criterion calling for anything else.
- **No use of `<Navigate>` element on the route definition.** React Router's data-router lets you embed `<Navigate>` as the route element, e.g. `{ path: '/sign-in', element: status === 'authed' ? <Navigate to="/" /> : <SignInPage /> }`. That requires reading auth state at the router-creation site, which would tie the `router` const to a hook context that doesn't exist outside React render. Wrapping inside the route element via guard components is the canonical React Router 7 pattern for auth-conditional routing.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- **HMR-rule lint warnings on `routes.tsx` are still pre-existing.** The fix requires extracting `AuthLayoutRoute` and `AppShellRoute` into their own file (or moving `router` to a separate file). Either is a refactor outside TASK-032's scope; flagging here in case a hardening pass picks it up alongside TASK-077-style cleanups.
- **`SERVER_SERVLET_SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE=true` in dev** still affects this task transitively — without an HTTPS dev origin, the cookie won't persist after `signIn`, so `/api/me` will 401, `useAuth().status` will resolve to `'unauthenticated'`, and the user will be redirected back to `/sign-in` immediately after a "successful" login. Not a guard bug; a backend env footgun documented in TASK-027/030 progress notes. Re-flagging because the redirect now makes the symptom highly visible (loop between `/sign-in` and `/sign-in`).
- **`VITE_API_BASE_URL` / Vite proxy** also still not wired (TASK-030/031 note). Without it, `httpClient` calls `/api/...` against the Vite dev origin and gets 404s. With this task in place, those 404s manifest as `useAuth().status === 'unauthenticated'`, which routes the user to `/sign-in` — the same UX as a true unauthenticated session, hiding the underlying network configuration problem. Set `VITE_API_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8080/api` in `.env` for local dev to actually reach the backend.
- **No backend changes.** Pure frontend wiring against the contract `useAuth` (TASK-030) already exposes. No migrations, no controllers, no DTOs.
- **TASK-033 (workspace schema and persistence)** is the next pending task. It's a pure backend task and unrelated to the route gating shipped here. The next user-visible signed-in surface (workspace switcher, dashboard project list) builds on TASK-033 onwards; the protected-route plumbing is now in place to host them safely.


================================================================================
TASK-033: add workspace schema and persistence
Date: 2026-04-26
================================================================================

What changed:
- `backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V4__workspaces.sql`: new Flyway migration creating two tables.
  - `workspaces`: `id UUID PK (default gen_random_uuid())`, `name TEXT NOT NULL`, `slug TEXT NOT NULL` (unique via `workspaces_slug_unique`), `owner_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE RESTRICT`, `created_at`/`updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()`. RESTRICT on the owner FK is deliberate: deleting a user who owns a workspace requires explicit transfer first; CASCADE here would silently drop the workspace and all downstream projects/tasks, which is far too destructive for a schema-level decision.
  - `workspace_members`: `id UUID PK`, `workspace_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES workspaces(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`, `user_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`, `role TEXT NOT NULL` with `CHECK (role IN ('OWNER','ADMIN','MEMBER','VIEWER'))`, `created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()`. Unique constraint on `(workspace_id, user_id)` (one membership row per user per workspace). Index `workspace_members_user_id_idx` on `user_id` to power the upcoming "list workspaces for current user" query (TASK-035).
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/workspace/domain/Workspace.java`: `@Entity @Table(name = "workspaces")`. UUID PK via `GenerationType.UUID`. `ownerId` is stored as a plain `UUID` field, not a `@ManyToOne User`, to keep the workspace module decoupled from the `user` package's JPA graph (architecture.md section 7). Mirrors `User.java`'s `@PrePersist`/`@PreUpdate` timestamp pattern. Public constructor `(name, slug, ownerId)` plus protected no-arg. Setters only for `name`/`slug`; `id`, `ownerId`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` are getter-only from the domain perspective.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/workspace/domain/WorkspaceMember.java`: `@Entity @Table(name = "workspace_members")`. Same opaque-FK pattern (`workspaceId`, `userId` as `UUID`). `role` is `@Enumerated(EnumType.STRING) WorkspaceRole`. `@PrePersist` only; membership rows are append-only at the schema level (no `@PreUpdate`). Public constructor `(workspaceId, userId, role)` plus protected no-arg.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/workspace/domain/WorkspaceRole.java`: plain enum with `OWNER`, `ADMIN`, `MEMBER`, `VIEWER`. No methods; the role taxonomy lives here so future tasks (TASK-076 authz checks) can extend or read it without touching the migration.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/workspace/infrastructure/WorkspaceRepository.java`: `JpaRepository<Workspace, UUID>` plus `findBySlug(String)` plus `existsBySlug(String)`. Slug methods are needed by TASK-034's auto-creation flow (slug uniqueness check before insert).
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/workspace/infrastructure/WorkspaceMemberRepository.java`: `JpaRepository<WorkspaceMember, UUID>` plus `findAllByUserId(UUID)` (TASK-035 list-workspaces backing query) plus `existsByWorkspaceIdAndUserId(UUID, UUID)` (TASK-076 boolean membership gate). No speculative methods beyond what the next 1-2 downstream tasks need.

What was tested:
- `./mvnw compile` from `backend/`: BUILD SUCCESS. Java 21 compile of the new entities and repos clean. The task's stated `test_steps` is exactly this command.
- `./mvnw test` from `backend/`: BUILD SUCCESS, 17 tests run, 0 failures, 0 errors. The Spring Boot test slices boot the full context against a real Postgres datasource and Flyway runs all four migrations including V4 on context startup; `ddl-auto: validate` then confirms each entity column maps to a real DB column with matching name/type/nullability. If any of the V4 column names had drifted from the Workspace/WorkspaceMember entities, every `@SpringBootTest` would have failed during context init; they all passed, so the entity-to-DDL mapping is correct.
- Code-reviewer agent verdict: pass. One informational note for a future task (see follow-up risk #1 below).
- The user's prompt asked to verify with `./mvnw -f backend compile test-compile` and `./mvnw -f backend test`. The backend agent ran the equivalent (`cd backend && ./mvnw compile test-compile`, `cd backend && ./mvnw test`), and I re-ran `./mvnw -q compile` and `./mvnw test` from `backend/` independently to confirm. Both green.

Design choices and trade-offs:
- **Opaque UUID FKs instead of `@ManyToOne` associations.** The textbook JPA approach would be `@ManyToOne private User owner;` and `@ManyToOne private Workspace workspace;` on the member row. I chose plain `UUID ownerId`/`workspaceId`/`userId` deliberately for two reasons. (1) Architecture.md section 7 calls for a modular monolith with crisp module boundaries; if `Workspace` references `User` directly, the `workspace` package now compiles only when `user.domain.User` is on the classpath, which leaks the user module's internal entity into a peer module. (2) It keeps the JPA graph shallow: no eager-load decisions to make, no LazyInitializationException risk, no fetch-join surprises in later board/timeline read paths. The cost is that joining workspace plus owner-display-name requires an explicit JPQL or projection, but every downstream task that needs that join (TASK-035, TASK-042) will assemble its own DTO anyway.
- **`OWNER`/`ADMIN`/`MEMBER`/`VIEWER` role taxonomy.** Architecture.md section 9 mentions "role-based actions like project admin, editor, viewer" in the *project* context, not the workspace context. For workspaces the doc only says "membership and roles". I picked a four-rank taxonomy to leave room for the natural distinctions: `OWNER` is the workspace creator (matched 1:1 with `workspaces.owner_id`), `ADMIN` can manage projects and members, `MEMBER` is the default seat, `VIEWER` is read-only. The CHECK constraint can be loosened in a later migration if more roles are needed; can't easily be tightened later, so erring narrow. TASK-034 will create the personal workspace with `OWNER` for its single member.
- **`ON DELETE RESTRICT` on `workspaces.owner_id`, `ON DELETE CASCADE` on `workspace_members`.** The asymmetry is intentional. A workspace member row going away when its user is deleted is correct (the membership is meaningless without the user). A whole workspace going away when its owner is deleted is *not* correct: that workspace probably has projects and tasks belonging to other members. RESTRICT forces the application layer to handle owner deletion explicitly (transfer ownership or delete workspace first). User deletion isn't in MVP scope anyway, but the schema baseline is the right place to encode "destructive cascade only where it is unambiguously safe."
- **Slug column on `workspaces` even though no API exposes it yet.** Slug is the URL-safe handle (`/workspaces/personal-mikhail/...` style). The architecture doesn't mandate slugs in section 10, but the dashboard URL flow in section 8 implicitly assumes some human-readable workspace identifier exists. Adding the column plus unique index now is cheap; backfilling an `ALTER TABLE` migration later for every existing workspace would be more work and would force a dual-read/dual-write window. TASK-034 will populate it with a derived slug (e.g., from the user's login).
- **No `WorkspaceMember` `@PreUpdate`.** Membership rows are append-only at this stage; role changes will be handled later (or by deleting plus reinserting). Skipping the `@PreUpdate` makes the no-`updated_at` schema column intentional rather than accidental.
- **Repositories expose only what TASK-034/035/076 need.** I resisted adding `findAllByWorkspaceId`, `countByWorkspaceId`, `findByOwnerId`, etc. The task scope is "support workspaces and workspace_members"; adding speculative finders now would be premature abstraction (CLAUDE.md cross-cutting rules: "no premature abstractions"). When TASK-034 lands and needs `findByOwnerIdAndUserId` or whatever, it can add the method then with a real call site.
- **Index only on `workspace_members.user_id`, not on `workspace_id`.** Postgres's unique constraint on `(workspace_id, user_id)` already creates a btree index that is leftmost-prefixed by `workspace_id`, so any `WHERE workspace_id = ?` query (e.g., "list members of this workspace") can use it. A separate `workspace_id` index would be redundant. The standalone `user_id` index is needed because user_id is the *second* column of the unique index and therefore not indexed-leftmost on its own.
- **No tests added.** The task's `test_steps` is `./mvnw compile`. The codebase pattern has been to add tests where the task asks for them (TASK-026, TASK-027, TASK-028 all explicitly request unit/MockMvc tests; TASK-022, entity plus repository, doesn't and didn't get a test). Following the established pattern: TASK-033 doesn't ask for a test; the next workspace task that adds *behavior* (TASK-034) will exercise both repositories through its application service.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- **TASK-076 will need `findByWorkspaceIdAndUserId` returning `Optional<WorkspaceMember>`, not just the boolean.** Code-reviewer flagged this. The current `existsByWorkspaceIdAndUserId` answers "is this user a member?" but not "what role do they have?". Role-based authorization (project admin vs viewer) needs the role itself. I deliberately left this off; adding it now without a call site is exactly the speculative-finder problem. The TASK-076 implementer should add it with their first usage.
- **No backend-bootstrap workspace yet.** A user can sign in (TASK-027) but ends up with zero workspaces. TASK-034 fixes this by auto-creating a personal workspace on first successful login. Until that lands, `MeService` returns the user but `/api/workspaces` (TASK-035) would return `[]`. This is expected and correct per the task ordering: TASK-033 is the schema layer, TASK-034 the seeding logic, TASK-035 the read endpoint.
- **JPA `ddl-auto: validate` is the safety net here, not Flyway alone.** Flyway will happily apply V4 even if the entity columns don't match (Flyway is schema-only, not validation). The validation comes from Hibernate's startup check; every `@SpringBootTest` exercises this implicitly. If anyone later adds a column to `Workspace.java` without writing a `Vn__add_column.sql`, the next test run will fail with "missing column" and force the migration. This is the intended workflow per CLAUDE.md ("Never edit a committed Flyway migration — write a new `Vn__...sql`").
