- **No frontend work in this task.** The `modules/workspace` frontend module is empty until TASK-036. The dashboard still shows placeholders (TASK-019).
- **Next pending task: TASK-034** (create personal workspace on first login). Will use both `WorkspaceRepository` and `WorkspaceMemberRepository` from this task and hook into the existing login success path from TASK-027.

================================================================================
TASK-034: create a personal workspace on first login
Date: 2026-04-26
================================================================================

What changed:
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/workspace/application/WorkspaceBootstrapService.java`: new `@Service`. Single public method `ensurePersonalWorkspace(UUID userId, String login)` annotated `@Transactional`. Idempotency rule: queries `workspaceMemberRepository.findAllByUserId(userId)` first; if any membership exists for the user, returns immediately with no writes. Otherwise creates one `Workspace` (name `"{login}'s workspace"`, slug derived from login, owner = userId) and one `WorkspaceMember` row with `WorkspaceRole.OWNER` linking that workspace to the user. Deliberately uses membership-presence as the idempotency signal — not a one-shot flag column on `users` — so the contract is "ensure the user has a workspace," which correctly handles re-entry from any login event.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/auth/controller/LoginController.java`: injected `WorkspaceBootstrapService` via the constructor; after `securityContextRepository.saveContext(...)` succeeds, calls `workspaceBootstrapService.ensurePersonalWorkspace(principal.userId(), principal.username())`. Hook is at the controller composition point — same site that already orchestrates `authenticationManager.authenticate` and the manual `SecurityContext` persistence — keeping all post-auth side effects in one explicit call sequence.
- `backend/src/test/java/com/trackify/workspace/application/WorkspaceBootstrapServiceTest.java`: new `@ExtendWith(MockitoExtension.class)` unit test, 4 test methods.
  - `firstLoginCreatesExactlyOneWorkspaceAndOneOwnerMembership` — empty `findAllByUserId` result, asserts exactly one `Workspace` saved with the expected name/slug/ownerId and exactly one `WorkspaceMember` saved with `OWNER` role linked to the new workspace's id.
  - `secondLoginDoesNotCreateDuplicateWorkspaceOrMembership` — `findAllByUserId` returns one existing membership, asserts `verify(..., never()).save(any())` on both repositories and that `existsBySlug` is never even consulted (early-return path).
  - `slugCollisionAppendsShortFragment` — `existsBySlug("bob") == true`, asserts the saved slug starts with `"bob-"` and is longer than the base. The fragment itself is non-deterministic (UUID-derived) so the assertion shape is "starts-with + length-greater-than" rather than equality.
  - `toKebabCaseConvertsLoginCorrectly` — direct unit test of the package-private static helper across whitespace, dot, underscore, uppercase, and trim cases.
- `backend/src/test/java/com/trackify/auth/controller/LoginControllerTest.java`: added `@MockitoBean private WorkspaceBootstrapService workspaceBootstrapService;`. The three pre-existing TASK-027 test methods continue to pass — the mock returns void from `ensurePersonalWorkspace`, so the login flow is unchanged from their perspective.

What was tested:
- `./mvnw -q compile test-compile` from `backend/`: clean.
- `./mvnw test` from `backend/`: BUILD SUCCESS, **21 tests run, 0 failures, 0 errors** (up from 17 tests before TASK-034 — 4 new tests in `WorkspaceBootstrapServiceTest`). The 3 pre-existing `LoginControllerTest` cases are unchanged by behaviour and still pass; the new `@MockitoBean` injection doesn't alter their assertions.
- The task's stated `test_steps` is "Add a backend test proving first login creates the workspace and second login does not create a duplicate." The first two test methods in `WorkspaceBootstrapServiceTest` cover exactly this. They are mock-based service tests, not full integration tests against a live Postgres — that's a deliberate trade-off (see Design choices below). The contract proven is the same: if `findAllByUserId` is empty, exactly one workspace and one OWNER membership are persisted; if it returns >= 1 row, no writes occur.

Design choices and trade-offs:
- **Hook in `LoginController`, not via `ApplicationListener<InteractiveAuthenticationSuccessEvent>` or a custom `AuthenticationSuccessHandler`.** Three reasons. (1) The login flow in this project is *manual* — `LoginController` already explicitly calls `authenticationManager.authenticate` and `securityContextRepository.saveContext`, so post-auth side effects naturally compose at the controller level rather than at a Spring Security extension point. (2) An event listener would decouple the bootstrap call from the request transaction in a way that buys nothing — there's no async dispatch, no other listeners, no observability layer hanging off auth events. (3) Test ergonomics: a controller-level call is trivially mockable in `LoginControllerTest` (a single `@MockitoBean` line); an `ApplicationListener` would force every login test to either spin up the listener bean or globally suppress it. The `LoginController` site is the canonical "after authenticate, before respond" hook in this codebase and that's where this belongs.
- **Idempotency by membership state, not by a `users.bootstrap_done` flag.** A flag column would require a fifth Flyway migration and would couple "has a personal workspace" to a denormalized boolean that could drift from reality (admin deletes a workspace -> flag still says "done" -> user logs in again with no workspace). Querying `workspace_members.user_id` is one indexed lookup (the `workspace_members_user_id_idx` index from V4) and answers the actual question — "does this user already participate in any workspace?" — directly. Future workspace-management UI (delete workspace, leave workspace) automatically composes correctly: if the user ends up with zero memberships, their next login re-bootstraps. That matches the user-visible promise of the system better than a sticky flag.
- **Idempotency on *any* membership, not specifically a "personal" one.** A more conservative implementation would check "does the user own a workspace whose slug equals their login?" — only skipping bootstrap when the *personal* workspace exists. I rejected that: once email invites land (out of MVP scope) a user might receive a workspace invitation before ever creating one of their own, and we don't want to force-create a "personal" workspace on top of an already-functional invited one just because the slug doesn't match. The acceptance criterion is "guarantees exactly one personal workspace and one owner membership" *for the first-login case* — and the start condition is "a signed-in user may exist without any workspace." Both are satisfied by membership-presence idempotency.
- **Slug derivation: `toKebabCase(login)` -> on collision, append a 4-char hex fragment from `UUID.randomUUID()`.** Two pragmatic choices baked in. (1) One retry, not N retries: if the suffixed slug is *also* taken (1-in-65k odds for a single-user collision, much less for repeat collisions), the database unique constraint will surface a 5xx and the user can retry login. The expected MVP user count is small enough that bounded retry is overkill; the unique constraint is the safety net and Hibernate will throw a `DataIntegrityViolationException` mapped through `GlobalExceptionHandler` (TASK-011). (2) Hex fragment over numeric suffix (`-1`, `-2`, ...): a numeric suffix would require a `findByOwnerIdOrderBySlugDesc`-style query plus a counter, while hex is collision-resistant in one shot. The aesthetic cost (`alice-7f3a` vs `alice-1`) is acceptable for what is currently an internal slug.
- **Workspace name `"{login}'s workspace"` instead of `"Personal"`.** A literal "Personal" name would be ambiguous when a user later joins multiple workspaces (which "Personal"?). Including the login in the default name makes the workspace switcher (TASK-037) self-explanatory at a glance. The name is mutable later (workspaces will get rename UI in scope or post-MVP) so this is just a sensible default, not a hard contract.
- **No new migration.** The V4 schema from TASK-033 already has every column needed: `workspaces.slug` (unique), `workspaces.owner_id`, `workspace_members.role` (CHECK includes OWNER). Adding a `bootstrapped_at` column on `users` was tempting (audit purposes) but it's pure speculation right now and would force `User` entity changes for no observable benefit. CLAUDE.md cross-cutting rule: no premature abstractions, no speculative columns.
- **Mock-based unit test for the service, not `@DataJpaTest` against a real H2/Postgres.** The behavioural contract being verified is "given empty `findAllByUserId`, save exactly one Workspace and one WorkspaceMember" and "given non-empty, save nothing." That contract is fully expressed at the repository-call boundary; a `@DataJpaTest` would re-test that JpaRepository's `save()` actually inserts a row, which is a Spring/Hibernate concern, not a TASK-034 concern. The `LoginControllerTest` already sits behind `@WebMvcTest` with the full Spring Security filter chain wired up, and the existing `@SpringBootTest` end-to-end test surface implicitly exercises Flyway + JPA validate against a real Postgres on every test run, so the persistence layer is not under-tested.
- **`LoginControllerTest` updated minimally — `@MockitoBean` only.** The bootstrap call is invoked-and-discarded from the controller's POV (return type is `void`, no thrown checked exceptions), so the tests don't need to assert anything about it; verifying the existing assertions (200, session, principal identity, 401, no session) still hold under the new wiring is sufficient to prove the bootstrap call doesn't break the login contract.
- **Static `toKebabCase` package-private, not private.** It is small enough to be unit-testable independently and the test exercises it directly. Marking it package-private lets the test live in the same package without using reflection.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- **Slug collision retry is 1-deep.** If the `{login}-{4hex}` slug happens to *also* collide (vanishingly rare in practice), the underlying `UNIQUE` constraint on `workspaces.slug` will surface as a `DataIntegrityViolationException` and the login response will be a 500. Future hardening: bump the fragment length, add a bounded retry loop, or switch to a different uniqueness strategy. Not blocking for MVP — the current user volume makes this an academic risk.
- **No transaction-scope check beyond `@Transactional`.** The `ensurePersonalWorkspace` call runs inside the controller's request thread, after the security context has already been persisted to the session. If the bootstrap transaction throws (e.g., a transient DB failure), the user has *already* received an authenticated session cookie but no workspace was created. Their next login will re-attempt the bootstrap (idempotency carries us through), but the *current* request will return 500 instead of the login response. A more defensive implementation would wrap the bootstrap call in a try/catch and log + continue. I left it strict on purpose: surfacing the failure is a better-than-silently-broken default for the MVP, and idempotency means the worst case is "user sees one login fail, retries, gets in."
- **`LoginController` is not `@Transactional`.** The `@Transactional` is only on `WorkspaceBootstrapService.ensurePersonalWorkspace`. The `findAllByUserId` + `existsBySlug` + 2x `save` form the boundary; auth + session writes are outside that boundary. This matches the project pattern of "transactions live in `application/`, not `controller/`" (CLAUDE.md modular monolith section).
- **TASK-035 read-side note** (passed up from the backend agent): `WorkspaceMemberRepository.findAllByUserId(userId)` returns `WorkspaceMember` rows whose only workspace identifier is the FK UUID. The `GET /api/workspaces` endpoint will need to fetch the corresponding `Workspace` entities (`workspaceRepository.findAllById(memberWorkspaceIds)`) to assemble names/slugs into a DTO. Either two repository calls plus an in-memory join, or a JPQL projection with `WorkspaceMember m JOIN Workspace w ON m.workspaceId = w.id WHERE m.userId = :userId`. No schema change needed.
- **TASK-076 (authorization checks) will add `findByWorkspaceIdAndUserId`** returning the membership with its role — same note as TASK-033's progress already flagged. Bootstrap doesn't need it (creation always uses `OWNER`), but every workspace-scoped read endpoint will.
- **No frontend work.** The `modules/workspace` frontend module is still empty; TASK-036 is the next frontend touchpoint and TASK-035 must land first to give it a real endpoint to call.
- **Next pending task: TASK-035** (`GET /api/workspaces`). Will consume `WorkspaceMemberRepository.findAllByUserId` directly and is the natural complement to TASK-034's writes — together they form the read-write loop for workspace list state.


================================================================================
TASK-035 — Implement GET /api/workspaces
Date: 2026-04-27
================================================================================

What changed:
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/workspace/dto/WorkspaceResponse.java` (new): record with `id`, `name`, `slug`. Minimal — only fields the workspace-selector UI needs. `ownerId`/timestamps intentionally excluded (no premature surface area).
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/workspace/application/WorkspaceQueryService.java` (new): `@Service` with one `@Transactional(readOnly = true)` method `listForUser(UUID userId)`. Two-step lookup: `findAllByUserId` → collect workspace IDs → `findAllById` → sort by name → map to `WorkspaceResponse`. Chose two-step over a JPQL join because both repositories are in the same module and the in-memory join is trivially correct for the expected cardinality (1–10 workspaces per user). A JPQL join would require either a `@ManyToOne` JPA association (breaks the module boundary) or a native/JPQL cross-entity query on unrelated root entities (verbose, fragile). No new repository method added — `findAllById` is from `JpaRepository`.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/workspace/controller/WorkspaceController.java` (new): thin `@RestController` at `/api/workspaces` with one `@GetMapping`. Extracts `principal.userId()` from `@AuthenticationPrincipal LocalUserPrincipal`, delegates to `WorkspaceQueryService.listForUser`. No business logic. No `@Transactional`. Security covered by the existing catch-all `.requestMatchers("/api/**").authenticated()` in `SecurityConfig` — no security chain change needed or made.
- `backend/src/test/java/com/trackify/workspace/controller/WorkspaceControllerTest.java` (new): `@WebMvcTest` slice over `WorkspaceController`, same `@Import` pattern as `MeControllerTest`. Four test methods:
  1. `authenticatedUserReceivesTheirOwnWorkspaces` — stubs `listForUser(aliceId)` to return 2 workspaces, asserts HTTP 200 and exact JSON shape (id, name, slug for each element, length == 2).
  2. `otherUsersWorkspacesAreNotVisible` — stubs both `listForUser(aliceId)` and `listForUser(bobId)` to different lists; Alice's request returns only Alice's workspace, proving isolation at the service call boundary.
  3. `userWithNoMembershipsReceivesEmptyList` — stubs `listForUser` to return `List.of()`, asserts HTTP 200 with `length == 0`.
  4. `unauthenticatedRequestIsRejectedWith401BeforeControllerRuns` — no auth context, Spring Security intercepts before controller runs, asserts HTTP 401.
- Marked TASK-035 status = done in tasks.json.

What was tested:
- `./mvnw compile test-compile` from `backend/` → BUILD SUCCESS, 72 source files compiled (was 69 + 3 new), 10 test-compile files.
- `./mvnw test` from `backend/` → Tests run: 25, Failures: 0, Errors: 0, Skipped: 0, BUILD SUCCESS. Was 21 tests before — 4 new test methods added, all green.

Design choices:
- Two-step lookup (not JPQL join): see `WorkspaceQueryService` note above. Simpler, correct, no cross-module coupling.
- Sorting by name alphabetically in the service: consistent ordering without depending on DB insertion order. Stable for the UI's workspace switcher.
- Principal resolved via `@AuthenticationPrincipal LocalUserPrincipal` — same pattern as `MeController`. No extra resolver, no reflection.
- Security chain NOT modified. `/api/workspaces` falls under the existing `/api/**` authenticated matcher.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- The `WorkspaceResponse` DTO does not expose the user's role in that workspace. TASK-076 (authz checks) will need role information; it should add a separate query path rather than expanding `WorkspaceResponse` (keep the list response lean, add a detail/membership endpoint later).
- Next pending task: TASK-036 (frontend workspace query and types). TASK-035 provides the endpoint TASK-036 will call.


================================================================================
TASK-036 — Add frontend workspace query and types
Date: 2026-04-27
================================================================================

What changed:
- `frontend/src/modules/workspace/types/workspace.ts` (new): `Workspace` interface mirroring the backend `WorkspaceResponse` DTO from TASK-035 — `id`, `name`, `slug`, all strings (`id` is a UUID string on the wire, no need to model it as anything richer for the UI). No `ownerId` or timestamps; the backend deliberately doesn't ship those, and the UI hooks shouldn't model fields it doesn't receive.
- `frontend/src/modules/workspace/api/workspaceApi.ts` (new): `workspaceApi` object with one method, `list(): Promise<Workspace[]>`, calling `httpClient.get<Workspace[]>('/workspaces')`. Mirrors the existing `authApi` pattern (`frontend/src/modules/auth/api/authApi.ts`). Uses the shared `httpClient` so the `credentials: 'include'` cookie behavior is preserved — no direct `fetch`.
- `frontend/src/modules/workspace/hooks/useWorkspaces.ts` (new): TanStack Query hook wrapping `workspaceApi.list()` with stable query key `['workspaces']`. `useQuery<Workspace[]>` so the result type flows through to consumers without `any`.
- `frontend/src/pages/DashboardPage.tsx` (modified): consumes `useWorkspaces()`. Renders "Loading workspaces…" while pending and a count once data arrives ("N workspaces" with a tiny pluralization). Both states are cosmetic placeholders that TASK-037 (workspace switcher) and TASK-042 (dashboard project list) will replace. The point of the consumer is to prove the hook is wired through to a page — the test step requires it explicitly.

What was tested:
- `cd frontend && npm run build` (the explicit task `test_step`): BUILD SUCCESS. `tsc -b && vite build`, 91 modules transformed, 326.84 kB JS bundle, no TypeScript errors, no warnings. Build time 75ms.
- Verified the hook is consumed: `DashboardPage.tsx` imports `useWorkspaces` from the new hook file (line 1) and calls it (line 4). Grep confirms the import is the only one in the codebase, so the consumer is real and not dead code.
- Manual file inspection: `httpClient` already sends `credentials: 'include'` (httpClient.ts line 27), so the workspace request will carry the session cookie correctly.

Design choices:
- Mirror `authApi`'s shape exactly (an exported object literal with named methods, not a function-per-export). This is the established convention for API helpers in this repo. Keeping the convention uniform makes it trivial to grep call sites and to add `create`/`get`/`update` methods later when TASK-041, TASK-042, TASK-043 land.
- Query key is the literal `['workspaces']`. No user-id parameterization on the key because the workspace list is *always* the current authenticated user's workspaces — there is no other variant the backend will ever return. If the user changes (login/logout), `AuthProvider` should already invalidate or reset the query cache; that's TASK-030's territory, not TASK-036's. Adding a synthetic key like `['workspaces', userId]` would only matter if the cache survived across logins, which it should not.
- `useQuery<Workspace[]>` typed at the call site, not on `workspaceApi.list()` only. Both type the same value but the hook generic makes the cached `data` type explicit at every consumer. Cheap insurance against `any` leaking through if `workspaceApi.list()` ever loses its return type.
- Consumed in `DashboardPage.tsx`, not `AppShell.tsx`. The architecture has the workspace switcher landing in `AppShell` (TASK-037), so wiring `useWorkspaces` into `AppShell` now would either preempt TASK-037's UI or leave `AppShell` calling the hook without rendering anything visible from it. The dashboard page already shows a TASK-042/-043 placeholder, so adding a "loading workspaces…" / count line there is a natural minimum-viable consumer that TASK-042 will replace cleanly.
- No `enabled` flag, no `staleTime`/`gcTime` overrides, no `select` transformer. The endpoint is fast, the data is small, and the QueryClient defaults are fine. Adding configuration without a measured reason is exactly the premature-abstraction trap CLAUDE.md flags.
- No Zustand store for workspace selection. That belongs to TASK-037 (workspace switcher needs a *currently-selected* workspace, which is genuinely cross-cutting) — `useWorkspaces` itself is pure server state and lives in TanStack Query only, per CLAUDE.md's state-boundary rules.
- No `useWorkspace(id)` single-fetch hook. There's no backend endpoint for it (no `GET /api/workspaces/{id}`), and the list-then-find pattern composes from `useWorkspaces` if a future task needs a single workspace. Adding it now would be speculative.
- No barrel `index.ts` export. Both modules in `auth/` use deep imports (`../auth/hooks/useAuth`, `../auth/api/authApi`); matching that convention keeps grep-discoverability and avoids a redundant re-export layer.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- Cache invalidation on logout is not exercised here. If a user logs out and back in as a different user, the TanStack Query cache for `['workspaces']` would still hold the previous user's list until the staleTime expires. The fix is `queryClient.removeQueries({ queryKey: ['workspaces'] })` (or `clear()`) in the logout flow — that belongs to whichever task hardens logout/auth state transitions, not TASK-036. Worth flagging for TASK-037 since it'll be the first task that visibly depends on per-user freshness.
- No error UI. If `/api/workspaces` 5xx's, `useWorkspaces()` exposes `isError`/`error` but `DashboardPage` ignores them; only `isLoading` and `data` render. TASK-078 (loading/empty/error states across core screens) is the canonical home for that. Until then, an error currently shows nothing on the dashboard. Acceptable for TASK-036 — adding error UI now would either be thrown away or become a half-finished implementation that TASK-078 has to merge with.
- The placeholder text in `DashboardPage` is meant to be replaced. TASK-042 will render the project list and TASK-043 the create-project flow; the "N workspaces" line is a minimum-viable consumer, not a UX decision. The TASK-042 implementer should expect to delete or restructure it without ceremony.
- No tests added. The task's `test_steps` is `npm run build` only. No automated frontend tests have been added to this codebase yet (auth module has none either) — adding a Vitest or React Testing Library setup would be net-new infrastructure outside TASK-036's scope. TASK-082 (manual smoke checklist) and TASK-083 (smoke pass) are the canonical places for end-to-end UI validation.
- Next pending task: TASK-037 (render the workspace switcher in `AppShell`). Will consume `useWorkspaces` directly and likely introduce a small Zustand store for the *selected* workspace id (or push it into the URL). The hook from TASK-036 is the data source; the selection state is the new concern.

TASK-037 — Render the workspace switcher in AppShell
Date: 2026-04-27
================================================================================

What changed:
- `frontend/src/modules/workspace/components/WorkspaceSwitcher.tsx` (new): self-contained switcher that consumes `useWorkspaces()` (TASK-036) and renders a native `<select>` of workspace names with a small uppercase "Workspace" caption above. Three render branches: loading placeholder, empty-list placeholder ("No workspaces yet"), and the populated `<select>`. URL query param `workspace` is the source of truth for the current selection; the value falls back to `workspaces[0].id` if the param is missing or doesn't match a fetched workspace id. Selecting an option calls `setSearchParams` to write the new id back into the URL.
- `frontend/src/app/layouts/AppShell.tsx` (modified): replaced the "Workspace switcher — TASK-037" placeholder paragraph with `<WorkspaceSwitcher />`. Removed the unused TASK-037 placeholder import nothing (no `<p>` anymore). The "Sign out — TASK-029/030" placeholder at the bottom of the sidebar is left intact — that belongs to TASK-029/030 and is out of scope here.
- Marked TASK-037 status = done in tasks.json.

What was tested:
- `cd frontend && npm run build` (the explicit task `test_step`): BUILD SUCCESS. `tsc -b && vite build`, 92 modules transformed (was 91 — one new file), 328.67 kB JS bundle, no TypeScript errors, no warnings. Build time 77ms.
- Started `npm run dev` (Vite dev server on :5174 since :5173 was busy) and curled `/src/modules/workspace/components/WorkspaceSwitcher.tsx` to confirm Vite compiled the new module on demand without errors. Both `useSearchParams` (from `react-router-dom`) and `useWorkspaces` resolve through Vite's module graph.
- Did NOT do a full live browser walkthrough with backend up: requires Postgres + backend + a logged-in user to actually see workspace names. The acceptance ("renders loaded workspace names") is satisfied by code: `workspaces.map((workspace) => <option key={workspace.id} value={workspace.id}>{workspace.name}</option>)`. Visual sanity in a browser will land in TASK-082's smoke checklist.

Design choices:
- URL query param (`?workspace=<id>`) as source of truth, per architecture.md §6 "URL state — selected workspace". No Zustand store added. The architecture mentions "current workspace id fallback" as global client state, but a fallback store only earns its keep when a downstream consumer actually needs the workspace id without it being in the URL. Right now nothing does; TASK-039 (`GET /api/workspaces/{workspaceId}/projects`) and TASK-042 (dashboard project list) will be the first real consumers, and they can read the same query param. If at that point a global fallback turns out to matter (e.g., for routes that don't carry the param), a tiny Zustand slice can be added without rewriting the switcher.
- Default to `workspaces[0].id` *without* writing to the URL on mount. Writing on mount would either pollute the user's URL bar with a redundant param or trigger a navigation/render loop; reading-only with a fallback keeps URL state clean (you only see `?workspace=X` after the user actually picked one). The cost is that downstream code that wants "the currently selected workspace id" has to go through the same fallback logic — that's a tiny shared helper if it ever needs more than one consumer, but it's premature to extract it for TASK-037 alone.
- `setSearchParams(next)` (no `{ replace: true }`). Choosing a different workspace is a navigation event the user might want to back-button out of; pushing a history entry matches the URL-state-is-shareable contract from architecture.md §6. If this turns out to be too noisy in practice (e.g., users churning the dropdown), it's a one-line change to `replace: true`.
- Native `<select>` rather than a custom dropdown component. Architecture.md §5.3 calls for "almost-flat" pastel-green styling, no Tailwind, no styled-components, ≤ 8px radius — a styled native `<select>` matches that brief and avoids inventing a popover primitive that nothing else in the app uses yet. If we later add filtering, recent items, or per-workspace icons, a custom component can replace this without touching `AppShell`.
- Component lives in `modules/workspace/components/` (not inline in `AppShell.tsx` and not in `app/layouts/`). Architecture.md §5.4 explicitly says `modules/workspace` owns the workspace switcher; `AppShell` is a shell that composes module components. Inline JSX in `AppShell` would violate that boundary; placing it in `app/layouts/` would too. The module-owns-component placement also means future tasks (e.g., TASK-076 authz/role badges, or member-list integration) edit one focused file rather than a shell layout file.
- Style objects extracted to module-level constants (`labelStyle`, `selectStyle`, etc.) instead of inline objects. Two reasons: (1) avoids re-creating object literals on every render, and (2) keeps the JSX readable so the component shape is obvious at a glance. Same pattern would let a future shared component refactor extract these into a `shared/styles/inputs.ts` helper without churning the component body.
- No `enabled`/`staleTime`/manual refetch wiring. The `useWorkspaces()` hook already owns its caching policy from TASK-036; the switcher is a pure consumer.
- No `aria-*` attributes beyond what the native `<label>`+`<select>` pair provides. Native form semantics are sufficient for an MVP switcher. Polish (focus rings, hover states, accessible name overrides) belongs to TASK-080/082 if at all.
- Did NOT modify `DashboardPage.tsx`. TASK-036 made the dashboard a placeholder consumer of `useWorkspaces()` to satisfy that task's "consumed by a page or layout component" requirement. Now that AppShell consumes it too, both pages share TanStack Query's deduped `['workspaces']` cache — no extra request. Removing the dashboard placeholder would expand the scope of TASK-037 into TASK-042's territory; better to leave the placeholder for TASK-042 to replace cleanly when it lands.
- Did NOT add `aria-busy` or skeleton UI for the loading state. The placeholder paragraph is a one-time bootstrap concern (workspace list is fetched once at app shell mount and cached); a skeleton would be visual noise that flashes for ~50ms.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- The selected workspace id is *not* yet shared with any other component. When TASK-039/042 land (project list per workspace), they will read the same `?workspace=<id>` param via `useSearchParams`. If at that point we discover the read-then-fallback pattern needs to live in three places, factor it out as `useSelectedWorkspaceId()` in `modules/workspace/hooks/` — a 5-line hook around the same logic. Don't do it now.
- The URL param survives across pages (because it's set on the AppShellRoute's URL, and React Router preserves search params on the current location). However, navigating to a new path via `<Link to="/projects/123/board">` without preserving the search will drop the workspace id. If/when navigation links are added (TASK-042 and beyond), they should carry `?workspace=<id>` or pass it explicitly. Not relevant for TASK-037's scope, but the next frontend task to add navigation should handle it.
- `<select>` styling is browser-native for the dropdown popup. On Windows/Chrome the popup looks platform-default (white background, blue selection); the closed control matches our tokens. Acceptable for MVP — TASK-080 polish can revisit if needed.
- Switching workspaces does not currently invalidate any other query cache (because no other workspace-scoped queries exist yet). When TASK-039/042/047 land, they'll use the workspace id in their query keys (e.g., `['workspaces', workspaceId, 'projects']`), so changing the URL param naturally re-fetches without an explicit invalidation. The switcher should NOT eagerly invalidate caches it doesn't know about.
- `setSearchParams` was used without `replace: true`, so dropdown changes accumulate in browser history. If user testing reveals this is annoying (e.g., a back-button has to click through every workspace flip), flip to `replace: true`. Documented here so the next maintainer doesn't have to re-derive the choice.
- Empty-list state ("No workspaces yet") is a defensive branch: in practice, TASK-034 ensures every authenticated user has at least one workspace, so a healthy session should never hit it. The branch exists for graceful degradation if the bootstrap ever fails or for a future admin/impersonation flow.
- Next pending task: TASK-038 (project schema and persistence). Backend territory. The frontend workspace state contract is now in place for TASK-039/042 to consume.

================================================================================
TASK-038 — Add project schema and persistence
Date: 2026-04-27
================================================================================

What changed:
- `backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V5__projects.sql` (new): creates `projects` (id UUID PK with `gen_random_uuid()` default, `workspace_id` UUID NOT NULL → workspaces ON DELETE CASCADE, `owner_id` UUID NOT NULL → users ON DELETE RESTRICT, `name` TEXT NOT NULL, `slug` TEXT NOT NULL, `description` TEXT NULL, `created_at`/`updated_at` TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(), unique constraint on `(workspace_id, slug)`, btree index on `workspace_id`) and `project_members` (id UUID PK, `project_id` UUID NOT NULL → projects ON DELETE CASCADE, `user_id` UUID NOT NULL → users ON DELETE CASCADE, `role` TEXT NOT NULL with CHECK in `OWNER/ADMIN/MEMBER/VIEWER`, `created_at` TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL, unique on `(project_id, user_id)`, btree index on `user_id`). `owner_id` uses ON DELETE RESTRICT — deleting a user requires explicit ownership transfer; matches the workspace migration's choice.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/project/domain/ProjectRole.java` (new): enum `OWNER, ADMIN, MEMBER, VIEWER`, matching the SQL CHECK constraint values verbatim. Mirrors `WorkspaceRole`.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/project/domain/Project.java` (new): JPA `@Entity` for `projects`, plain Java (no Lombok), opaque UUID FKs for `workspaceId`/`ownerId` (no `@ManyToOne` — keeps the project module decoupled from the workspace and user JPA graphs per architecture.md §7). `@PrePersist`/`@PreUpdate` set `createdAt`/`updatedAt`. Mirrors `Workspace.java` exactly except `ownerId` lacks `updatable = false` (deliberate — ownership transfer is foreseeable for projects).
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/project/domain/ProjectMember.java` (new): JPA `@Entity` for `project_members`, plain Java, `@Enumerated(EnumType.STRING)` on the `role` column so the DB stores the enum name and the CHECK constraint enforces it. `@PrePersist` only (membership rows aren't mutated; if the role changes we'll add `@PreUpdate` then). Mirrors `WorkspaceMember.java`.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/project/infrastructure/ProjectRepository.java` (new): `JpaRepository<Project, UUID>` with `findAllByWorkspaceId`, `findByWorkspaceIdAndSlug`, `existsByWorkspaceIdAndSlug`. The set TASK-039 (list projects per workspace) and TASK-041 (create project — slug uniqueness check) will need; nothing speculative.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/project/infrastructure/ProjectMemberRepository.java` (new): `JpaRepository<ProjectMember, UUID>` with `findAllByProjectId`, `findAllByUserId`, `findByProjectIdAndUserId`, `existsByProjectIdAndUserId`. Mirrors `WorkspaceMemberRepository`.
- Marked TASK-038 status = done in tasks.json.

What was tested:
- `./mvnw -f backend compile test-compile` → BUILD SUCCESS, all 75 source files compile (was 69 + 6 new), 10 test-compile files unchanged.
- `./mvnw -f backend test` → 25 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped, BUILD SUCCESS. No new tests added — task `test_steps` only requires migrations + compile.
- Live Flyway smoke test against the running Postgres container: applied V1→V5 in sequence to a fresh `trackify_v5_test` database (`docker exec -i trackify-db psql -U trackify -d trackify_v5_test < V*__*.sql`). All four migrations succeeded. `\d projects` and `\d project_members` confirm: column types/nullability/defaults match the entity annotations, both unique constraints present, both FKs with the intended `ON DELETE` actions, the role CHECK constraint fires on the expected literal values. Cleaned up by dropping the temp DB. The dev DB (`trackify`) still only has V1–V3 applied because the backend hasn't been booted since V4 landed; first `spring-boot:run` will catch it up.

Design choices:
- Schema-and-persistence-only scope. No controller, no service, no DTO, no business logic — those land in TASK-039/040/041. The repositories expose only finders TASK-039/041 will plausibly need; no `findByOwnerId`, `countByWorkspaceId`, etc. — those would be speculative.
- Opaque UUID FKs (no `@ManyToOne` into `workspace` or `user` modules). This is the same call the workspace module made: keeps the module graph one-way (no JPA fetch chains crossing module boundaries), and the application layer joins via repository calls when it needs to. Architecture.md §7 modular monolith is the binding rule.
- Slug is unique within a workspace, not globally (`UNIQUE (workspace_id, slug)`). Two different workspaces can both have a `marketing` project. Workspace slug is global because the URL `/{workspaceSlug}` is a top-level route; project slug only ever appears under a workspace, so the looser scope is correct.
- `owner_id` on `Project` is `nullable = false` but NOT `updatable = false`. This is the only deliberate divergence from the workspace template. Workspaces represent the user's identity tier (their personal workspace cannot meaningfully change owner), but project ownership transfer (e.g., a leaving teammate's projects reassigned) is a foreseeable operation in TASK-076's authorization work. Locking it down now would require a follow-up migration; leaving it mutable costs nothing because no service-layer mutation exists to expose it yet.
- `ON DELETE RESTRICT` on the `owner_id` FK so deleting a user is a deliberate failure rather than silently orphaning project ownership. The workspace migration made the same call. If we later add a "soft delete" or "transfer-on-delete" flow, the constraint will surface the requirement at the boundary.
- `ON DELETE CASCADE` on `workspace_id` and on both `project_members` FKs. Workspace deletion implies project deletion (workspace is the container), and both ends of a project↔member relation are derivative — no need for the membership row to outlive either endpoint.
- Index on `(workspace_id)` for `findAllByWorkspaceId` and on `(user_id)` in `project_members` for `findAllByUserId`. Both will be the hot read path for TASK-039/042. Did NOT add an index on `project_members(project_id)` — the unique `(project_id, user_id)` constraint already creates a btree starting with `project_id`, so a separate index would be redundant.
- `ProjectRole` enum mirrors the SQL CHECK list exactly. The CHECK constraint is the load-bearing enforcement (since the column type is plain TEXT) — `@Enumerated(EnumType.STRING)` makes the JPA write side compatible. If the enum gains a new value, both the migration's CHECK and the Java enum need to update together; that's the contract.
- Did NOT add a `@Modifying` or custom `@Query` method on either repository. Spring Data derived queries cover everything the upcoming tasks need, and adding hand-written JPQL up front would just be wallpaper.
- Did NOT register the entities anywhere globally (no `@EntityScan` change). Spring Boot auto-scans under the application's base package (`com.trackify`), so `com.trackify.project.domain.*` is already in the entity scan path.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- The dev DB (`trackify`) currently has V1–V3 applied; V4 (workspaces) and V5 (projects) will both run on the next `spring-boot:run`. Verified the migration sequence is sound by running it against a fresh temp DB. If the dev DB has any rows in `users` that conflict with the new constraints, those are existing test data and should be fine — `users(id)` is the only referenced column.
- `ownerId` mutability deviates from the workspace template by intent. TASK-076 (workspace/project authorization checks) is the natural place to harden the rule one way or the other. If product wants strict project-ownership immutability, add `updatable = false` to the entity field and a `Vn__lock_project_owner_id.sql` migration that drops/recreates the column with explicit non-update enforcement (or just rely on the entity-side flag — Hibernate will refuse to write).
- No `Project::getRole(userId)` or `ProjectMember::isOwner()` convenience helpers. Those should live in an application service when TASK-076 needs them, not on the entity — keeps domain objects free of authorization logic.
- Next pending task: TASK-039 (`GET /api/workspaces/{workspaceId}/projects`). It will be the first consumer of `ProjectRepository.findAllByWorkspaceId` and the first task that boots the app against the live DB after V4/V5 land — whatever drift exists between SQL and JPA will surface there. The temp-DB Flyway run done here is a strong indicator that drift is zero, but the spring-boot:run startup with `ddl-auto: validate` is the contractual gate.

================================================================================
TASK-039 — Implement GET /api/workspaces/{workspaceId}/projects
Date: 2026-04-27
================================================================================

What changed:
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/common/exception/ForbiddenException.java` (new): mirror of `NotFoundException`, runtime exception thrown when an authenticated caller is not permitted to access a resource.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/common/exception/GlobalExceptionHandler.java`: added `@ExceptionHandler(ForbiddenException.class)` that maps to HTTP 403 with `error="FORBIDDEN"` in the existing `ApiError` envelope. No other handlers touched.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/project/dto/ProjectResponse.java` (new): record exposing `id`, `workspaceId`, `name`, `slug`, `description`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`. Mirrors `WorkspaceResponse` in shape; entities never cross this boundary.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/project/application/ProjectQueryService.java` (new): `@Service` with `@Transactional(readOnly = true) listForWorkspace(workspaceId, userId)`. Two-step flow: (1) `WorkspaceMemberRepository.existsByWorkspaceIdAndUserId` — non-member → `ForbiddenException`; (2) `ProjectRepository.findAllByWorkspaceId`, sorted by name, mapped through a private static `toResponse`. Same shape as `WorkspaceQueryService` (architecture.md §7 layering).
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/project/controller/ProjectController.java` (new): `@RestController @RequestMapping("/api/workspaces/{workspaceId}/projects")` with one `@GetMapping listProjects(@PathVariable UUID workspaceId, @AuthenticationPrincipal LocalUserPrincipal principal)`. No business logic; delegates straight to `ProjectQueryService`. Authentication is enforced by the catch-all `.requestMatchers("/api/**").authenticated()` in `SecurityConfig`.
- `backend/src/test/java/com/trackify/project/controller/ProjectControllerTest.java` (new): `@WebMvcTest(ProjectController.class)` mirroring the existing `WorkspaceControllerTest` setup (`SecurityConfig`+`WebConfig`+`JacksonConfig` imports, CORS test props). Covers: (1) member happy path — returns the two stubbed projects with correct DTO field names; (2) non-member forbidden — service throws `ForbiddenException`, expects 403 with `{status:403, error:"FORBIDDEN", message:"Workspace not accessible"}` envelope; (3) member with no projects — empty array; (4) unauthenticated — 401 from Spring Security and `verifyNoInteractions(projectQueryService)` to prove the controller never ran.
- Marked TASK-039 status = done in tasks.json.

What was tested:
- `./mvnw -f backend compile test-compile` → BUILD SUCCESS, 79 source files (was 75 + 4 new), 11 test-compile files (was 10 + 1 new).
- `./mvnw -f backend test` → Tests run: 29, Failures: 0, Errors: 0, Skipped: 0. Was 25 before; +4 from `ProjectControllerTest`. All previous tests still green.
- The `GlobalExceptionHandlerTest` already covers the legacy `NotFoundException`→404 path; the new `ForbiddenException`→403 path is exercised by the controller test rather than a duplicate handler unit test, since `@WebMvcTest` runs the real `GlobalExceptionHandler` and asserts the rendered envelope (status, error, message). That removes the temptation to keep both tests in sync.

Design choices:
- `ForbiddenException` introduced now (TASK-039) rather than waiting for TASK-077. The task's own `test_steps` say "controller test for the authorized happy path and forbidden path", which is only meaningful with a 403 response. TASK-077 is "standardize 401/403/404" — the JSON envelope is already standardized through `ApiError.of(...)`, so TASK-077's remaining work is to extend the envelope (e.g., timestamps, request ids) and prove a consistent test for each code; introducing the 403 type now does not foreclose that. The alternative — collapsing forbidden into `NotFoundException` to avoid leaking workspace existence — is defensible at the API surface but conflicts with the task wording and would need to be unwound in TASK-077 anyway. Keep semantics literal; let TASK-077 decide envelope-level changes.
- Authorization at the *application service* layer, not the controller. The controller is dumb on purpose (architecture.md §7.1 — "controller: HTTP only, thin, no business logic"). Putting the membership check in `ProjectQueryService.listForWorkspace` means TASK-040/041 (project detail / project create) reuse the same gate the same way without duplicating the check across controllers. TASK-076 will harden / standardize this further, but the seam is in the right place from day one.
- `WorkspaceMemberRepository` is the source of truth for workspace membership. The project module *reuses* it rather than introducing its own materialized "is the user in this workspace" view. This is the same pattern `WorkspaceQueryService` uses to drive `/api/workspaces` (architecture.md §10 — read paths cross modules through repositories, not through cross-module JPA fetches). The single-direction module dependency `project → workspace.infrastructure` is acceptable; the reverse would not be.
- DTO includes `description`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt` even though TASK-039 only requires "projects in a workspace". TASK-042 (dashboard project list) will render project cards, and rendering metadata up front avoids a second fetch round-trip per project. Also: keeping the read DTO at the `Project` entity's full public shape makes TASK-040 (`GET /api/projects/{id}`) trivially reuse the same DTO without inventing a `ProjectDetailResponse`. If a UI component ever wants a strict subset, that's a `ProjectSummaryResponse` change at *that* time, not now.
- Sort by `name` (case-sensitive `Comparator.comparing`) for stable ordering. Same rule as the workspace listing. If product wants case-insensitive or recency-based later (e.g., "recently active first"), that's a service-level change that doesn't affect the contract.
- `@PathVariable UUID workspaceId` — Spring will respond with 400 for malformed UUIDs via the default conversion path. No custom binding needed for TASK-039; this matches the implicit behavior the workspace endpoints already rely on for any future path-bound IDs.
- No service-level unit test for `ProjectQueryService`. The controller test exercises both the happy and forbidden branches end-to-end through the real `GlobalExceptionHandler`, which is a stronger signal than a mocked-repository unit test would be. If TASK-076 introduces more nuanced authz (e.g., role-based), that's the right time to spin up `ProjectQueryServiceTest` with `@ExtendWith(MockitoExtension.class)`. Adding it preemptively would test code that currently has only one branch worth testing (membership: yes/no), which the controller test already covers.
- `@MockitoBean private ProjectQueryService projectQueryService` (Spring Boot 3.4 `org.springframework.test.context.bean.override.mockito.MockitoBean`), matching the `WorkspaceControllerTest` style — *not* the deprecated `@MockBean`. This avoids a deprecation warning and keeps both controller tests consistent.
- No `@PreAuthorize` on the controller. Spring Security's URL-level `.requestMatchers("/api/**").authenticated()` already covers authentication; method-level role authz is not needed because the workspace-membership check is the meaningful gate and lives in the service. Adding `@PreAuthorize("isAuthenticated()")` would be dead wallpaper.
- `ProjectController` lives at `/api/workspaces/{workspaceId}/projects` exactly as architecture.md §11 specifies, and as TASK-039 description states. A flatter `/api/projects?workspaceId=...` would duplicate the URL hierarchy without simplifying anything; the nested form is also what TASK-040/041 will pivot off.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- The 403 envelope will be re-asserted in TASK-077 against a standardized shape. If TASK-077 changes `ApiError` (e.g., adds a stable `requestId`), the literal `jsonPath` matchers in `ProjectControllerTest.nonMemberReceivesForbidden` will keep passing as long as the existing fields stay the same; that's why the test only matches `status`, `error`, `message` and not the entire body.
- `ProjectQueryService` does not currently distinguish "workspace doesn't exist" from "workspace exists but caller is not a member" — both throw `ForbiddenException`. This is intentional: it doesn't leak workspace existence to outsiders, and `existsByWorkspaceIdAndUserId` collapses the two cases into one query. TASK-040 (`GET /api/projects/{id}`) will face the same call — if product later wants to surface 404 explicitly for "project does not exist at all" while keeping 403 for "exists in another workspace", the join-on-project-then-check-membership pattern lives there.
- Did NOT touch `SecurityConfig`. The catch-all `/api/**` rule is doing its job. Anything else would require a frontend or audit-driven change outside this task's scope.
- Did NOT add a `ProjectMember`-level check yet. TASK-039 is workspace-scoped (the URL is workspace-keyed), and project-level membership only matters for TASK-040 onwards (`/api/projects/{projectId}/...`). If a workspace member should be allowed to *see* every project in their workspace even if they're not a project-member — which is the current semantics, since we filter by `workspace_id` only — that's the implicit MVP rule. TASK-076 will be the place to lock down "workspace member but not project member can list but not edit" if product wants that distinction.
- Next pending task: TASK-040 — `GET /api/projects/{projectId}`. It will reuse `ProjectRepository` (already has `findById` from `JpaRepository`) and the same membership pattern, but keyed off the project's `workspaceId` rather than a path-supplied one. The forbidden/not-found split decision noted above will need to be resolved there.

================================================================================
TASK-040 — Implement GET /api/projects/{projectId}
Date: 2026-04-27
================================================================================

What changed:
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/project/application/ProjectQueryService.java`: added `getById(UUID projectId, UUID userId)` (`@Transactional(readOnly = true)`). Loads via `projectRepository.findById` — absent → `NotFoundException("Project not found")`. Then calls the same `workspaceMemberRepository.existsByWorkspaceIdAndUserId(project.getWorkspaceId(), userId)` gate as `listForWorkspace` — non-member → `ForbiddenException("Project not accessible")`. Reuses the existing private static `toResponse` mapper so the DTO shape is identical to the list endpoint. Imported `NotFoundException`; added a Javadoc paragraph explaining why this method's authorization semantics differ from `listForWorkspace`.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/project/controller/ProjectByIdController.java` (new): `@RestController @RequestMapping("/api/projects")` with one `@GetMapping("/{projectId}") getProject(@PathVariable UUID projectId, @AuthenticationPrincipal LocalUserPrincipal principal)`. No business logic; delegates to `projectQueryService.getById`. Authentication is enforced by `SecurityConfig`'s catch-all `/api/**` rule.
- `backend/src/test/java/com/trackify/project/controller/ProjectByIdControllerTest.java` (new): `@WebMvcTest(controllers = ProjectByIdController.class)` mirroring `ProjectControllerTest`'s setup (`SecurityConfig`+`WebConfig`+`JacksonConfig` imports, CORS test props, `@MockitoBean ProjectQueryService`, principal-based authentication helper). Covers all four cases: (1) found — happy path returns DTO with id/workspaceId/name/slug/description; (2) missing — `NotFoundException` → 404 with `{status:404, error:"NOT_FOUND", message:"Project not found"}`; (3) forbidden — `ForbiddenException` → 403 with `{status:403, error:"FORBIDDEN", message:"Project not accessible"}`; (4) unauthenticated — 401 plus `verifyNoInteractions(projectQueryService)` to prove the controller never ran.
- Marked TASK-040 status = done in tasks.json.

What was tested:
- `./mvnw -f backend compile test-compile` → BUILD SUCCESS, 82 source files (was 79 + 3 new) and 12 test-compile files (was 11 + 1 new).
- `./mvnw -f backend test` → Tests run: 33, Failures: 0, Errors: 0, Skipped: 0. Was 29 before; +4 from `ProjectByIdControllerTest`. All previous tests still green. (Surefire prints the `ForbiddenException`/`NotFoundException` stack traces from the `@WebMvcTest` exception handler chain — that's expected logging from the negative-path mocks, not a failure; the suite-level `Tests run` line is the source of truth.)

Design choices:
- Authorization split (404 vs 403) deliberately differs from `listForWorkspace`. The TASK-039 follow-up note in progress.txt anticipated this: when the URL is project-keyed, "missing" and "forbidden" are observably different cases and the task spec lists both in `test_steps`. So `getById` loads the project first, then checks membership. The trade-off is minor existence leakage — an attacker can probe `/api/projects/{uuid}` and tell "exists somewhere I'm not a member of" (403) from "doesn't exist at all" (404). For the MVP this is acceptable, and TASK-076/077 are the places to tighten the policy if product objects (e.g., collapse 403 into 404 to prevent enumeration). Documented this in the `getById` Javadoc.
- New controller class `ProjectByIdController` rather than folding the endpoint into `ProjectController`. The two endpoints sit at different `@RequestMapping` roots (`/api/workspaces/{workspaceId}/projects` vs `/api/projects`) and Spring does not allow a class-level `@RequestMapping` to host both. The cheapest options were (a) drop the workspace-scoped controller's class-level mapping and put the prefix on each method, or (b) split into two classes. Picked (b) because it keeps the existing `ProjectController` untouched (less diff, less risk of breaking the `ProjectControllerTest` suite) and gives each controller a single concern. If TASK-041 (`POST /api/workspaces/{workspaceId}/projects`) lands in `ProjectController`, the symmetry still works: workspace-scoped reads + workspace-scoped writes in one class, project-scoped reads in the other.
- Reuse `ProjectResponse` rather than introduce `ProjectDetailResponse`. The TASK-039 progress note explicitly anticipated this: the response shape is already at the entity's full public projection, so no new DTO is warranted. If a future task needs a strict subset (e.g., a read-only list summary that excludes `description`), that's a `ProjectSummaryResponse` change at *that* time, not preemptive splitting.
- `projectRepository.findById(projectId).orElseThrow(...)` instead of a custom derived query like `findByIdAndWorkspaceMember(...)`. The pure-`findById` form is simpler, exposes the membership-not-met case as its own branch, and lets the test mock `ProjectQueryService` cleanly without needing a join-aware repository fixture. A `JOIN FETCH workspace_members` query would be a premature optimization for one row.
- Service-level reuse of `WorkspaceMemberRepository` (already imported by this class) rather than introducing a new abstraction in the project module. Single-direction module dependency `project → workspace.infrastructure` matches the existing pattern; architecture.md §10 explicitly allows read paths to cross modules through repositories.
- No `ProjectQueryServiceTest` unit test. The controller test runs the real `GlobalExceptionHandler` and asserts the 404/403/200 envelopes end-to-end through `@WebMvcTest`. That is a stronger signal than a mocked-repository unit test would be, and matches the precedent set by `ProjectControllerTest` (which also defers to the controller-level integration). When TASK-076 introduces role-based authorization, that's the right time to spin up `ProjectQueryServiceTest` with mocked repos to exercise the role permutations.
- `@MockitoBean` (NOT `@MockBean`) — Spring Boot 3.4 deprecated `@MockBean` in favor of `org.springframework.test.context.bean.override.mockito.MockitoBean`. Both `WorkspaceControllerTest` and `ProjectControllerTest` use `@MockitoBean`, so this stays consistent.
- Test message strings are pinned to the exact production strings ("Project not found", "Project not accessible"). If TASK-077 standardizes the envelope text, these matchers will need to update — but they currently encode the contract that the controller advertises, which is the correct level of strictness.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- The 404 vs 403 distinction at `/api/projects/{id}` lets a caller learn whether a UUID corresponds to *any* project, even one they cannot access. This is a known minor information leak. TASK-076 (workspace/project authorization checks) is the natural place to decide whether to suppress it; one option is to rewrite `getById` to use a single `findByIdAndWorkspaceMember` derived query and collapse both negative cases into 404. Documented in the Javadoc on the service method.
- Did NOT add `@PreAuthorize` to the controller. Spring Security's URL-level `.requestMatchers("/api/**").authenticated()` covers authentication; method-level role authz is irrelevant because the membership check lives in the service.
- Did NOT add a `ProjectMember`-level check. Identical rationale to TASK-039 — workspace membership is the gate, project-membership is a TASK-076 question. If product wants "workspace member can read every project but only project members can edit", that distinction is independent of the read endpoint and lands at write-time.
- Did NOT modify `SecurityConfig`, `WebConfig`, or `JacksonConfig`. The new endpoint slots into the existing rules without any infra change.
- Did NOT touch `ProjectController`, `ProjectControllerTest`, or `ProjectResponse`. All TASK-039 behavior remains exactly as it was — additive changes only.
- Next pending task: TASK-041 (`POST /api/workspaces/{workspaceId}/projects`). It will be the first project-write endpoint, and the first time the project module needs a slug-uniqueness rule. The existing `ProjectQueryService.listForWorkspace` membership-gate pattern is the natural template for the create command's pre-check; the create itself will need a new `ProjectCommandService` (or `ProjectService`) keeping the read/write split clean.

TASK-041 — Implement POST /api/workspaces/{workspaceId}/projects
Date: 2026-04-27
================================================================================

What changed:
- backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/common/exception/ConflictException.java (new): thin RuntimeException subclass mapped to HTTP 409 by GlobalExceptionHandler. Created because the DB has UNIQUE(workspace_id, slug) on projects; without a pre-check a slug collision surfaces as a raw 500 from JPA/JDBC. Same shape as ForbiddenException.
- backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/common/exception/GlobalExceptionHandler.java: added @ExceptionHandler(ConflictException.class) handler returning ApiError with status:409, error:"CONFLICT". Inserted between handleForbidden and handleValidation.
- backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/project/dto/ProjectCreateRequest.java (new): record with @NotBlank @Size(max=255) on name, @Size(max=255) on slug (optional — null means service derives from name), @Size(max=2000) on description. Validation errors flow through existing GlobalExceptionHandler.handleValidation -> 400 VALIDATION_FAILED.
- backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/project/application/ProjectCreateService.java (new): @Service with @Transactional create(workspaceId, userId, request). Checks workspace membership (-> 403). Derives slug from name when client omits it (lowercase, whitespace->hyphens, strip non-alphanumeric/hyphen). Pre-checks existsByWorkspaceIdAndSlug (-> 409 if collision). Saves Project, maps to ProjectResponse. Package-private static deriveSlug() is separately testable.
- backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/project/controller/ProjectController.java: injected ProjectCreateService (second constructor arg); added @PostMapping createProject returning ResponseEntity<ProjectResponse> with status 201 and Location header built via ServletUriComponentsBuilder.
- backend/src/test/java/com/trackify/project/controller/ProjectControllerTest.java: added @MockitoBean ProjectCreateService; added 4 new test methods: memberCanCreateProject (201 + Location + body + verify service invoked), nonMemberCannotCreateProject (403), blankNameIsRejectedWith400 (400 VALIDATION_FAILED + verifyNoInteractions), unauthenticatedPostRequestIsRejectedWith401 (401 + verifyNoInteractions). Injected ObjectMapper via @Autowired for serializing request bodies.
- Marked TASK-041 status = done in tasks.json.

What was tested:
- cd backend && ./mvnw compile test-compile -> BUILD SUCCESS, 85 source files + 12 test files.
- cd backend && ./mvnw test -> Tests run: 37, Failures: 0, Errors: 0, Skipped: 0, BUILD SUCCESS. Was 33 before; +4 new POST tests. All 33 previous tests unchanged.

Design choices:
- Service split: separate ProjectCreateService rather than adding create() to ProjectQueryService. A QueryService that mutates state is semantically misleading; matches UserRegistrationService precedent in auth module (dedicated write service alongside the read service).
- Slug strategy: client-supplied slug is optional; service derives from name when absent (deterministic: lowercase + hyphens). Client can override. Slug uniqueness enforced per-workspace (matches DB UNIQUE constraint). Pre-check existsByWorkspaceIdAndSlug -> 409 ConflictException before insert, avoiding DataIntegrityViolation 500. Race condition (two concurrent POSTs same slug) is acceptable for MVP; TASK-076 can add retry.
- Response: 201 Created with Location header and full ProjectResponse body. Reused ProjectResponse — no ProjectCreatedResponse variant since shape is identical to list/get.
- Forbidden for non-member: 403 ForbiddenException("Workspace not accessible"). Same gate as listForWorkspace.
- ConflictException is genuinely new: without it a slug collision surfaces as opaque 500, which is user-hostile and leaks internal errors. Satisfies the "genuinely need one" bar.
- No ProjectMember auto-insertion: V5 has project_members table but architecture.md does not specify auto-adding the creator on project create. Deferred to later task.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- TASK-042 (frontend project creation form) can now POST to /api/workspaces/{workspaceId}/projects with name, optional slug, optional description and expect 201 + ProjectResponse.
- TASK-045 (board-column auto-creation) should hook into ProjectCreateService or a domain event — not bypass task application services.
- TASK-076: the existsByWorkspaceIdAndSlug pre-check + save pair is not atomic. Mitigation: catch DataIntegrityViolationException in ProjectCreateService and re-throw as ConflictException. Self-contained change inside that service.
- GlobalExceptionHandlerTest does not yet cover ConflictException -> 409. The ProjectControllerTest does not exercise this path either (keeping scope minimal per task). Deferred.
- Did NOT modify SecurityConfig, ProjectByIdController, ProjectByIdControllerTest, or any Flyway migration.

TASK-042 — Add dashboard project list UI
Date: 2026-04-27
================================================================================

What changed:
- frontend/src/modules/project/types/project.ts (new): Project interface mirroring the backend ProjectResponse DTO field-for-field (id, workspaceId, name, slug, description, createdAt, updatedAt). description is `string | null` because the backend column is nullable; createdAt/updatedAt are `string` because httpClient returns the raw JSON shape (Instant serializes to an ISO-8601 string).
- frontend/src/modules/project/api/projectApi.ts (new): single `listForWorkspace(workspaceId)` method calling httpClient.get<Project[]> on `/workspaces/{workspaceId}/projects`. Mirrors workspaceApi shape exactly (object literal, named methods).
- frontend/src/modules/project/hooks/useProjects.ts (new): TanStack Query hook keyed `['workspaces', workspaceId, 'projects']`. Uses `enabled: Boolean(workspaceId)` so the query is parked while the workspace list is still loading or empty (no spurious 404/UUID-cast 400 from the backend).
- frontend/src/pages/DashboardPage.tsx (rewritten): now reads the selected workspace from `useSearchParams().get('workspace')`, falling back to the first workspace when the URL param is absent or stale (same fallback as WorkspaceSwitcher). Calls useProjects(selectedWorkspaceId) and renders one of: workspaces-loading, no-workspace, projects-loading, projects-error, empty-state, or a CSS-grid of ProjectCard tiles. Pure inline-style components using design tokens (var(--space-*), --color-*, --radius-*, --shadow-xs); no Tailwind, no styled-components.
- Marked TASK-042 status = done in tasks.json.

What was tested:
- `cd frontend && npm run build` -> BUILD SUCCESS. tsc -b clean (TS strict on); vite build emits 94 modules transformed (was 91), 330 kB JS bundle, no warnings, 89 ms.
- Dev server smoke test: `npm run dev` boots cleanly, GET / returns 200, and Vite serves /src/pages/DashboardPage.tsx with the expected import graph (useSearchParams from react-router-dom, useWorkspaces, useProjects). Confirms HMR module wiring is intact.
- ESLint pass: only the two pre-existing react-refresh/only-export-components errors in src/app/routes.tsx (untouched by this task) — no new lint errors introduced.
- Could not perform a full headless browser E2E (no users seeded in the dev DB; AUTH_BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_* left blank in .env.example and no .env present), but verified the data-shape contract end-to-end by reading the backend ProjectResponse record (com.trackify.project.dto.ProjectResponse: id/workspaceId/name/slug/description/createdAt/updatedAt) and confirming the frontend Project interface mirrors it field-for-field, including description nullability. The endpoint URL path matches what ProjectController.listProjects exposes (TASK-039). No drift between layers.

Design choices:
- URL state for selected workspace, server state via TanStack Query, no Zustand store — exactly the boundary architecture.md §6 prescribes. The dashboard derives `selectedWorkspaceId` from the same URL key (`workspace`) that WorkspaceSwitcher writes, so picking a workspace in the sidebar instantly re-fetches projects via React Router's re-render of the dashboard with new search params. Zero state-sync code on the client.
- Did NOT extract a `useSelectedWorkspaceId` hook even though both DashboardPage and WorkspaceSwitcher now compute the same fallback. Two call sites is below the abstraction-pull threshold (architecture.md / CLAUDE.md "three similar lines is better than a premature abstraction"). When TASK-049 (board page) and TASK-069 (timeline page) want the *project*-id URL state, that pattern lives at the *project* level, not the workspace level. If a third workspace-scoped page lands later, that's the right time to pull the helper.
- Query key is `['workspaces', workspaceId, 'projects']`. Flat tuple, workspaceId in the middle. This nests cleanly under the workspace namespace so a future per-workspace cache invalidation (e.g., on workspace delete) can predicate on the prefix without invalidating unrelated queries. Documented in TASK-039's progress note as the expected key shape for TASK-042.
- `enabled: Boolean(workspaceId)` rather than throwing/asserting. While workspaces are loading (`workspacesLoading=true`), `selectedWorkspaceId` is undefined, the projects query is parked, and the UI shows the workspace-loading message. No double spinner, no flash of "no projects yet" before the first useProjects call.
- ProjectCard is a small co-located function component, not a separate file. The dashboard is its only consumer; pulling it into `modules/project/components/ProjectCard.tsx` would be speculation about a future reuse that has no concrete second site (TASK-080's settings page is a *list/detail* of one project, not a list of cards). If TASK-049/068/080 add a second card-style list, that is the right moment to extract.
- Empty state copy points to TASK-043 ("No projects yet. Project creation lands in TASK-043."). Intentional — TASK-043 will replace the placeholder with a real "Create project" button, and the breadcrumb in copy makes the intermediate state navigable for the next iteration. It is the ONLY task-id reference left on the page; the previous "TASK-042/043" hint has been removed because the page now does what TASK-042 promised.
- Did NOT touch httpClient, queryKeys.ts, AppShell, or routes.tsx. The base URL (/api) and credentials handling are already correct. Nothing else needs to change.
- Did NOT model `description` as `string | undefined`. The Java DTO emits null literally (Jackson does not strip nulls by default in this codebase), so `string | null` matches what JSON.parse will hand to the React tree. The conditional render `{project.description && <p>...}` falsy-coerces both null and empty string, which is the rendering behavior we want.

Follow-up risk / notes:
- A user with no ?workspace= query param sees the *first* workspace's projects, which is fine for MVP. The WorkspaceSwitcher does NOT push the default workspace into the URL on first load (it only writes when the user changes the dropdown), so the URL stays clean. If TASK-080's deep-links break because of this implicit selection, the fix is to canonicalize on first mount in WorkspaceSwitcher with `setSearchParams(..., { replace: true })`. Not a TASK-042 concern.
- `description` rendering uses raw text. If a future task adds Markdown or rich text in the project description, this card needs sanitization. Until then plain text is correct and safe.
- The 240px-min-width grid template is a reasonable guess based on the design tokens. If a project has a very long name, the card grows vertically without overflow handling. TASK-078 (loading/empty/error states) is the natural place to harden text overflow if needed.
- Could not run a full browser E2E (no seeded users + no .env). The login + workspace bootstrap path is already covered by backend tests (UserRegistrationServiceTest, WorkspaceBootstrapServiceTest) and the project read path by ProjectControllerTest, so the wire format is contract-tested even without a live click-through.
- Next pending task: TASK-043 — Add create-project UI. It will reuse projectApi (extend with a create method), wire to useMutation with queryClient.invalidateQueries on the same ['workspaces', workspaceId, 'projects'] key, and replace the empty-state copy with a real form/modal. The query-key shape chosen in TASK-042 makes the invalidation a one-liner.
