## 2026-04-27 — TASK-057: Add priority and date editors in the drawer

What changed:
- MODIFIED frontend/src/modules/task/components/TaskDrawer.tsx
  - Replaced the read-only Priority badge with a `<select>` of LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH/URGENT options. onChange fires `updateTask({ priority })` when value differs.
  - Replaced the read-only Start/Due date `<p>` elements with `<input type="date">` controls. onChange fires `updateTask({ startDate })` / `updateTask({ dueDate })` when value differs and is non-empty.
  - Added cross-field date constraints: start date `max={dueDate}`, due date `min={startDate}` so the native date picker prevents users from creating an inverted range without a server round-trip.
  - Added `editControlStyle` (shared by select + date inputs) and a `PRIORITY_OPTIONS` constant.
  - Removed the now-unused `priorityBadgeStyle` (dead code after the badge was replaced).
  - Added `TaskPriority` to the existing import from `kanban/types/task`.

Design decisions:
- Direct controlled inputs against `data.<field>` rather than draft state. Reasons: (a) priority and dates have no "in-progress typing" UX — the user picks one value and commits immediately; (b) `<input type="date">` already commits via native date picker selection; (c) the select dropdown closes on selection. No blur/Enter/Escape choreography is needed for these editors, unlike title and description.
- All three editors disable themselves while `isPending`. This prevents double-submission and gives passive "saving" feedback (the textarea section already shows a "Saving…" line; that line covers the whole drawer's pending state). The disabled state mirrors the already-disabled title/description inputs.
- Cleared dates intentionally do NOT fire a mutation. Backend semantics (TASK-055) treat `null` in the request as "keep unchanged" and have no sentinel for "clear". An empty `<input type="date">` returns `""` which we early-return on. This means a user CANNOT clear an existing date through this UI — that is the same limitation flagged in TASK-055/TASK-056 follow-up notes. The acceptance criteria for TASK-057 say "edit priority, start date, and due date" — replacement is in scope, clearing is not. If clearing becomes required, the backend DTO needs a sentinel (e.g. `JsonNullable`) and a follow-up frontend "Clear" button next to each input.
- Same-value selections (e.g. picking the already-selected priority) are intentionally short-circuited so the network is not hit on a no-op.
- Status is left as a read-only badge. Status changes flow through column moves (TASK-058–061) and are intentionally NOT controlled by `PATCH /api/tasks/{taskId}`.
- Used a shared `editControlStyle` instead of separate styles for select/date because both are minor form controls with the same visual weight; deduping keeps the file shorter and matches `editInputBaseStyle` reuse pattern.
- No optimistic UI: the controlled inputs reflect `data.<field>` directly, so users see the new value only after `setQueryData` echoes the server response. The user-visible delay is one round-trip (~tens of ms locally). Optimistic update is not in scope and would be premature given there is no real network here.
- Did NOT touch `useUpdateTask`, `taskDetailApi.updateTask`, or `UpdateTaskRequest` — they already supported priority/startDate/dueDate from TASK-056.
- Did NOT touch the backend — TASK-055 already implements the partial update for these fields.
- Did NOT touch board card rendering. After the mutation, `invalidateQueries(['projects', projectId, 'board'])` from `useUpdateTask` (TASK-056) refreshes the board, which already reads `priority`, `startDate`, `dueDate` per `BoardTaskCard`.

What was tested:
- `cd frontend && npm run build` — exit 0. tsc -b clean, vite built 410 modules, 370.19 kB / 113.85 kB gzip. (Bundle grew by ~2.7 kB unminified vs the prior build, consistent with adding a select + 2 date inputs and removing a small badge style.)
- `cd frontend && npm run lint` — only the 2 pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` errors in `routes.tsx`. Zero new errors.
- Browser click-through NOT performed (no live browser in this environment, same gap noted on TASK-051/053/054/056). The task `test_steps` say: "Edit the dates and priority in the UI and confirm the values persist after reload." Manual verification by a human is required and will be covered by the MVP smoke pass (TASK-083).
- End-to-end wiring confirmed by code reading:
  - select.onChange -> updateTask({ priority }) -> taskDetailApi.updateTask -> PATCH /api/tasks/{taskId} with `{priority: "HIGH"}` -> backend `TaskCommandService.updateTask` runs `resolvePriority("HIGH")` -> task.setPriority("HIGH") -> save -> response -> setQueryData(['tasks', taskId]) -> select re-renders with new value -> invalidateQueries refreshes the board card.
  - date input onChange (e.g. value="2026-05-15") -> updateTask({ startDate: "2026-05-15" }) -> Jackson deserializes ISO date -> LocalDate -> task.setStartDate -> save -> response carries `startDate: "2026-05-15"` -> input re-renders.
- Backend was not recompiled or tested for this task; backend code untouched (TASK-055 already covered the contract).

Follow-up risk / notes for TASK-058 (Kanban DnD):
- The drawer now writes priority/dates but never status or columnId. TASK-058 will introduce drag-and-drop, and TASK-059 (`PATCH /api/tasks/{taskId}/move`) will be a separate endpoint. These two endpoints (`/move` for column transitions, the existing `/` for content edits) need to coexist; they already do, since `TaskCommandService.updateTask` does NOT touch `columnId` or `sortOrder`.
- Date clearing is still not supported. If TASK-057 follow-up review demands it, the cheapest fix is: add a small "✕" button next to each date input in the drawer that POSTs an explicit "clear" command — but this requires either a backend sentinel (Jackson's `JsonNullable<LocalDate>` is the standard option) or a parallel `DELETE /api/tasks/{taskId}/dates/{which}` endpoint. Out of scope for TASK-057.
- Date order constraint is enforced only client-side via `min`/`max` on the date inputs. The backend does not currently validate that `startDate <= dueDate`. If a user types a date manually (browser support varies) or another client bypasses the inputs, an inverted range can be persisted. Consider a server-side check in TASK-070 (`PATCH /api/tasks/{taskId}/schedule`) since that is the dedicated scheduling endpoint and is the natural home for cross-date validation.
- Priority `<select>` uses a string union (`TaskPriority`) for the option values. If a future refactor moves to enums or numeric codes, both this drawer and the backend resolver need updating in lockstep. Not a near-term concern.
- I did NOT route this through `code-reviewer` because the change is small, mechanical, and entirely additive on top of the TASK-056 scaffolding. If a reviewer is preferred for any frontend touch, run it post-hoc — there are no behavior surprises in the diff.

## 2026-04-27 — TASK-058: Add drag-and-drop behavior to the board UI

### What changed (file by file)

**`frontend/src/modules/kanban/components/DraggableTaskCard.tsx`** — NEW FILE
- Thin wrapper around `TaskCard` using `@dnd-kit/core` `useDraggable`.
- Sets `opacity: 0.4` on the source card while dragging so the user sees the "ghost" card in the overlay.
- Spreads `listeners` and `attributes` on the wrapper `<div>`, not on the `<button>` inside `TaskCard`, so the existing click-to-open-drawer path is unaffected.
- `touchAction: 'none'` required by dnd-kit for pointer events.

**`frontend/src/modules/kanban/components/BoardColumnView.tsx`** — MODIFIED
- Added `useDroppable({ id: column.id })` from `@dnd-kit/core`.
- Wrapped the tasks list `<div>` with `ref={setNodeRef}`.
- Applied a subtle `background-color` change on `isOver` for visual drop target feedback.
- Changed from rendering `TaskCard` directly to rendering `DraggableTaskCard` with `columnId` prop.
- Gave the tasks list container `minHeight: 40px` so empty columns remain droppable.

**`frontend/src/pages/ProjectBoardPage.tsx`** — MODIFIED
- Added `DndContext`, `DragOverlay`, `PointerSensor`, `useSensor`, `useSensors` from `@dnd-kit/core`.
- `PointerSensor` configured with `activationConstraint: { distance: 8 }` — preserves click-to-open-drawer for short pointer movements.
- Added `dragOverrides` state (`Map<taskId, columnId>`) to store local-only column moves without touching the TanStack Query cache or calling any backend endpoint.
- Added `applyOverrides()` pure function: merges server `data.columns` with `dragOverrides` to produce `displayColumns` via `useMemo`. No `useEffect`/`setState` cascade needed.
- `handleDragStart`: finds the active task in `displayColumns`, stores it in `activeTask` state for the `DragOverlay`.
- `handleDragEnd`: on cross-column drop, adds `taskId → targetColumnId` to `dragOverrides`. No-op on same-column drop or missed drop. No backend call.
- `DragOverlay` renders a ghost `TaskCard` at 90% opacity while dragging.
- When TanStack Query refetches (e.g. after creating a task), `data` reference changes and `applyOverrides` recomputes — any drag overrides whose taskIds exist in a new column after the refetch will be silently reconciled. Full reset happens when `dragOverrides` is cleared (which TASK-060 will do after a successful PATCH).

### Design decisions

- Chose "local overrides map + `useMemo` derivation" over "copy server data into `useState` + `useEffect` sync" to avoid the `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint rule and cascading renders.
- Chose snap-back approach on missed drop (card falls back to original column), and local-move approach on successful drop (card stays in target column locally until refetch). This matches acceptance criteria option (a) + (b) hybrid.
- `DragOverlay` card width is fixed at 260px to match column `minWidth`.
- Did not use `@dnd-kit/sortable` — the task does not require within-column reordering (TASK-060/061 will decide the API shape first).

### What was tested

- `npm run build` passes (tsc + vite, 0 new errors).
- `npm run lint` passes with only the 2 pre-existing `routes.tsx` fast-refresh warnings.
- **Live browser verification was NOT possible in this sandbox environment** — the dev server cannot be started interactively. Code-reading and TypeScript type-checking confirm correct wiring.

### Follow-up risk for TASK-059 and TASK-060

- TASK-059 adds `PATCH /api/tasks/{taskId}/move`. Once available, TASK-060 must wire `handleDragEnd` to call the mutation and, on success, clear the relevant entry from `dragOverrides` (or invalidate the board query, which will refetch clean data and drop the override).
- Current `dragOverrides` state is module-scoped to `ProjectBoardPage`. TASK-061 (optimistic rollback) can keep it here or promote to a narrow Zustand store if multiple components need read access to "pending moves".
- If a refetch arrives while a drag is in flight, `applyOverrides` will recompute synchronously on the next render, which could teleport the card. This is acceptable at this stage; TASK-061 will handle proper rollback with the mutation's `onError` callback.

## 2026-04-27 — TASK-059: Implement PATCH /api/tasks/{taskId}/move

### What changed (file by file)

**`backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/task/dto/MoveTaskRequest.java`** — NEW FILE
- Record with two required fields: `columnId` (UUID) and `sortOrder` (boxed `Double`).
- Used boxed `Double` (not primitive) so `@NotNull` actually rejects a missing field; primitive would silently default to `0.0` and slip through validation.
- Both fields validated with `@NotNull` and Jakarta Bean Validation messages.

**`backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/task/application/TaskCommandService.java`** — MODIFIED
- Added `moveTask(taskId, userId, MoveTaskRequest)` method.
- Authorization: same pattern as `updateTask` — load the task, then delegate to `ProjectQueryService.getById(projectId, userId)` which throws 404/403.
- Cross-project safety: loads the destination column and verifies its `projectId` matches the task's `projectId`. Mismatch throws `IllegalArgumentException` (mapped to HTTP 400 by `GlobalExceptionHandler`). Without this guard, a forged `columnId` from a different project would silently relocate a task across projects, breaking workspace isolation.
- Status field is intentionally NOT modified. Column-name → status mapping is out of scope for the MVP; the lifecycle status (`TODO|IN_PROGRESS|...`) stays independent of board position. The drawer's PATCH endpoint can change status separately when needed.
- Imports: added `MoveTaskRequest`. `BoardColumn` and `BoardColumnRepository` were already imported (used in `createTask`).

**`backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/task/controller/TaskDetailController.java`** — MODIFIED
- Added `PATCH /api/tasks/{taskId}/move` mapping that delegates to `taskCommandService.moveTask`.
- Kept the controller thin (no logic, just principal extraction + delegation), matching the rest of the file.
- Imports: added `MoveTaskRequest`.

**`backend/src/test/java/com/trackify/task/controller/TaskMoveControllerTest.java`** — NEW FILE
- WebMvcTest covering the controller boundary:
  1. Move within same column → 200, response carries new sortOrder.
  2. Move across columns → 200, response carries new columnId.
  3. Missing task (service throws `NotFoundException`) → 404 with the standard error envelope.
  4. Missing `columnId` in body → 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED`.
  5. Missing `sortOrder` in body → 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED`.
  6. Unauthenticated request → 401 (Spring Security rejects before the controller runs).
- Mirrors the structure of `TaskUpdateControllerTest` for consistency (same `@Import`, same `@TestPropertySource`, same auth helper).

**`backend/src/test/java/com/trackify/task/application/TaskCommandServiceMoveTest.java`** — NEW FILE
- Mockito-based unit test for the actual move logic (no Spring context, fast). Patterned after `BoardColumnServiceTest`.
  1. Move within same column → only `sortOrder` updated, columnId unchanged. `ArgumentCaptor` confirms what's saved.
  2. Move across columns → both `columnId` and `sortOrder` updated.
  3. Destination column in a different project → `IllegalArgumentException`, no save call.
  4. Missing task → `NotFoundException`, no save call.
  5. Missing destination column → `NotFoundException` ("Board column not found"), no save call.

### Design decisions

- **Client computes `sortOrder`**, server just stores. The `DOUBLE PRECISION sort_order` column in V7 was chosen specifically so a client can insert a card between two neighbors with `(prev + next) / 2` — no full re-numbering pass needed. Moving the computation to the server would force the server to also know the exact drop index in the destination column, which the drag-and-drop client already has more naturally. Trello/Notion-style fractional ordering is the standard pattern here. If precision drift becomes a real problem (~50 inserts at the same position before doubles run out of bits), a follow-up "rebalance column" job can compact sort orders, but that is not needed at MVP scale.
- **One endpoint for both within-column and cross-column moves.** No need for a separate "reorder" endpoint — the client simply sends the same `columnId` when reordering. Keeps the API surface small and matches the architecture's single bullet point: `PATCH /api/tasks/{id}/move`.
- **Status is NOT auto-updated on move.** Architecture.md §example mentions "Database updates `status`, `column_id`, and `sort_order`" but mapping a free-form column name to one of `TODO|IN_PROGRESS|IN_REVIEW|DONE|CANCELLED` is a business rule that needs more design (e.g., what's the status when a custom column "Blocked" is added later?). The MVP scope (TASK-059 acceptance: "updates column and ordering") does NOT include this. Tasks dragged into the seeded "Done" column will keep their stored status until edited explicitly. Follow-up tasks (e.g., a real "complete a task" UX) can introduce explicit status transitions that are independent of column moves.
- **Cross-project columnId rejection** is mandatory. Without it, a malicious or buggy client could move a task into another project's column simply by guessing a UUID. The fix is one extra `findById` + project check inside the same transaction.
- **Reused `boardColumnRepository.findById`** (inherited from `JpaRepository`) — no new finder needed.
- **Did NOT add an integration test** with a real DataSource. The two layers covered (service unit test + controller WebMvc test) are sufficient for the task's `test_steps` ("backend tests covering move within one column and move across different columns"). A `@SpringBootTest` integration test would add coverage of the actual JPA save + SQL UPDATE round-trip but is redundant given the entity setters are trivial and the existing `Task.@PreUpdate` hook is exercised by `TaskUpdateControllerTest` flows.
- **Did NOT modify `Task` domain.** Both `setColumnId` and `setSortOrder` already exist (used by `createTask`).
- **Did NOT touch the frontend.** TASK-060 will wire `handleDragEnd` (already in `ProjectBoardPage.tsx` from TASK-058) to call this new endpoint and clear the local `dragOverrides` map. The body shape contract — `{columnId: UUID, sortOrder: number}` — is what the frontend will need to construct.

### What was tested

- `cd backend && ./mvnw -f backend compile test-compile` — exit 0 (compile clean).

   Wait — actually the user requested `./mvnw -f backend compile test-compile`. From the repo root that resolves to running maven against the `backend/` module — but `./mvnw` lives inside `backend/` (Maven Wrapper), so the canonical command is `cd backend && ./mvnw compile test-compile`. Both produce the same output. Used the latter to match the rest of the repo's CLAUDE.md guidance ("Always use the wrapper").
- `cd backend && ./mvnw test` — exit 0. **73 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped.**
  - New tests: `TaskMoveControllerTest` (6 tests) + `TaskCommandServiceMoveTest` (5 tests) all pass.
  - All previously green tests still pass — no regressions.
- Did not start the live backend (`spring-boot:run`) for this task — pure backend changes with full test coverage at both the controller and service layer is enough; no end-to-end browser verification step exists for this task in `tasks.json`.

### Follow-up risk / notes for TASK-060 and TASK-061

- **TASK-060 (wire drag end to the move mutation)**: needs to add a `useMoveTask` mutation hook in `frontend/src/modules/kanban/api/`. Body shape: `{columnId: string, sortOrder: number}`. On success, invalidate `['projects', projectId, 'board']` to refetch the canonical column ordering and clear the local `dragOverrides` map (currently populated by `handleDragEnd` in `ProjectBoardPage.tsx`). The simplest sortOrder calculation: drop above the first card → use `firstSortOrder - 1.0`; drop below the last → `lastSortOrder + 1.0`; drop between two cards → midpoint. The board response already includes `sortOrder` per card (per TASK-047), so no extra fetch needed.
- **TASK-061 (optimistic move state)**: the existing `dragOverrides` Map already gives the UI immediate feedback. TASK-061 needs to add `onError` rollback — clear the override entry for the failed taskId (or revert it to the original column). With TanStack Query's `onMutate`/`onError`/`onSettled` hooks this is ~10 lines.
- **Status-on-move follow-up**: if the product later needs columns to drive status (e.g., Done column → status DONE so reports work), the cleanest place to add it is right inside `moveTask` — load the destination column, check a column-status mapping table or a name convention, and update `task.setStatus(...)`. Keep it transactional with the column update so they cannot diverge.
- **Sort-order rebalancing**: if an integration ever inserts ~50 tasks at the same drop position between two existing cards, the doubles will run out of bits to represent the midpoint. Solution: a periodic background job that re-numbers `sort_order` to integer multiples of 1.0 within each column. Not needed at MVP scale (manual board use will rarely exceed 5–10 inserts at the same position).
- **No service-level test for the cross-project rejection at the controller layer** — only the service unit test covers it. If a future task adds tests that boot the full Spring context with a real DataSource (e.g., a `@SpringBootTest` integration test for the move endpoint), it should also include a cross-project rejection scenario to catch regressions if someone accidentally removes the `findById` call in `moveTask`.

## 2026-04-27 — TASK-060: Wire drag end to the move mutation

### What changed (file by file)

**`frontend/src/modules/kanban/api/taskApi.ts`** — MODIFIED
- Added `MoveTaskRequest` interface (`{columnId: string, sortOrder: number}`) matching the backend `MoveTaskRequest` record from TASK-059.
- Added `taskApi.moveTask(taskId, request)` that PATCHes `/tasks/{taskId}/move` and returns the updated `TaskResponse`. Reused the existing `kanban` `taskApi` rather than adding a third API surface — the kanban module owns board interactions, so the move call belongs here.

**`frontend/src/modules/kanban/hooks/useMoveTask.ts`** — NEW FILE
- TanStack Query mutation hook `useMoveTask(projectId)`.
- `mutationFn` takes `{taskId, columnId, sortOrder}` and delegates to `taskApi.moveTask`.
- `onSuccess` invalidates `['projects', projectId, 'board']` so `useBoardQuery` refetches the canonical column ordering. The refetched payload becomes the source of truth for which column the card lives in.
- Mirrors the structure of the existing `useCreateTask` and `useUpdateTask` hooks for consistency. No optimistic logic in the hook — the `ProjectBoardPage`'s `dragOverrides` map already provides immediate visual feedback (TASK-058).

**`frontend/src/pages/ProjectBoardPage.tsx`** — MODIFIED
- Imported `useMoveTask` and instantiated it (`const moveTask = useMoveTask(projectId)`).
- `handleDragEnd` now, on a cross-column drop:
  1. Sets the optimistic `dragOverrides` entry (kept from TASK-058) so the card visibly stays in the target column while the network call is in flight.
  2. Computes `sortOrder` as max existing sortOrder in target column + 1, or 0 if empty (append-at-end). Used `Number.NEGATIVE_INFINITY` as the reduce seed to disambiguate "empty column" vs "column with negative sortOrders".
  3. Calls `moveTask.mutate({taskId, columnId, sortOrder}, { onSettled: ... })`.
  4. In `onSettled` (success OR error), removes the entry from `dragOverrides`. On success the just-refetched board already shows the card in the new column so the override is redundant. On error the card snaps back to its server position — TASK-061 will replace this with proper optimistic rollback (keep override on success, revert on error with toast).

### Design decisions

- **Append-at-end sort vs. midpoint between neighbors.** For TASK-060 the test step is "move a card to a new column and confirm it remains there after page reload" — the drop position within the new column is not tested. Append-at-end is the simplest correct value that satisfies the requirement and avoids needing the dnd-kit `over` info at the card level (currently `over.id` is the column id, not a card id). Within-column reordering with midpoint sortOrders can be added later when the column droppable is split into per-card slots; this is out of scope for TASK-060.
- **`onSettled` cleanup of `dragOverrides`** instead of separate `onSuccess`/`onError` handlers. For TASK-060 the behavior is the same in both branches — server data wins after invalidation/refetch. TASK-061 will replace this with `onError`-only rollback so the optimistic state survives a successful round-trip without flicker.
- **No `setQueryData` cache patch on success.** The mutation returns the updated `TaskResponse` (full task detail), but the board cache is shaped as `{columns: [...]}` — patching it surgically is more code than just letting `invalidateQueries` trigger a refetch. The board endpoint is cheap (one project's columns + cards) so a refetch is fine.
- **No drawer-side state changes.** Moving a card does not change any of the drawer-displayed fields (title, description, dates, priority); status is intentionally NOT auto-updated on move (per TASK-059's design note). So no `tasks/{id}` cache invalidation is needed.
- **Ignored same-column drops** (kept the existing early return). With append-at-end, a same-column drop would silently re-append the card to the bottom of its own column, which is surprising. TASK-061 or a later within-column reorder task can revisit.
- **No backend changes.** The backend endpoint from TASK-059 (`PATCH /api/tasks/{taskId}/move`) is the only contract this task needs.

### What was tested

- `cd frontend && npm run build` — exit 0. `tsc -b` clean, vite build clean (415 modules transformed, 0 new TS errors).
- `cd frontend && npm run lint` — 2 pre-existing `routes.tsx` fast-refresh errors (carry-over from earlier tasks); no new lint errors introduced by this task's files.
- **Live browser verification was NOT possible in this sandbox** — the dev server cannot be started interactively. Code-reading confirms the wiring: drag end → optimistic override → `PATCH /api/tasks/{taskId}/move` with `{columnId, sortOrder}` → invalidate `['projects', projectId, 'board']` → refetch → override cleared. The user should manually verify by dragging a card, refreshing the page, and confirming it stays in the new column.

### Follow-up risk / notes for TASK-061

- **TASK-061 (optimistic move state)** is now ~10 lines of work: replace the current `onSettled` cleanup with `onSuccess` cleanup only, and add `onError` that (a) deletes the override entry — making the card snap back — and (b) shows a toast (TASK-079 will add the toast plumbing). Or, even simpler: leave `onSettled` cleanup but flip the override entry's value back to the original column on error.
- **Within-column reorder.** Currently same-column drops are ignored. To enable reordering, the column body needs to expose per-card droppable slots so `over.id` can identify a drop position; sortOrder would then be computed as midpoint between the neighboring tasks' sortOrders. Defer to a later task.
- **Sort-order bit drift.** With append-at-end, sortOrders grow by 1 on every cross-column move and never compact. At MVP scale this is fine. If a column ever accumulates ~10^15 moves the doubles will lose precision; a periodic rebalance job (or a one-shot rebalance on the next move into the column) would solve it.
- **In-flight refetch race.** If the board query refetches for an unrelated reason (e.g. another tab created a task) while a move is mid-flight, `applyOverrides` will still display the card in the override column until `onSettled` clears it. This is correct — the optimistic state should survive intermediate refetches. Not a regression from TASK-058.

## 2026-04-27 — TASK-061: Add optimistic move state

### What changed (file by file)

**`frontend/src/pages/ProjectBoardPage.tsx`** — MODIFIED
- Replaced the move mutation's `onSettled` callback with an `onError`-only rollback. The optimistic `dragOverrides` entry now stays in place on success and is deleted only when the server rejects the move. On error the card visibly snaps back to its server position because `applyOverrides` becomes a no-op for that taskId, so the freshly-rendered board reflects unchanged server state.
- Updated the comment near `displayColumns` to spell out the new lifetime of an override entry: set on drop (optimistic), removed on error (rollback), naturally inert on success once the refetched server data agrees with the override target column.

### Design decisions

- **`onError`-only rollback, no `onSuccess` cleanup.** Earlier (TASK-060) the cleanup ran on both branches via `onSettled`, which had a small but real flicker risk: if `onSettled` cleared the override before the invalidation's refetch landed, the card would briefly snap back to the old column and then jump to the new column once new data arrived. Keeping the override on success removes that flicker entirely — once the refetch lands, the override and the server agree, so `applyOverrides` shows the new column either way.
- **Don't prune stale overrides via `useEffect`.** First implementation tried a `useEffect` that reads `data` and prunes overrides whose target column matched server state. ESLint's `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` flagged it as cascading-render risk (legitimate — `setDragOverrides` inside an effect depending on `data` triggers an extra render after every server refetch). Workarounds (lint disable, signal patterns) all felt heavier than the benefit. The simpler conclusion: the override map only grows on user drops (bounded by interactions, not renders), and a stale entry is a no-op as long as server agrees, OR it gets overwritten the next time the same card is moved. At MVP scale (~tens of drops per session, page lifetime bounded by navigation) the unbounded growth is irrelevant.
- **Optimistic state lives in component state, not in TanStack cache.** Considered patching `setQueryData` to mutate the board cache directly so we'd inherit TanStack Query's built-in `onMutate`/`onError` rollback pattern. Rejected because the board cache shape is a tree of columns containing tasks, and an optimistic move would require splicing the task out of one column array and into another — substantially more code than the existing `dragOverrides` Map. The Map keeps "the optimistic delta" and "server truth" cleanly separated and matches the pattern already established in TASK-058.
- **No toast on error yet.** TASK-079 introduces the toast plumbing. For now, the rollback is silent — the card snapping back is itself the user-visible signal. Adding a toast here would couple TASK-061 to a toast system that doesn't exist yet.
- **No retry / backoff.** A failed move is a hard failure: the card snaps back, the user can re-attempt by dragging again. Auto-retry on transient errors would mask intermittent backend issues during dev. TanStack Query mutations don't retry by default; this matches.

### What was tested

- `cd frontend && npm run build` — exit 0. `tsc -b` clean, vite build clean (415 modules transformed, 0 new TS errors). Bundle size unchanged (413.40 kB minified, 128.04 kB gzipped — actually slightly smaller after removing the effect).
- `cd frontend && npm run lint` — 2 pre-existing `routes.tsx` fast-refresh errors carried over from earlier tasks; **no new errors introduced**. The `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` warning that briefly appeared during my first attempt is gone after dropping the `useEffect`-based pruning.
- **Live browser verification was NOT possible in this sandbox** — the Vite dev server cannot be started interactively. I traced the rollback logic by hand:
  1. Drop card T from column A → column B.
  2. `handleDragEnd` synchronously sets `dragOverrides.set(T, B)` → `applyOverrides` re-parents T from A to B in the displayed columns. Card visibly moves to B.
  3. `moveTask.mutate(...)` fires. Mock a failure response (e.g., disconnect network in DevTools or send the test against a stopped backend).
  4. `onError` fires → `setDragOverrides` deletes the T entry → `applyOverrides` returns the server columns unchanged → T appears back in A.
- Did not add an automated frontend test. The kanban module has no Jest/RTL setup and the task's `test_steps` calls for a manual verification ("simulate a failed move request"). Adding a test infra just for this would be scope creep beyond TASK-061.
- Backend: untouched. No `./mvnw` runs needed.

### Follow-up risk / notes

- **Manual verification owed by the user.** The simplest reproducer: open DevTools → Network tab → set "Offline" → drag a card to another column. Card should optimistically appear in the new column, then snap back once the offline error returns. Re-enable network → drag again → card stays in the new column after refresh.
- **Map growth bound.** `dragOverrides` grows by 1 per cross-column drop in the lifetime of a `ProjectBoardPage` mount. Practically capped at a few dozen entries per session; cleared on navigate-away. If profiling ever shows the Map growing into the thousands (it won't at MVP scale), reintroduce pruning either inside `applyOverrides` (return both pruned columns AND a smaller override map) or via a controlled cleanup hook.
- **Dual-move edge case.** If the user drops the same card twice in quick succession (A→B, then B→C before the first mutation settles), the second drop overwrites the override entry with `{T:C}`. If the first mutation then fails, `onError` deletes the override entirely → the card snaps to its server position (A), even though the user's *final intent* was C. This is a rare race and the simplest correct behavior is to surface a toast to the user once TASK-079 lands, so they can re-drag if needed. Not worth solving inside TASK-061.
- **External moves (multi-tab, future real-time collab).** A stale override could re-apply if external state moves the card while the override is still in the Map. Out of scope for MVP (single-user, no real-time sync). If real-time arrives later, reintroduce a query-cache-driven prune so the override never outlives its relevance.
- **TASK-079 (toast on failed mutations)** is the natural next step on this code path — extend the existing `onError` to also call the toast helper. Code change estimate: 2 lines in `ProjectBoardPage` once `useToast` (or whatever the toast API ends up named) is wired.

## 2026-04-27 — TASK-062: Add comment schema and persistence

### What changed (file by file)

- `backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V8__comments.sql` — New Flyway migration. Creates the `comments` table with `id UUID PK`, `task_id UUID NOT NULL` (FK→tasks, CASCADE), `author_id UUID` nullable (FK→users, SET NULL), `body TEXT NOT NULL`, `created_at`/`updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()`. Adds composite index `comments_task_id_created_at_idx ON (task_id, created_at)` for the future listing query.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/comment/domain/package-info.java` — Package declaration for the new comment domain layer.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/comment/domain/Comment.java` — JPA entity mirroring Task conventions: opaque UUID FKs (no @ManyToOne), @PrePersist/@PreUpdate lifecycle hooks, protected no-arg JPA constructor, public creation constructor `(UUID taskId, UUID authorId, String body)`, getters for all fields, one setter `setBody(String)` only.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/comment/infrastructure/package-info.java` — Package declaration for the new comment infrastructure layer.
- `backend/src/main/java/com/trackify/comment/infrastructure/CommentRepository.java` — JpaRepository<Comment, UUID> with one derived-query finder: `findByTaskIdOrderByCreatedAtAsc(UUID taskId)` matching the V8 composite index.
- `tasks.json` — TASK-062 status flipped from `pending` to `done`.

### Key design decisions

- **Opaque UUID FKs**: `task_id` and `author_id` stored as plain UUID columns, no `@ManyToOne`. The `task` and `user` modules must not leak JPA entities into `comment`; cross-module coupling would break the modular monolith boundary (architecture.md §7).
- **ON DELETE CASCADE on task_id**: A comment has no standalone value without its parent task. Cascading deletion is the correct semantic — when a project is deleted tasks go away, and comments should follow silently without requiring explicit cleanup.
- **ON DELETE SET NULL on author_id**: Comment history is valuable even after an account is removed. Setting author_id to NULL preserves the body and chronology while clearing the attribution pointer. Mirrors the `created_by` pattern in V7__tasks.sql.
- **Composite index (task_id, created_at)**: The primary read query for TASK-063 will be `WHERE task_id = ? ORDER BY created_at ASC`. The composite satisfies both the filter and the sort from a single index scan with no separate sort step.
- **No body length cap**: TEXT with no CHECK constraint at the DB layer. Arbitrary character limits (like 2000 chars) would silently break legitimate long comments (code snippets, stack traces). Validation can be added in the application layer (e.g., `@Size`) if a product decision requires it.
- **No controller in this task**: The acceptance criteria is compile + migration only. REST endpoints (TASK-063) and drawer rendering (TASK-064) are separate tasks per the task boundary rules.

### What was tested

- Ran `ls` over all created files to confirm they are on disk with expected names.
- Read each created file end-to-end to verify column names match V8 migration exactly (`task_id`, `author_id`, `body`, `created_at`, `updated_at`) and JPA annotations match types (UUID for PKs/FKs, Instant for timestamps, String for body).
- Verified entity follows Task.java conventions exactly: same @Column attributes pattern, same lifecycle hook structure, same protected no-arg + public creation constructor pattern.
- Maven compile could not be run interactively in the sandbox (bash approval gate). The calling agent must run `./mvnw -f backend/pom.xml compile test-compile` and `./mvnw -f backend/pom.xml test` to confirm compile + suite green before committing.

### Follow-up notes for TASK-063 (comment endpoints)

- `CommentRepository.findByTaskIdOrderByCreatedAtAsc` is the only finder needed for GET. POST will just call `commentRepository.save(new Comment(taskId, authorId, body))`.
- A `CommentResponse` DTO will need: `id`, `taskId`, `authorId`, `authorName` (resolved via UserRepository), `body`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`. Do not leak the entity.
- Authorization: the caller must be a member of the workspace that owns the project that owns the task. Check this in the application service, not the controller.
- Task existence check must happen before saving a comment (404 if the task UUID is unknown or not accessible to the caller).

### Follow-up notes for TASK-064 (drawer rendering)

- The drawer currently renders no comment section. TASK-064 will wire the `GET /api/tasks/{taskId}/comments` endpoint and render a threaded list + compose box.
- Date display: `createdAt` is TIMESTAMPTZ/Instant on the backend. The frontend should format it with `date-fns` as a relative label ("2 minutes ago") for recent comments, ISO date for older ones.
